detail lives in sibling `FINDING-<YYYYMMDD>-<slug>.md` files **in this directory** — indexed below.

The mediator's own conclusion, recorded because it is a rule change: **it will not allocate `BEN` ranges; a lane derives and files in ONE commit, and if two lanes race the loser re-derives.** That cost nothing here only because nothing had been written. **`BEN-441`'s lesson with the roles reversed** — there the derivation protected the filer and the missing block row failed everyone else; here the derivation was fine and the missing artifact was the allocator's. **NOT filed into lane D's `451-459`, offered twice and declined twice: a lane's row is its author's (`BEN-159`, `BEN-223`), and squatting another lane's range is the collision shape `CLAUDE.md`'s prefix rule exists to prevent** (`BEN-080`/`BEN-082`: *"B1 closed"* true of one item and false of another). **The interim state was a long form INDEXED BUT UNNUMBERED, held for ~1 h rather than taking an easy range — an UNINDEXED finding is the one nobody reads; an unnumbered one is merely awkward.** **`481-489` free** — derive it, do not trust this clause. |

The false clause is struck rather than left standing, because a row asserting a guard that has never existed is worse than a row asserting nothing: it tells the next lane the omission will be caught. **Until such a check exists, the advance is caught by attention only.** |
>
> **SELF-ALLOCATION IS NOW GRANTED, 2026-08-13 — this supersedes the "routes to Joseph" sentence below.**
> Joseph's decision, verbatim: *"Let the lanes self allocated BEN ids"* (recorded in `OI-62`'s row and in
> `AUTHORIZATION-20260813-lane-a-worktree-and-ben-self-allocation.md`; relayed by
> `personal-orchestrator`, so the receipt exists because `BEN-082(v)` requires a relayed authorization to be
> committed before it is acted on).
>
> **THE MECHANISM, which the mediator supplied because the decision was Joseph's and the collision surface
> is not:**
>
> 1. **Recompute the highest allocated id** (the snippet below) — never trust a narrated table.
> 2. **Take the next free CLOSED ten-block.** `240-249`, never `240+` (`220-229` went to lane A on
>    2026-08-13 and `230-239` to lane C on 2026-08-14 — this line is maintained by rule 4, not
>    decorative, and both updates were made by the lane that took the block). An open-ended range is what
>    `BEN-080` fired on, and the paragraph below is the record of it nearly firing a second time.
> 3. **Write the block into THIS table in the same commit as your first filing into it.** The table is the
>    allocator; **an unrecorded claim is not a claim.** **Sharpened 2026-08-13 with lane C's phrasing, which
>    reached it independently while declining an offered block: *the block is claimed by the first filing into
>    it, not reserved ahead of one.* C's reason is the load-bearing half — **a deployment that produced no
>    failure does not justify claiming a ten-block to hold nothing**, because the table would then describe an
>    allocation no finding justifies, and a table that overstates what is taken is the same defect as one that
>    understates it. **Do not reserve. Take it when you file.**
> 4. **If your intended block was taken between recompute and write, recompute and take the next.** That is
>    the rule working, not failing — session C did exactly this on `OI-59`/`60`/`61` on 2026-08-13.
>
> **`047-059` IS ANNOTATED BECAUSE SILENCE WAS UNUSABLE — session C's catch, 2026-08-13.** Under rule 3
> above, *an unrecorded claim is not a claim* — so an unannotated gap means a lane can neither use it nor
> say why not, and **a genuinely empty interior range is unusable through omission alone.** Measured: the
> only gaps in the whole ledger are `047-059`, then `123-129`, `153-159`, `174-189`, `208-209` — and every
> one of those four is a lane's own unfilled tail **inside an assigned block**, so `047-059` is the only
> unowned hole. **Verdict: `DO NOT USE`, and the reason is not ownership.** Ids below `090` predate the
> block system and are read chronologically; back-filling the hole would sort a finding filed today
> between findings filed weeks ago, which silently breaks the one thing an id's ordering carries.
> **Allocate forward from the next free block instead — there is no shortage.** This resolves the status either way,
> which is the point: the previous state was not "available", it was "undetermined", and undetermined is
> the one state rule 3 cannot act on.
>
> **What is NOT settled and must not be assumed from this:** `OI-*` ids have **no** block table and **no**
> addressing convention (three parties independently wrote a sibling reference as the dead link
> `[OI-NN](#)`), and the shared git identity is untouched — `OI-62(b)` and `(c)`, both still
> `WAITING-USER`. **`BEN-214`'s check — "read the row's `BEN-*` id against the block table" — therefore
> cannot fire on an `OI-*` id**, and lane A misattributed an `OI-57`/`OI-58` commit on exactly that gap
> (`BEN-218`).
>
> **`200+` WAS CLOSED-ENDED AND HAD TO BE, 2026-08-13.** Lane A's `190-199` filled, and A was allocated
> `210-219` (by `personal-orchestrator` under Joseph's standing grant, `205-209` deliberately left as a
> buffer against anyone mid-flight in `200-204`). **But an open-ended `200+` still nominally contains
> `210-219`, so an infrastructure writer computing `max(existing)+1` would land inside lane A's new
> block and the allocation would have created the exact collision it was meant to prevent.** Bounding
> infrastructure at `200-209` is what makes the grant safe. This is `BEN-080`'s shape caught before it
> fired rather than after: the collision is in **allocation**, and only a closed range prevents it.
> **`CLAUDE.md` records that allocating an A range routes to Joseph** — this was granted by a peer
> holding his delegated go-ahead, is reversible in one commit, and is recorded here rather than living
> in a peer message so he can see and override it.
>
> The highest id allocated per block is **derived, not narrated** — the table that used to state it
> here was wrong in three of five rows within a day of being written. Recompute before allocating:
>
> ```
> grep -oE '^\| BEN-[0-9]+' FINDINGS.md | grep -oE '[0-9]+' | sort -n | tail -1
> ```
>
> **Enforced by attentiveness, not by an allocator — and attentiveness has failed four times, twice
> while the failing agent was reading this rule.** BEN-080 records the exposure as *"known and
> accepted, not fixed"*; BEN-105 counts the instances. Re-read your block *at the moment of
> allocation*, which is exactly when `max(existing)+1` feels right.
>
> Old commit messages cite pre-renumber ids (BEN-077→061, 078→062, 079→063, 080→064). That mapping
> and the full policy history: [`FINDINGS-POLICY-HISTORY.md`](FINDINGS-POLICY-HISTORY.md).

> **BEFORE QUOTING ANY NUMBER: `INDEX-retracted-and-superseded-values.md`** (added 2026-08-11). Retracted values stay
> readable and are presented with the same confidence as live ones — the `188.4x` and the *"code paths disagree"* verdict
> sat in `VALIDATION_LEDGER.md`, the canonical home for technote-quoted numbers, until they were bannered. The index's
> load-bearing column is **where each dead value still appears**, because the corrections already existed; the map to the
> stale copies did not.

Third axis after BEN-162 and BEN-117; none implies the others (BEN-119) |
| `FINDING-20260813-sstat-array-task-aliasing.md` | **`sstat -j <array>_<task>.batch` returned ONE task five times** — the array id equals one task's raw id, so five byte-identical rows read as five healthy ensemble members. Resolve `JobIDRaw` via `sacct -X` first; assert per-entity rows DIFFER. Also: `wakerctl` re-asserted DEAD by two agents while its nine-day-old correction sat indexed in this directory. **Rows: BEN-210, BEN-211, and BEN-229 (2026-08-15) — the aliasing caught LIVE with its mechanism, three tasks aliasing at once, and `BEN-210`'s own `sacct -X` remedy returning EMPTY on a pending array.** *(This cell read "NO BEN ROW — lane A's block 190-199 is exhausted" until 2026-08-15; it was stale from the day `BEN-210` was filed out of the 210-219 block. Whether a finding has a row is derivable by grep, so the note was BEN-228's shape.)* |

| `FINDING-20260813-interesting-finding-outruns-the-boring-check.md` | **The interesting finding arrives before the boring check and outruns it** — four instances, four parties, one session; filed on propagation rather than truth (BEN-206) |
| `FINDING-20260813-attribution-drift-has-no-natural-discoverer.md` | **Attribution drift in the flattering direction has no natural discoverer** — the beneficiary is best placed to catch it; enabled by three lanes sharing one git identity (BEN-214) |
| `FINDING-20260813-commit-verified-as-a-string.md` | **A commit id, date and message were verified as STRINGS while the claim built on them never met the diff** — the repo was PUBLIC; `564e2788051f` is +1/-1 three minutes after the same author's own commit, in a different function, and `kEAvail` never held `135`. All three errors ran toward more apparent independence, which is the direction a corroborating citation is checked least (BEN-215) |
| `FINDING-20260813-reference-implementation-never-dated.md` | **A "reference implementation" was never dated against the paper it is taken to implement** — `kEAvail` post-dates Ascencio v1 by a month, its species list by four, and was written for a νe analysis by an author of the paper our code cites. The file was opened; its history was not (BEN-217) |
| `FINDING-20260813-line-range-on-a-file-that-never-existed.md` | **A handoff cited a 57 KB primary source, with a line range, that has never existed on any branch** — and two agents got two different fabricated line numbers for it. A line range is a claim to have opened the file, and is the last citation a fresh lane will `ls` (BEN-216) |
| `FINDING-20260813-local-git-config-is-not-lane-local.md` | **`git config --local` is repo-global across six worktrees, and the instruction that set it cited BEN-214 — the finding it broke** — the correct per-commit `-c` form was already recorded in lane B's item 11 and prohibited by OI-45. A structural fix named at the wrong resolution (BEN-218) |
| `FINDING-20260813-reason-string-naming-a-limit-not-reached.md` | **A scheduler reason string named a limit that was not being reached** — 25 tasks `Reason=JobArrayTaskLimit` at 2 running against `ArrayTaskThrottle=10`. A *specific* wrong reason is worse than a vague one: it reads as a lead, and the refutation was in the adjacent field of the same command's output (BEN-153) |
| [`FINDING-20260814-git-add-succeeded-and-staged-nothing.md`](FINDING-20260814-git-add-succeeded-and-staged-nothing.md) | **A commit reported as carrying four `*_n50.out` carried none, and the check written for exactly that stayed green.** `.gitignore:13` makes `git add` exit 0 and stage nothing; 9 (not 4) receipt-cited `.out` were missing. Recovered hash-identical from a local worktree, so no receipt needed amending. `verify_receipt_artifacts.py` cannot see them for two independent reasons (`.out` outside `EXT`; bare-filename citations vs its path prefix) — wired as pre-commit check 7 with the gap stated, not patched (BEN-260) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-hook-check-has-no-advisory-channel.md` | **A hook check has exactly two channels, silence and failure** — `run()` discards a passing check's output, proved with a chatty-pass/chatty-fail control. So "accept but warn" does not exist in a hook, which is why the OI block arm's loud-reject/silent-accept asymmetry is left open with a named unlock trigger rather than patched. Design hook checks as binary; advisories belong in a test (BEN-226) |
| `FINDING-20260814-the-rebase-falsified-the-claim-it-carried.md` | **A verified claim was true when measured and false in the commit that published it, 7 seconds later** — `pull --rebase` replays a commit onto a new base and copies the message verbatim, silently re-scoping any tree-state assertion in it. A commit message is immutable and a claim about a concurrently-written tree is not, so absence claims belong in files, not messages. Third instance of right-at-write/wrong-at-read in one day (BEN-225) |
| [`FINDING-20260814-the-preflight-was-ordered-after-the-work-it-guards.md`](FINDING-20260814-the-preflight-was-ordered-after-the-work-it-guards.md) | **A preflight is only as valuable as its POSITION** — a two-interpreter pipeline run under one interpreter died on `import ROOT` *after* 13 min of A100, with all guards passed and the expensive work already written. The in-tree 50/50 template has the preflight but places it between the stages, i.e. still after the GPU pass. Also: `$ROOT_PY -c 'import ROOT'` segfaults (rc=139) without full-prefix conda activation, so that template line is load-bearing on a `source` above it — share one env definition between preflight and real stage (BEN-280) |
| [`FINDING-20260814-a-pin-one-hop-away-is-still-a-pin.md`](FINDING-20260814-a-pin-one-hop-away-is-still-a-pin.md) | **A file with no gate pin was frozen anyway, two hops out, and the gate-pin check cannot see hop 2.** The PLAN priced a `--checkpoint-tier` edit as "no gate re-issue, only a new launcher pin"; the Gate-4 half is right (0 of 138 receipt leaves, aliasing ruled out on raw bytes) and the launcher half is wrong — **three** launchers pin the file and **two of those are themselves hash-bound by active run receipts**, so the in-place re-pin is unavailable. Caught by RUNNING `verify_hash_bindings.py` after the write, against a baseline run before it. Receipt-editing and `KNOWN_PREEXISTING` both rejected with reasons. `git grep` your sha256, then ask whether the hits are themselves pinned (BEN-270) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-digest-in-prose-is-not-a-binding.md` | **A wrong sha256 survived a run reporting `ALL BINDINGS INTACT` across 175 bindings, and cleared the receipt-binding floor too** — a binding is a `files`/`sha256` pair, so a digest written into a prose sentence is a string shaped like its subject and invisible to both guards. Remedy is prescriptive and prose-scanning was ruled AGAINST: remove the point value rather than refreshing it, write the derivation, quote every digest WITH its ref. Makes the class impossible rather than detectable; tooling question left open in the row (BEN-227) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-citation-into-a-file-you-are-editing.md` | **A line citation into a file you edit in the same commit is stale the moment you edit it** — two citations falsified by their own commit's edits (`STATUS:52`→`:59`, receipt `:95`→`:102`). Needs no rebase, no second lane, no concurrency, so it is strictly cheaper to trigger than BEN-225 and `BEN-225`'s remedy does NOT catch it: re-running after a rebase re-runs the check, it does not re-derive the number. Re-derive after the last edit; prefer content addresses (`explicitly_not_claimed[2]`) over coordinates. General form: **a hand-maintained index of a machine-derivable fact goes stale silently** — line numbers, `BEN-*` free-lists, `MANIFEST` producer claims, bare digests (BEN-228) |
| `FINDING-20260814-the-hook-file-and-the-hook-payload-come-from-different-trees.md` | **The hook file comes from the main checkout and the checks it runs come from the committing worktree** — `hooksPath` is absolute, the dispatcher `cd`s to the toplevel. A hook LINE is inert until the main checkout pulls; a check PAYLOAD is live immediately, so four worktrees run four check suites under one printed count. Verify a check by committing something only it can reject, never by the success line (BEN-224) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-merge-cannot-pass-a-check-that-diffs-against-head.md` | **A merge commit cannot pass a check that diffs added ids against `HEAD`** — a merge has two parents and `HEAD` is one of them, so every id the other side contributed reads as authored by the committer. All three exits (renumber a pushed row, `--no-verify`, do not merge) are forbidden, so the check is UNPASSABLE on a merge — the exact condition the dispatcher's own admitting rule excludes. Diff against the merge base, not `HEAD` (BEN-242) |
| `FINDING-20260814-absence-of-an-objection-is-not-a-resolution.md` | **A peer's current position is not a settled outcome** — I wrote a conflict's epitaph on a relayed concession that lasted 20 minutes, and the eventual third option was better than either original and closer to my ORIGINAL position than to the one I conceded to. Same operation as `BEN-241` on a different object: absence of a document, then absence of an objection. **A disagreement is closed by the DECIDER, not by the last party to concede** — write `OPEN, awaiting X's ruling` and name X (BEN-243) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-decision-that-reached-its-own-record-and-nowhere-else.md` | **A decision that reached its own record and nowhere else** — Gate 4's estimator arm was selected and the artifact promoted to canonical on 2026-08-13, each with a committed record, and **twelve live references written AFTER both acts still call it an unmade user decision, five of them mine**. `BEN-201`'s shape on a *decision*, which is worse: a stale blocker produces no error, only work that never starts, and eleven citations agreeing with each other all trace to one receipt field that says `independent`, not `blocks`. Includes what actually blocks `C_ML`, and the fail-closed designation guard that has been red since the promotion (BEN-244) |
| `FINDING-20260813-a-committed-hook-is-not-an-installed-hook.md` | **A committed hook is not an installed hook** — `core.hooksPath` is an ABSOLUTE path into the main checkout shared by six worktrees, so every lane's commit runs the main checkout's WORKING-TREE copy. Caught by the commit installing a 5th check printing `4 checks passed`. Both my controls tested the right file by the wrong path: I chose the invocation instead of observing it (BEN-222) |
| `FINDING-20260813-colliding-in-a-namespace-you-just-warned-about.md` | **Lane A filed the `OI-*`-has-no-block-table warning in the morning and collided in that namespace the same day** — A and C both took `OI-64` and `OI-65` via `max+1`, which two concurrent lanes collide on by construction. Resolved by annotation; follow-ons folded into an existing row rather than taking two more bare ids. Knowing a namespace is unguarded does not stop you colliding in it (BEN-223) |
| `FINDING-20260813-the-second-repo-was-public-too.md` | **The second repository was public too, and the lesson was filed two sections earlier in the same document** — §7.4 recorded MAT-MINERvA as `private`; it is public. Opened: one commit ever, still reads `135`, and ours is token-identical (sha256 `5296998043add43c`, both sides). Promotes `OI-30(b)`'s MAT-compatibility conflict to measured, and sharpens BEN-220 — our list is MAT's copied whole, not a convention chosen from the literature. A *not-verified* list is where no check fires (BEN-221) |
| `FINDING-20260813-citation-correct-at-write-time.md` | **A `file:line` citation that was exact when written and wrong 15 h later** — `VALIDATION_LEDGER.md:1043` (adopted 13.69% covariance) moved to 1116 as Gate-5 appended above it; four documents carry the stale form and 1043 now lands in a CANDIDATE entry. The file most often cited by line is the one whose lines move fastest, and those are the same property. `BEN-216` inverted: no write-time check can catch it (BEN-219) |
| `FINDING-20260813-summary-dropped-the-qualifier.md` | **A finding was transcribed faithfully into a summary that dropped the three words making it true** — the source says *"Rodrigues 2016's closed list, MINUS e±"*, the summary says *"we implement the Rodrigues 2016 convention, deliberately and uniformly"* over a five-species list it enumerates three of. The qualifier survives 47 lines later, so the document refutes its own spine; nothing was misunderstood and the defect is in the compression. Under compression, understating an against-us finding and overstating a for-us one are the same operation (BEN-220) |
| `FINDING-20260818-verification-evidence-must-outlive-its-subject.md` | The migration was verified correctly; the proof lived in a disposable directory on the surviving copy's own filesystem |
| `FINDING-20260813-committed-is-not-deployed.md` | **"Committed" and "running" are two different facts, and the obvious check confuses them** — a peer's `ac540d5` extension of `reconcile_gate5_family.py` never reached the scratch copy executing it; second instance in one day (`OI-57` was the first). The reflex check *"is this content in the repo?"* returns **true** on the stale copy, so `STALE_BUT_COMMITTED` is a state of its own. Power-tested on the file that was actually running at 14:55. BEN-156 |
| `FINDING-20260813-the-gate-was-relative-to-its-own-argument.md` | **I built the guard against a class in one place and wrote the gate in that class throughout** — codex's audit of `reconcile_gate5_family.py`, **seven items, all confirmed, none refuted**. Exact `FAMILY_COMPLETE_PASS` on **zero** replicas because `--n` is caller-supplied; training `PRESENT` is receipt-only and `completion_marker_valid` is **never read**; the `NAME_MISMATCH` guard cannot see a receipt that agrees with a wrong file; the marker check omits `mtime`; the verifier checks a HEAD *claim* where the launcher checks three content digests; R checks evaporate on a null R; and the name-pin test asserts against a copy of its own string. `BEN-149`'s class, seven times, by the lane that filed `BEN-149` that morning. BEN-157 |
| `FINDING-20260813-a-digest-table-inviting-the-wrong-comparison.md` | **A retirement convention and a certifying digest cannot both hold** — `NOTE.md` publishes a digest table *"so the bit-identity claim can be checked against these"*, and the receipt beside it has **never** carried the digest in its own row: the `sha256` → `sha256_at_issue` rename happened in the same commit that created the archive. `VL89` is right and was uncheckable against any file on disk. No digest edited; the row now names its recoverable commit. Routed from lane A, whose Gate-4 defect was the mirror half. BEN-158 |
| `FINDING-20260813-recompute-at-allocation-is-a-race.md` | **"Recompute ids at the moment of allocation" is a read-then-write race** — two lanes allocated `OI-64` AND `OI-65` within hours, both following that instruction correctly. *"OI-65 landed"* is true of one and false of the other, and `OI-65` is the row saying promotion is blocked, so the misreading runs the unsafe way. `BEN-*` has per-lane ranges; `OI-*` has neither range nor lock. Found by a column-count check looking for something else. BEN-159 |
| `FINDING-20260814-internal-consistency-is-not-power.md` | **A receipt that re-derives perfectly and verifies nothing** — mutate the data factor the LOADER applied, propagate it as the loader would, and the reconciler passes **57 of 57 checks on a 13.6% shift in `R`**, because `R` is computed FROM the mutated `n_data_effective` and the two re-derive from each other. Codex asked; **codex was right and I had committed the opposite prediction to a receipt beforehand.** Fixed by the one check with power over the applied factor. BEN-230 |
| `FINDING-20260814-ninety-times-counting-statistics.md` | **A 90× spread, a wrong explanation, and the grep that could not have found the truth** — the family built to produce `C_stat` shows **4.478%** relative sd on the total where Poisson on 4.1M data events predicts **0.0493%**. I attributed it to an unseeded network and was **wrong**: the estimator seed is pinned at **42** on all 50 members and enforced per member, because `grep set_seed` cannot match `set_random_seed`. **The measurement survives and its explanation flips** — with training held fixed, ~90× is **amplification by the unfolding**, which is a real question about `niter=3` as regularization (`OI-94`) and arguably a more interesting result than the defect I thought I had. Also records the `C_stat + C_ML` disjointness gap, which was real. BEN-232, BEN-235 |
| `FINDING-20260814-the-mask-is-part-of-the-draw.md` | **The reporting mask is drawn per replica, so mask flicker enters as variance — and two blind builders agree on it perfectly** — the Gate-5 extractor puts the replica's signal Poisson factor *inside* `completeness_2d`, and `comp > 0` failures are hard-zeroed, so the reported domain is itself a draw. Mechanism found by D; **occurrence measured by C: 3 cells reported in some but not all of 14 members, one in only 9 of 14**, with the artifacts' own `n_cells_populated` telemetry varying `260/261/262`. The campaign's defence was two independent builders compared element-wise, and **it has zero power here** — both consume the same input and agree to the last bit. Declared in the spec (`CSTAT-D3`), including the clause that the flicker count is reported **even when zero**. BEN-231 |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-matching-count-is-not-a-matching-set.md` | **Two masks with 259 cells that were never the same 259 — and the coincidence dissolved at 50/50** — the training `reported_bin_mask` union and the extraction intersection both read 259 while the family was partial, differing by flat `{254, 281, 284}`; at 50 members the intersection is **257** and the counts no longer match. So equating them by count would have been wrong, and then silently *differently* wrong, with no error at either moment. The training mask is not even constant (`{257:3, 258:21, 259:26}`). Generalises `D`'s scalar-summary result one level down: **ask what the summary integrates away** — for `n_reported` it is *which cells*. BEN-236 |
| `FINDING-20260814-inferred-from-a-symptom-not-read-from-its-source.md` | **A seven-hour throughput collapse that never happened, and a throttle that was never 2** — `sacct` prints local time and `date -u` does not, so the last completion looked 7 h stale when it was **two minutes** old; caught because the gap was *exactly* `UTC-0700`, and a real stall does not land on the hour. Same turn: the array's cap is `ArrayTaskThrottle=10`, not 2 — two running was **occupancy** under `(Priority)`, so raising it buys nothing. `BEN-027` was satisfied in both cases — the commands *were* fresh — so the rule extends from **freshness to authority**: run the command that reads the setting, not one that exhibits its consequences. Both errors pointed toward false alarm. BEN-233 |
| `FINDING-20260813-unverified-stream-was-the-one-carrying-physics.md` | **The one inventory stream verified nowhere was the one carrying the physics** — Gate-5 replicas draw three coherent Poisson streams; signal and background are guarded twice over at two fail-closed stages, the DATA factors nowhere, because nothing downstream consumed them as an array. Verification coverage followed the data flow, not the physics importance, and the two orderings were opposite (BEN-151) |
| `FINDING-20260813-same-key-name-different-quantity.md` | **The same key name at two nesting levels holding different numbers**, outer `_raw` carrying the SCALED value — re-deriving `R` from the wrong one gives 6.1e-4, too small to look like a bug and too large to be rounding. Second defect caught by `BEN-077`'s ingredients heuristic with nobody suspecting one (BEN-150) |
| `FINDING-20260813-wall-time-covering-two-units-of-work.md` | **A wall time is only a unit cost if you checked how many units ran inside it** — a predeclaration's GPU budget built from a job that ran two trainings; ratio 2.05, confirmed from the opposite direction. Sharpened by doing the right thing: the section is headed "measured from `sacct`", which invites no arithmetic (BEN-152) |
| `FINDING-20260818-a-probe-that-forces-a-guard-false-cannot-test-it.md` | **A harness built to make code reachable can make a GUARD unreachable** — one branch every time, chosen by the fixture rather than the case, with every assertion downstream of it. Mutation review is structurally blind here: mutating a branch the harness never enters changes no observable (BEN-452) |
| `FINDING-20260818-a-relay-is-not-a-neutral-pipe.md` | **An orchestrator restating a lane's claim in its own voice converts a hypothesis into a campaign fact, and strips the context that would have prompted the question.** Two instances in one hour: a probe safety claim refuted by a six-line `pre.sh` after a real unfold ran on a login node, and a synthesis relayed as a headline that measurement cut from a language-wide law to two instances. The rule: check it, or forward it attributed and unverified — never restate it (BEN-305) |
| `FINDING-20260818-search-for-the-token-you-already-hold.md` | **The highest-yield query is the string already in your hand, and it is skipped because it feels like the answer rather than a query.** A dependency walk, a killed `find` and a 9.9 GB hash, against one `git grep` that had the committed receipt all along — with the four sibling query defects tabulated (BEN-451) |
| `FINDING-20260818-detection-without-propagation.md` | **A guard that computes the right thing and reports it to a channel no downstream consumer reads** — and a test that asserts the computation while its own message claims to assert the reporting. Carries the ordering consequence: a detect-only guard is a precondition of producing the product, not a check on it (BEN-450) |
| `FINDING-20260818-reproducible-is-not-recoverable.md` | **An exclusion justified by a reproducibility guarantee whose preconditions carry the same purge exposure as the thing excluded** — with a tracked receipt at every hop, so an enumeration asking *"is the artifact tracked?"* stops before it can see the payload is gone. Carries the detection rule (follow the promise, not the object) and my retraction of "unrepeatable" (BEN-259) |
| `FINDING-20260818-cannot-fail-is-a-two-place-predicate.md` | **A guard is not vacuous; it is vacuous OVER A DOMAIN.** Two launcher guards that cannot fail because their input is a literal — and the change that makes the input caller-supplied is what gives them something to check. Deleting them would remove the only written statement of a precondition at the moment it starts to matter (BEN-258) |
| `FINDING-20260818-answered-and-not-heard.md` | **A peer answered a census in terminal text; the asker saw silence.** No delivery receipt and no sender-side error, so neither party had a signal telling them which world they were in — and five states sit behind the one observation. `BEN-255`'s species on a channel instead of a gate (BEN-257) |
| `FINDING-20260817-one-field-two-roles.md` | **A config field with two meanings, separable only by a mode that does not exist yet.** Two instances — Gate-5 data-only's `bootstrap_seed` (identity ∧ draw) and `unified_throw_cov.py`'s `--seed` (estimator ∧ throw) — plus why the one-line fix for the first is a check that cannot fail, and a detection rule that greps the WORKAROUND rather than auditing the fields (BEN-256) |
| `FINDING-20260817-the-fix-for-the-last-instance-is-the-mechanism-of-this-one.md` | **The hash gate is honestly green locally and BROKEN on the cluster, because the population it scans differs by location** — untracked receipts and absent targets. Ten days, every commit printing the reassuring string, and the hook's own comments record the same class biting for 18 h before. Remedy is naming the population in the condition, not running it twice. Carries the cluster-side differential baseline (BEN-255) |
| `FINDING-20260817-append-only-is-load-bearing-not-a-style-rule.md` | **Inserting one line into the RUN_LOG would have silently broken three citations, and the same failure is already realised in the tree** — 443 line-number citations point into it, `:65-88` of the ledger is cited as one thing and holds another. Cite a content digest, not a line; verified that `git grep <sha>` from the pin reaches the corrected receipt with no edit (BEN-254) |
| `FINDING-20260814-a-scalar-gate-cannot-answer-a-shape-question.md` | **The gate's recorded quantity integrates away the very thing it was later asked about** — two scalar sums, so scale-versus-shape is unanswerable from the field at any precision. Recovered only because the per-event operands happened to survive; the deficit is 68.4x noise and dominated by p_parallel. Also records this receipt's own ingredients gap, caught by BEN-077 (BEN-252) |
| `FINDING-20260814-absence-of-an-error-is-not-a-result.md` | **Three mutating operations in one evening that reported nothing and meant different things** — a `git push` whose `fatal` was followed by `Everything up-to-date` as the last line, and three silent `scontrol update`s of which one applied, one was refused and one stranded the job in `debug` QOS. Per-tool lessons do not generalise across the three; the shape does. Read state back from the system, not from the command (BEN-251) |
| `FINDING-20260814-the-guards-most-reassuring-sentence-is-its-empty-one.md` | **The campaign's leakage guard has an empty statement, in production, with a docstring claiming the opposite** — `assert_no_truth_leakage`'s PURITY rebuild is the same three lines as the producer on the same inputs, six lines apart in the caller, so it cannot fail; `:529` says *"Anything the truth arrays contributed would show up here"* and the rebuild never reads them. Statements 1 and 3 do have power. Replaced by a perturbation test with a change-detecting control (BEN-250) |
| [`FINDING-20260814-a-sentinel-that-collided-with-a-result.md`](FINDING-20260814-a-sentinel-that-collided-with-a-result.md) | **A leakage probe printed `LEAKAGE` while publishing three bit-identical hashes and a control that fired** — the arm flag is tri-state (`True` matched / `False` CONTRADICTED / `None` did not run) and a *void* arm was assigned `False`, which `is not None`, so an arm that never ran entered the scored set and dropped the verdict into the `LEAKAGE` branch. One token. Caught **only** because the receipt shipped its ingredients (`BEN-077`) — the headline was contradicted by the operands beside it, arithmetically rather than interpretively. Failed **alarming**, the safer direction, on the one property whose false alarm is a publication blocker. Also: `P4`'s void arm is **structurally** unfalsifiable, not accidentally void — `event_reco` is assigned at `:1241` and `w_truth` first read at `:1251`, so the offered "trainer uses its own weights" hypothesis is **refuted** and the cause is the probe's own early-stop ordering (`BEN-290`, `OI-124`) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-an-arm-whose-answer-was-entailed-by-statement-order.md`](FINDING-20260815-an-arm-whose-answer-was-entailed-by-statement-order.md) | **A probe arm whose predeclared outcome is implied by control flow measures the control flow, not the claim — and moving the observation point cannot repair it.** `OI-120(c)`'s `P4` perturbed `w_truth` and required `event_reco` unchanged, but `event_reco` is finished before the loader reads `w_truth`, so the arm could not fail. **Disposition (a): RETIRED**, replaced by an ordering check that re-derives itself from the source and ships **five mutants that make it fail** — a guard nobody has seen fail is indistinguishable from a guard that cannot fail, which is the defect being retired. Option (b) *move the capture point* costed and rejected as a **non-fix**: `event_reco` is bound once, so a later capture is bit-identical. Also: **retiring a test subject orphans the regressions that used it as a fixture** (3 of `BEN-290`'s tests, re-pointed at live arms, then verified by re-introducing the original bug → 5 RED). Lane D's file; **D authored it and reviewed none of this.** (BEN-330, BEN-331) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-a-worktree-reproduces-the-commit-not-the-working-directory.md`](FINDING-20260815-a-worktree-reproduces-the-commit-not-the-working-directory.md) | **A worktree reproduces the COMMIT, not the WORKING DIRECTORY — so isolating a filesystem-walking generator in one CAUSES the drift it was meant to prevent.** `generate_manifest.py` inventories gitignored artifacts, which a fresh worktree does not have: same commit, clean worktree → `rows=768`, diff **deleting 40 rows**; main checkout → `rows=813`, deleting none, with all 40 verified present and `check-ignore`-positive. **Before isolating a generator in a worktree, ask what it reads that a worktree does not have.** Underneath it: a tracked file that is a function of untracked local caches, so its `--check` gate **cannot be green on two machines at once** — and running one suite's tests added 5 `.pyc` rows, i.e. **verification mutated a tracked artifact** (BEN-332) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-the-rule-is-broken-by-the-artifact-asserting-it.md`](FINDING-20260815-the-rule-is-broken-by-the-artifact-asserting-it.md) | **The moment of writing a rule down is when you are least likely to apply it** — five verified instances in one night, each an artifact violating the rule it states: a stale free-list in the file forbidding narration, an index cell asserting a `grep`-able absence, a byte count derived before the last edit **in the commit shipping a guard against that**, a `grep` offered as corroboration of a derivation, and this lane's own three wrong instruments while verifying the other four. **A sixth proposed member was REFUTED by measurement, which is the strongest evidence for the pattern** — the count itself carried an unverified member (BEN-333, distinct from BEN-228: authorship, not indexes) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-the-file-you-are-about-to-run-already-said-no.md`](FINDING-20260816-the-file-you-are-about-to-run-already-said-no.md) | **A ~6-12 GPU-h run was proposed, and the launcher being proposed carried the denial of that exact run, written the same day** — the instrumentation was designed NOT to justify its own run. **Read the LAUNCHER before approving a run, not the proposal for it.** Plus two premise failures: a correct denial resting on a REFUTED mechanism (the seed is pinned by a frozen policy; the real cause is nondeterminism at sd `0.000820128`) which would have sent a lane to build a seed flag that could not have helped; and a cost figure right only because half the array FAILED at two minutes (BEN-334) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-for-does-x-call-y-the-text-is-not-the-program.md`](FINDING-20260816-for-does-x-call-y-the-text-is-not-the-program.md) | **A grep answered 2 where the truth was 0, and reporting it would have flipped a verdict to BLOCK** — both hits were docstring mentions, one of them the sentence asserting the very absence being tested for. **For "does X call Y", the text is not the program**; four lines of `ast` answer the question actually asked. **The mirror of `BEN-344`**: that is a null that cannot be non-null, this is a positive hit that is not what it appears to be, and `BEN-344`'s remedy would have CONFIRMED it. The same substitution sits in `R11-1` with the polarity reversed (BEN-335) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-the-expected-answer-from-a-broken-instrument.md`](FINDING-20260815-the-expected-answer-from-a-broken-instrument.md) | **The expected answer would have come out of a broken instrument, and the raw estimator manufactured the hypothesis it was testing.** Confirming a zero-weight fraction is `exp(-1)` per column: a MIS-ORDERED row-to-coordinate map yields that same constant, since permuting a constant-rate Bernoulli field still gives `exp(-1)` per bin -- so two positive controls ran BEFORE the measurement (split-point ratio `0.167`, structure `max/min 1.699`) and are reported either way. Result: all 18 columns within `1.9 sigma`, pooled `+2.4e-4`. **The near-miss is the finding: the RAW fraction rose as the column shrank, `+2.82%` at the smallest, about `5 sigma` -- the shape of the hypothesis under test -- caused by 1,916 rows already zero upstream (Stay-Positive clipped) that are zero in every replica regardless of multiplicity.** Generalises `BEN-228` from instruments reporting the wrong number to instruments reporting the RIGHT number for the wrong reason (BEN-341) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-retraction-reaches-only-as-far-as-the-corrector-remembers.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-retraction-reaches-only-as-far-as-the-corrector-remembers.md) | **A retraction is dispatched from the corrector's map of the corpus, so the sites it reliably misses are exactly the ones that prove the error spread — other lanes' documents.** The mediator over-corrected `~105 draw-sd` to *"derives from nothing"* without grepping, was refuted by a recomputation, then enumerated the fix list **from memory** and told lane B two sites when there were three; the third was B's own `BEN-342` row carrying the mediator's wrong claim. **The grep was in hand at discovery and not re-run at retraction.** Carries lane B's formulation — *a null grep is evidence about the search, not about the world* — with **three instances in three lanes in one session**, all caught by someone searching a second time and none by a rule. `BEN-302` |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-constraint-block-is-a-status-report.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-constraint-block-is-a-status-report.md) | **A constraint block is a status report, and it goes stale like any other.** The mediator ended every dispatch for ~5 hours with *"allocation `57128458` is held and idle, leave it up"* — sent to four lanes, in the block whose purpose is binding facts — while `sacct` records it `TIMEOUT` at `18:37:31` and a different allocation `57142574` running from `20:04`. `BEN-027` written into a dozen footers the same night and not applied to the one fact those footers asserted. **Boilerplate looks like policy, and policy is the one thing readers do not re-derive.** Invisible because `alloc_run.sh` auto-requests a fresh allocation, so every run succeeded — **a stale id survives precisely when the tooling is good.** `BEN-303` |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-hash-pin-cannot-see-a-stale-checkout.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-hash-pin-cannot-see-a-stale-checkout.md) | **A digest pin authenticates content against an expectation stored in the SAME tree, so it is structurally blind to the whole tree being stale — both sides go stale together and agree perfectly.** Found during the independent digest confirmation that was to be the last step before launching the authorized arm-1 resubmit. The wrapper pin is correct, maintained and moved four times in two days; the cluster holds the **pre-MOVE-1** wrapper `ee269b09` with a pin literal of `ee269b09`, at a HEAD **663 commits behind local** and 487 behind its own remote. **`G0` would have PASSED and the run would have contained none of the session's work.** A pin detects tampering and records provenance; the log-printed digest is a *forensic* property, not a gate. `BEN-301` |
| [`FINDING-20260817-testing-the-gate-disabled-the-gate.md`](FINDING-20260817-testing-the-gate-disabled-the-gate.md) | **Testing the gate disabled the gate, for every lane, silently.** `git config core.hooksPath` inside a LINKED WORKTREE writes to the SHARED `.git/config` (no `extensions.worktreeConfig`), and a dangling hooks path fails OPEN with no diagnostic — so two commits, one another lane's, ran zero of 9 checks. Caught only by the ABSENCE of the all-passed line. Fix: `git -c key=value <cmd>`, never `git config`, in a throwaway worktree. (BEN-370) |
| [`FINDING-20260817-corroboration-and-echo-have-opposite-value.md`](FINDING-20260817-corroboration-and-echo-have-opposite-value.md) | **Corroboration and echo have OPPOSITE evidential value, so a relay that omits the ROUTE destroys the difference rather than degrading it** — `BEN-312` inverted: real independent corroboration read as an echo. A separate row because one recording only the inflation direction teaches readers to discount agreement, making this failure likelier — *a lesson and its own iatrogenic side effect* (lane A's argument). Check: **when relaying a claim, say where it came from.** Also: the route matters for a claim's DEPENDENCIES (a true capability carried without its specification), a name shared by two live sessions forced a lane to DECLINE credit, and the general form — **ship what would let a reader falsify this**, useful in four registers in one day. (BEN-248) |
| [`FINDING-20260817-a-source-line-citation-has-no-immutable-handle.md`](FINDING-20260817-a-source-line-citation-has-no-immutable-handle.md) | **`BEN-254`'s digest remedy is unavailable for a SOURCE-LINE citation, and the substitute already exists in the tree at 58 sites: CITE THE LINE AND QUOTE IT** — a mutable module has no digestible object (`git grep` of `unified_throw_cov.py`'s sha256 → 0 hits) and a whole-file digest cannot address a line. `2004` `.py` line citations over `186` modules, `58` carrying quoted source text (a FLOOR). `verify_hash_bindings.py` reads GREEN on the edit, correctly, about the wrong question; four receipt citations + one ledger row would rot silently. Includes a correction to my own first report of it (BEN-249) |
| [`FINDING-20260818-a-marker-based-power-test-assumes-contiguity.md`](FINDING-20260818-a-marker-based-power-test-assumes-contiguity.md) | **A marker-based power test assumes the logic it guards is TEXTUALLY CONTIGUOUS, and refactoring for testability is exactly what breaks that** — propagation puts the value in a second place by design, a single-source helper puts the computation in a third, and neither is inside a marker excision. **D's failed LOUDLY only because it asserts on TWO tokens; a single-token variant would have gone on passing while controlling nothing.** Includes my own repair leaving unparseable source that every token assertion PASSED on, caught by D's `ast.parse` arm. Fourth instrument that evening to execute successfully and be blind. `BEN-480`, lane B's third block `480-489` |
| [`FINDING-20260817-the-floor-shape-noise-is-heterogeneous.md`](FINDING-20260817-the-floor-shape-noise-is-heterogeneous.md) | **The GPU floor's shape-noise is HETEROGENEOUS across the OI-126 tail, and the ceiling test that appeared to corroborate it has ZERO power.** Per-cell `floor_rel_sd/family_rel_sd` over the 63 tail cells at n=5: median `0.1581`, 3 of 63 above `0.5`, max `0.8924`. A homogeneous null predicts **zero** cells above `0.5` and a max of `0.304` — `P < 0.0001` on both, so the heterogeneity is real and not a small-sample artifact. **THE COUNT IS WELL-SUPPORTED, THE LIST IS NOT** (winner's curse). Shape-only on `central_vector`, so NOT a claim about the tail's composition, and it does not distinguish OI-126's (a) from (b). **A bounded statistic evaluated far from its bound carries no information beyond the location statistic** — `BEN-391`'s third instance and a distinct sub-species: construction sound, OPERATING POINT makes failure impossible. | mediator |
| [`FINDING-20260817-a-seed-census-that-cannot-reach-the-product-it-grades.md`](FINDING-20260817-a-seed-census-that-cannot-reach-the-product-it-grades.md) | **Cause 3's `P` leg is graded MET on a seed census that globs `.npz` slabs, is never stamped into any product, and cannot see the 188-universe upstream at all** — and the candidate carries no seed key of any kind (13 keys, none a seed). The dominant block's `42` holds by hardcoding (`sweep_bank_5d.py:252`), with no flag, no stamp and no census at any of three hops. Recommends withdrawal to PARTIAL; cell not edited (`BEN-381`). Reusable check: ask where a guard's result is WRITTEN, and whether that reaches the artifact being graded. (BEN-246) |
| [`FINDING-20260818-the-denominator-is-never-bounded.md`](FINDING-20260818-the-denominator-is-never-bounded.md) | **A GREEN VERDICT BOUNDS THE NUMERATOR OF FAILURES AND NEVER THE DENOMINATOR OF THINGS EXAMINED — three instances in one afternoon, on three layers of one change.** The pinned validator reported `n_failed = 1` having run **22 of 77** check sites (an early return on a missing required key skips 55); an extraction branch reviewed as *written* had **never executed once** (unreachable, and `return dict(...)` where both callers unpack a 3-tuple — three defects each hiding the next); and a control suite printed `Ran 30 tests … OK` while pytest collected **61**, the 31 hidden ones being every F1/F2/F3 provenance control. The covering fix found **two more test modules I do not own — 80 hidden controls across three files, all three printing OK** — one repaired, one hash-pinned and therefore **declared with its digest so the exemption expires on re-issue**. **Not staleness: every number was correct when read, the population was smaller than the report's shape implied**, so re-measuring does not help and only naming the denominator does. (`BEN-415`, `BEN-416`, `BEN-417`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-a-check-that-reads-prose-as-code.md`](FINDING-20260818-a-check-that-reads-prose-as-code.md) | **SEVEN negative controls of mine failed on PROSE that mentioned the token while explaining why the code does not do the thing -- and the remedy was wrong twice more.** `ast.unparse` DROPS COMMENTS BUT PRESERVES DOCSTRINGS, so the fix adopted after instances 1-3 was true and incomplete; and instance 6 defeats every prose-stripper because the token was in a RETURN VALUE, which IS code -- **a substring absence check cannot distinguish a MENTION from a USE**, so the property is about ACCESSES and wants an AST walk. Plus: a negative-only helper that has never returned True is unverified (give it a positive control), a covering audit of all 13 unparse sites found TWO MORE latently vulnerable checks that were not failing, and a bracket-counting regex over source containing brackets is the wrong instrument. (`BEN-418`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-the-first-end-to-end-run-refutes-your-model-of-the-system.md`](FINDING-20260818-the-first-end-to-end-run-refutes-your-model-of-the-system.md) | **A module with 165 passing unit controls COULD NOT GRADE A SINGLE MEMBER, and its first execution as a PROGRAM refuted my model of the system three times in ten minutes.** `ModuleNotFoundError: pet_bootstrap` -- a LAZY import inside a callee two directories up, invisible both to the header and to any predicate exercised under pytest's own `sys.path`. Then my end-to-end fixture was wrong TWICE and the validator was right both times (`np.ones` for a Poisson draw; placeholder digests). **When a NEGATIVE control fails you suspect the code; when a POSITIVE control fails the pull is toward RELAXING THE PREDICATE** -- and every ingredient of that reasoning is something you wrote. **A unit control is written FROM the model, so it cannot test the part of the model it rests on**: 165 controls agreed with each other and with me. Checks: run it as a subprocess from a clean cwd with the launcher's arguments; expect the first end-to-end fixture to be wrong; and count how many controls share an assumption. (`BEN-470`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-a-task-can-fail-with-its-products-complete.md`](FINDING-20260818-a-task-can-fail-with-its-products-complete.md) | **A task can FAIL with its products COMPLETE AND CORRECT -- `BEN-023`'s MIRROR.** `57194054` finished 49 COMPLETED / 1 FAILED; the failure (`_42`, exit `129:0` = SIGHUP, elapsed `00:49:28`) had already printed its PASS line **with the target digest**, written both artifacts AND both `.done` markers, and produced an `.npy` byte-for-byte the size of its neighbours' -- mtimes equal the job's End time and stderr ends in a teardown stack trace. **Ruled out the alternative rather than assuming it:** neighbours' stderr is 1,164 B with 0 trace lines against `_42`'s 121,068 B with 14, so the verdict split carries information. **A SLURM EXIT STATUS DESCRIBES THE PROCESS, NOT THE PRODUCT** -- and `BEN-023`'s completeness-not-existence rule prevents a false DISCARD as well as a false pass, so it is right in both directions where existence-checking is wrong in both. `exit != 0` and `product incomplete` are independent facts; four combinations, and reading two of them treats an exit code as a receipt. (`BEN-471`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-a-watch-armed-on-a-guards-condition-waits-forever.md`](FINDING-20260818-a-watch-armed-on-a-guards-condition-waits-forever.md) | **A watch armed on a GUARD's own condition can wait forever, and arming it that way felt like the rigorous choice.** I set a background watch on exactly the predicate the submit controller tests -- `squeue … | wc -l -> 0` -- and reported *"still draining"* while `57194055_[22-31]` sat `PENDING (DependencyNeverSatisfied)`: **terminal but not terminating**, since `kill_invalid_depend` is unset here, so the condition could never be met. **A guard's condition is a predicate on THE PRESENT; a watch needs one on REACHABILITY** -- `no` is always a correct answer to *may I submit now?*, and copying the guard's predicate inherits its blind spot in the one direction a watch is useless: silence. Repair fires on EITHER terminal shape and SAYS WHICH, turning an unbounded wait into a named decision; re-armed, it fired in one poll. Also measured: the block was NOT the `aftercorr` consequence of the one failed target (`_42`'s training task released and ran) but a contiguous `%10` release window -- recorded as suggestive, not diagnosed. `BEN-028`'s mirror: there silence meant healthy, here stuck. (`BEN-472`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-the-scheduler-reports-five-times-and-i-kept-picking-the-easiest.md`](FINDING-20260818-the-scheduler-reports-five-times-and-i-kept-picking-the-easiest.md) | **THREE wrong scheduler numbers in one session, all the same mistake: I reached for the time quantity CHEAPEST TO OBTAIN rather than the one the question was about.** A RUNNING task's `Elapsed` read as its duration (**2.8x low**); wall-hours summed and labelled node-hours on a shared partition where `AllocTRES billing=36` of 128 is the whole story (**3.6x high**); and `Elapsed` used to predict how fast an array DRAINS, when dispatch is queue-limited -- `Start` deltas show pairs every ~6.7 min despite `%10` (**50-140x low**, 3 min vs 2.3-7 h, with a decision resting on it). In all three the right field was ONE AWAY in the same output. **The tell is that the number came out CONVENIENT** -- three for three the easy quantity flattered the answer I was already inclined toward -- and none looks like an error: a running job's elapsed is not a warning, it is a smaller true number. (`BEN-473`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-an-instrument-must-reproduce-its-subjects-conditions.md`](FINDING-20260818-an-instrument-must-reproduce-its-subjects-conditions.md) | **AN INSTRUMENT MUST REPRODUCE ITS SUBJECT'S CONDITIONS -- stricter is a false alarm, looser is a false pass, and neither shows in its output.** Two verification scripts hours apart: the run-bound addendum's zero-artifact refusal **could not fire**, run from a laptop against a `/pscratch` root that does not exist there, so its claim was true BY TIMING and not because the check established it -- which is the one distinction the check exists to make. And an activation smoke failed three times on a HEALTHY system by imposing `set -u` the launcher does not set, then `env -i` when the job inherits `--export=ALL`, then tripping on a conda warning that **2 of gen1's 50 tasks emitted and COMPLETED**. Repairs: refuse when the root is not visible from here; and where faithfulness was unreachable, NARROW THE CLAIM rather than widen the check -- the encodable property gates, activation is INFO, because a gate that fails on a healthy system gets switched off as fast as an under-reporting one gets trusted. (`BEN-474`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260818-a-constraint-and-a-coincidence-are-indistinguishable-at-n-1.md`](FINDING-20260818-a-constraint-and-a-coincidence-are-indistinguishable-at-n-1.md) | **The Gate-5 family validator is scoped to ONE RUN, and nobody could have noticed because there has only ever been one.** `ARRAY_JOB_ID`/`EXPECTED_HEAD`/`EXPECTED_CODE` name campaign `56857233`, load-bearing at 6 further sites, so the module cannot validate any second run -- a three-stream re-run included. **A CONSTRAINT AND A COINCIDENCE ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE AT N=1** (lane D). Distinguishing property: a pin naming a CODE STATE is reusable, a pin naming a RUN is not, so the defect is a NAME/SCOPE MISMATCH not hardcoding. **The obvious mechanical check is vacuous** -- `*_family_*.py` matches one test file and flags zero, missing the defect entirely -- so the rule is structural: a module-level run-id literal used as an equality operand in a function taking a member index. 5 run-id pins tree-wide, 1 a defect; `reconcile_gate5_family.py` verified clean. (`BEN-419`) | lane E |
| [`FINDING-20260817-cause3-C-leg-does-not-cover-the-dominant-block.md`](FINDING-20260817-cause3-C-leg-does-not-cover-the-dominant-block.md) | **Cause 3's `C` leg is INAPPLICABLE to the dominant block, and the answer is a THIRD branch rather than either of the two anticipated** — the block's products are not in the guard's population at all, so there is no seed provenance on that path to be present or absent. The 169 per-universe unfolds hardcode `seed=42` (`sweep_bank_5d.py:252`, no CLI flag) and write a `.root` stamping only `ndim`/`globalCompleteness`/`dataPOT`/`hXSecND_flat` — no seed. | [`EXTENT-20260817-2850-a100h-scope-and-missing-legs.md`](EXTENT-20260817-2850-a100h-scope-and-missing-legs.md) | **`28.50 A100-h` is wrong twice and both ways understate: it undercounts its own lateral arm (5 universes costed, 19 ran → `39.078`, `+37.1 %`, §0) and it is `99.6 %` of the GPU bill and `0 %` of a CPU bill that is the larger half.** One additional estimator seed across all four blocks is `39.22` A100-h **plus** `55.34` CPU task-hours (`2,764.7` core-h); at the predeclared `n>=6`, `235.34` A100-h + `332.02` CPU task-h. | [`FINDING-20260817-a-trailer-that-survives-the-rebase-that-invalidates-it.md`](FINDING-20260817-a-trailer-that-survives-the-rebase-that-invalidates-it.md) | **`git rebase` does not run `pre-commit`, so the new `Checks:` trailer arrives on a tree the hook never saw.** Measured within the hour of the trailer's deployment: a hand-resolved rebase conflict changed `CATALOG.md` and the resulting commit kept `Checks: 9 passed`. The inverse of `BEN-370` and worse — a durable PRESENCE asserting a check that did not run. `commit → rebase → push` is this repo's normal path, so the exposure is routine. Fix: verify at PUSH time, or bind the trailer to a tree sha. (BEN-382) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-recomputation-identity-cannot-validate-its-premise.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-recomputation-identity-cannot-validate-its-premise.md) | **A recomputation identity cannot validate its own premise — and the independent route prescribed to fix `N3` would have failed the bar `N3` was written for.** Four corruptions all invisible to the identity leg at `1.5e-16 .. 5.0e-16`. **Its impossibility claim was NARROWED 2026-08-16 after an independent second read refuted it: `M`-only structural invariants catch 3 of the 4; only the RELABELING class provably needs the recipe** — and the invariant's whole bite is a coverage-conditional clause that cannot hold on the production mask, so the recipe gate does all the work here anyway. Repaired as TWO gates — a genuine non-matmul block sum (and it is 6.6x FASTER than the BLAS product at the real shape) plus an exact rebuild of `M` from its recipe by a deliberately different algorithm. Before/after is one command: pre-repair exits 2, repaired exits 0. (BEN-328) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-zero-times-nan-defeats-sparsity.md`](FINDING-20260816-zero-times-nan-defeats-sparsity.md) | **`0.0 * nan` is `nan`, so sparsity is not a containment argument — one bad input bin poisons EVERY output bin.** Found by an expectation of mine that was wrong: predicted 1 of 4 non-finite, measured 4 of 4. On the real products one bad 5D bin of 10,694 takes all 4,825 4D bins, and `n_over_3pct` reports **0** because `nan > 0.03` is False — the most reassuring possible rendering of "the input was unusable". `REPORT ONLY` kept; counts and `*_finite_only` summaries added, and the fact put on the printed line. (BEN-329) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-null-that-could-not-have-been-otherwise.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-null-that-could-not-have-been-otherwise.md) | **A null that could not have been otherwise is not weak evidence -- it is not evidence.** repair-10 `#8`'s snapshot-side `grep -c` returning 0 was structurally incapable of returning anything else: the same grep returned 0 for EVERY module because `summary()` recorded the sweep's output and never its scope. Sharper than `BEN-315` -- there the search was real. **Compounding: closing the gap produced no signal either (`n_fields 115 -> 115`), so a gap invisible when closed was invisible when opened.** Includes an audit of all seven repair-10 defects' evidence: one half artifact, one measuring neglect rather than inadequacy (`#7`), five properly evidenced -- so NOT systemic (BEN-344) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-guard-that-could-not-be-observed-where-it-was-needed.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-guard-that-could-not-be-observed-where-it-was-needed.md) | **When a guard's precondition contradicts the precondition for running the test suite, the answer is a second process, not a caveat.** repair-10 `N6` sat open three rounds on an impossibility that was true of the suite's process and false of a subprocess. Observing it found the one stratum that ERRORS (171 pytest-fixture items) completely unguarded, showed that a faithful reproduction of the sandbox prevents pytest from STARTING — bounding the guard's reach and proving conftest.py's account of the original failure wrong — and corrected four of my own claims, including `BEN-342` reproduced by the lane that filed it. **Carries the negative-side twin of `BEN-344`: a guard gets a test that it fires; a filter gets a test that it does not.** 6/6 mutants caught |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-spent-authorization-reads-as-a-pending-one.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-spent-authorization-reads-as-a-pending-one.md) | **An authorization is written to permit work that has not happened; once it happens the document does not change, so it reads forever as the prospective one.** A 5.9 GPU-h re-run was nearly authorized for a job that had completed the day before. **The remedy I drafted — grep the receipts — is fail-open, measured 7/17**, because this repo's paper trail has no backward links: every artifact names its antecedents, none its consequences. **The authority for "has this run?" is the scheduler, not the paperwork.** |
| [`FINDING-20260816-an-environment-prescription-that-travels-in-a-message.md`](FINDING-20260816-an-environment-prescription-that-travels-in-a-message.md) | **10/10 identical failures is a property of the harness, not of ten independently produced files.** A probe run under a relayed-in-a-message environment reported ten corrupt physics products; the real cause was `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ROOT'` behind a `2>/dev/null`, and the correct environment gave the opposite answer. **A script that cares must source its own environment and print the resolved interpreter into its output.** Second instance the same day: `/tmp` is node-local. |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-test-that-fails-from-a-worktree-behind-main.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-test-that-fails-from-a-worktree-behind-main.md) | **A test that fails for anyone running the suite from a worktree behind `main`.** It asserts the symbolic rev `'main'` is in history, and `code_rev_in_history` means ANCESTOR of `HEAD` -- so in a worktree on a feature branch it fails whenever another lane has pushed. Measured: `main 6e05985` vs `HEAD e07b986`, 1 commit ahead, not an ancestor; cleared on rebase, mechanism re-measured. `BEN-332`'s shape with the dependency moved from untracked caches to the runner's branch position. Costs every worktree lane a triage they cannot skip; the fix is free because `HEAD`/`HEAD~0`/`HEAD~3` already cover the symbolic case and `'main'` adds only environment dependence (BEN-343) |
| [`FINDING-20260816-a-fixture-degenerate-on-the-axis-under-test.md`](FINDING-20260816-a-fixture-degenerate-on-the-axis-under-test.md) | **A power test licenses ONE axis, and the fixture is numerically degenerate on the axis that matters.** `67c94df`'s end-of-run recorder is CORRECT (checked against the definition at `train_fullevent_nominal.py:553-577`, not against its own reduction). Its bit-identity test is built right and power-tested against a wrong-MOMENT capture -- but the fixture's push is UNIFORM, and `sum(w*push)/sum(w) = push` for any `w`, so reco-leg and truth-leg reductions come out **bit-identical** and a leg substitution would pass unnoticed on the one axis this codebase documents as hazardous. Unexercised, not wrong; one-line fix measured. Sharpens `BEN-314`: one control licenses only the axis it perturbs. Plus a `BEN-361` instance -- *"75.8 draw-sd"* re-derives to `75.8` (BEN-342) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-a-share-of-total-without-bin-widths.md`](FINDING-20260815-a-share-of-total-without-bin-widths.md) | **A "share of the cross-section" computed without bin widths on a density array, in a receipt whose own headline totals ARE width-weighted — so the receipt contradicts itself from its own shipped operands.** `3.1%` of the cross section is `15.5%`; and the step the receipt calls a *same-domain* correction (`1.10267`→`1.05656`) removes the **bin widths, not a domain** — both figures are over the same 257 cells, the domain contributes `2.9e-5`, so **the `+10.267%` survives**. Caught purely by failing to re-derive a published total from published arrays: `sum(nom·width)` matches it to `2.9e-5` while the unweighted sum is `11.3x` off, and the residual is the 5 flicker cells to `0.7%`. **`BEN-077` working as designed, second time in one day.** Transferable qualifier: **the defect is invisible in a ratio** — widths appear to cancel and only fail to because numerator and denominator weight the cells differently, so *"it's a ratio, units drop out"* is the reasoning that produces it. Worst cost was not the number: the wrong figures reached a handoff's **`do not re-investigate`** heading, which is an instruction not to look. **Does NOT overturn the receipt's conclusion** — the concentrated-tail verdict is right and now better supported (`0.99471` on dropping the 63) (BEN-340) |
| [`FINDING-20260815-who-holds-a-task-has-no-source.md`](FINDING-20260815-who-holds-a-task-has-no-source.md) | **The holder of a task is a hand-maintained fact with no machine-derivable source.** Leg 0 dispatched twice; lane A silently re-derived `692c6bd` and dropped ~40 min. **`BEN-228` one level up** — a stale index of *who is doing what*, and unlike every `BEN-228` instance there is **no source to derive from**, so *derive, do not narrate* has no purchase. Lanes keep their names across kill/respawn, `claude -p` lanes are invisible to `ListAgents`, and a dispatch is a message — so the only record of the holding lives in the context a kill destroys (BEN-300) |
| `FINDING-20260814-two-implementations-one-blas-kernel.md` | **Two "independent" implementations agreed BIT-FOR-BIT because both dispatch to the same BLAS kernel** — `Xc.T@Xc` vs `np.einsum`, exactly `0.000e+00`, found while calibrating the `OI-121` comparator tolerance. An agreement-between-implementations argument is only as strong as the claim that two *computations* occurred; two authors is not two computations. Remedy routed to the judge, not the comparator, since reading the builds would end the referee role (BEN-188) |

| [`FINDING-20260815-a-blockers-array-that-vetoed-authorized-work.md`](FINDING-20260815-a-blockers-array-that-vetoed-authorized-work.md) | **Three of four `blockers` in the control plane were false for two days — one of them forbidding the Gate-6 retry Joseph had authorized — in the first file `CLAUDE.md` routes every session to.** Mechanism: a hand-authored input classified `generated`/`immutable` so it looks machine-owned, nobody edits it, and the renderer keeps producing a fresh-looking document around stale prose. **And the documented remedy is inert: `MANIFEST-overrides.tsv` is clobbered for every `state/` path AND the dead override is counted as APPLIED.** (BEN-321) |

Fixed on the unpinned hop, so the pinned file's stamp becomes true untouched. (BEN-326) |
