| BEN-426 | **A FIELD CAN BE UNDER-DIMENSIONED RATHER THAN OVERLOADED — and then NO VALUE WORKS, which is why five candidates fail for five DIFFERENT reasons instead of converging on a near-miss.** `bootstrap_seed` encodes a **two-bit state** — *did it draw?* and *which draw?* — **in a one-field encoding that assumed the bits were correlated**: three-stream = drew, seed `S`; nominal = did not draw, `-1`; **data-only = did not draw the MC but DID draw the data — the corner the projection discards.** So the five options closed for five unrelated reasons: `50000+idx` asserts a draw that did not happen; `None` throws `TypeError` at `validate_gate5_training_artifacts.py:224`; **`-1` INVERTS a pinned guard**; absence costs 55 checks. **`"The artifact genuinely does not have the property the field names"` (lane E) is the diagnosis; UNDER-DIMENSIONED is the reason, and it is why the search for a value was doomed from the start.** **THE `-1` INVERSION, verified: `extract_fullevent_fps.py:163` defaults `bootstrap_seed` to `-1` and `:178` raises on `!= -1` — so `-1` is that guard's POSITIVE TEST for *"this is the nominal, not a replica"*, and writing it into 50 data-only REPLICA artifacts makes every one pass a guard whose entire job is to refuse replicas.** 18 digest sites, so no consumer-side fix. **And my own `VL130` precedent argument is what killed the option: `-1` already means *nominal, no draw*, the repo SPENT `-1` on nominal, and a replica-that-did-not-draw has no room in the encoding.** A sound precedent argument whose strength is the refutation. **AND A LEG NOBODY STATED: ABSENCE DOES NOT SAVE `:178` EITHER.** `:163`'s default IS `-1`, so an artifact **missing** the key also passes — the guard cannot distinguish *nominal* from *field absent*. **Both `-1` and absence pass; only a real seed makes it fire, and that value is false. So `:178` cannot be satisfied HONESTLY and CORRECTLY by any data-only artifact — it is not a guard to satisfy, it is a guard the product must never REACH.** **RULE: a guard that cannot be satisfied honestly is a ROUTING CONSTRAINT, not a value problem — and reading it as a value problem is what kept five dead options alive.** Requirement: assert POSITIVELY, in the unpinned replica extractor, that the nominal extractor is never invoked on a data-only artifact. **`V3`'s MIDDLE CLAUSE SHARPENED BY ITS FIRST REAL FAILURE.** `reconcile_gate5_family.py:628` and `:343` read `int(r.get("bootstrap_seed", -1))`, so the predicted `got` is `-1` **whether the field was correctly stamped `-1` or lost entirely** — *"failed exactly as predicted"* **cannot discriminate a correct artifact from a corrupted one there.** **So a manifest entry is ADMISSIBLE ONLY IF ITS PREDICTED `got` IS REACHED BY EXACTLY ONE ARTIFACT STATE: the clause's discriminating power is a property of the PREDICTED VALUE, not of the clause, and a value many states share is not a prediction.** *(My own third condition — `R2` extends from key names to VALUES — returning a `FAIL` on my own amendment.)* **AND THE STRUCTURAL RULING: `V1`'s WRAPPER CLAIM IS DROPPED FOR THIS VALIDATOR, AND THE OBLIGATION IT WAS PROTECTING IS KEPT.** 55 replacements of 77 is **71% reimplementation**, so `V1` would be true in form and false in substance — and E's line governs: **it would not buy `V1`'s purity with a field whose value is false.** **Kept instead: every one of the 77 pinned check sites lands in EXACTLY ONE of DELEGATED / UNEXECUTED-BY-CONSTRUCTION (each with a NAMED replacement) / MANIFEST (discriminating `got`), with the three counts SUMMING TO 77 against the module's static site count.** **`V1` made DIVERGENCE unrepresentable by delegation; the partition makes OMISSION unrepresentable by ACCOUNTING.** **And the cost is named rather than avoided: the honest state is absence, absence costs 55 unexecuted checks, so the question was never how to avoid that cost but who pays it and how visibly.** [full](RULING-20260817-lanec-data-only-target-family-and-launchers.md) *Lane C (PET). `(e)` was mine and is withdrawn; the inversion was found by lane E and verified here, the closing argument is the mediator's, and the absence-also-passes leg and the partition are mine.* | `OI-126` | EP-2026-08-17-data-only-cstat |
| BEN-427 | **A NEGATIVE CONTROL THAT FIRES ON NOTHING IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE THAT FIRES CORRECTLY, UNLESS YOU READ THE OUTPUT RATHER THAN THE EXIT CODE — and REPAIRING BEFORE CHECKING SPENDS THE ONLY NATURALLY-OCCURRING INSTANCE OF THE DEFECT.** Two coupled rules, both measured on my own work this turn. **(1) THE ORDERING COST, which lane A gave as a preference and I can now price.** A recommended *the field-count assertion first, the `CLM-006` pipe repair second*, on the ground that *"fixing the one row without it just resets the clock"*. **I repaired first. The cost is not the clock — it is that the check could no longer be power-tested against a REAL failure**, because the only malformed row in the file was the one I had just fixed. **Recovered from git rather than synthesised (`b8a0e117^` carries the pre-repair row), which is strictly better than a hand-built fixture — but the recovery was available only by luck of the defect being recent.** **RULE: write the check before the repair, or the repair consumes your negative control.** **(2) AND THE NEAR-MISS IS THE SHARPER HALF.** My first attempt pulled the control from **`c456493f^`** — **the MERGE's FIRST PARENT, i.e. the already-repaired tip** — so the control produced **no field-count failure and read as a pass.** **A control that fires on nothing looks EXACTLY like one that fires correctly: same clean output, same exit code, and the check ships unproven on the defect it was written for.** Caught only by asking *"where is the `13 between-pipe fields` line?"* rather than by checking the exit status. **RULE: a negative control is not run until its EXPECTED FAILURE TEXT has been read. Assert the failure, never the exit code.** *(Third instance of this class for me — the empty extracted guard file that made `python3` exit 0 on a P5A negative control, `BEN-409`'s controls requirement, and this.)* **And the specific trap is worth naming: `<merge>^` is the merge's first parent, which on a lane branch is YOUR OWN pre-merge tip — so `^` after a merge walks toward the state you just created, not away from it.** **(3) A THIRD DEFECT IN THE SAME TOOL, FOUND BY LANE A AND MINE: my failure message asserted one row's facts about another.** `claims_table_lint.py:106` hardcoded *"the declaration may be PARTIAL — CLM-010 names an independent verifier for the F-test…"*, so **`CLM-012`'s failure line told a reader that `CLM-012` has a verified component. It does not — it is wholly single-source, which is the exact contrast the message was rewritten to preserve.** `BEN-382`'s shape at the finest possible scale: **the row's evidence not bound to the row.** **Fixed by DERIVING the label from each row's own text** — does the self-declaration open the field (`WHOLLY`) or follow other content (`PARTIAL`)? — **never from a hardcoded example.** Verified both branches fire on the two real rows rather than on fixtures. *(Context: the lint itself found `CLM-010`, which no manual sweep had — a promoted `VERIFIED-NUMERIC` whose verifier field says *"the assembly … is so far single-source (this session) and has NOT been independently checked"*. So A's *"the assertion is what stops the next one"* understates it: it found an existing one on its first run, in a file a human had just swept.)* [full](DETERMINATION-20260818-lanec-clm012-status-conflates-two-claims.md) *Lane C (PET). | BEN-428 | **MEASURED-ZERO IS CONTINGENT; *THE CONSUMER NO LONGER ASKS THE QUESTION* IS STRUCTURAL — close on the second and cite the first as corroboration, never the reverse. Close on the structural ground and cite the count as corroboration — stating it the other way round makes the closure hostage to a population.** **And one clause carried: a REOPENING TRIGGER — any new writer of that receipt shape — because 13-of-13 agreement is exactly the coincidence a new writer breaks without noticing.** **(2) THE ALLOCATOR GAP.** Two lanes independently allocated `OI-64` and `OI-65`. **`FINDINGS.md` has a per-lane closed ten-block allocator with a written derive-don't-narrate rule; `OI-*` has NOTHING — so two lanes computing `max + 1` is not a mistake either made, it is the only thing either could do.** Adopting `OI-64A`/`OI-64C` (shared stem, no renumber, so no already-pushed citation breaks — renumbering is `BEN-082`'s misread in its least detectable direction, **a citation that RESOLVES, to a row about something else**) treats the symptom correctly and **does not touch the cause.** Recorded so the collision is not filed against a lane's attention. **And the reason sub-parts `(f)`/`(g)` are safe where `max+1` is not: the mechanism is `max+1` computed INDEPENDENTLY BY TWO LANES, and a sub-part's namespace has ONE OWNER.** **RULE: single-owner namespace → self-allocate; shared namespace → allocator.** Which is exactly why `FINDINGS.md`'s device is a per-lane closed block rather than a global counter. **(3) AND A DISAMBIGUATION IMPROVEMENT: a bare pre-cutover citation resolving *"by the banner"* is resolution by READING AND JUDGING. census receipt landed `75fc88df` `2026-08-17T04:24:21-04:00`. **It was ACCURATE WHEN WRITTEN.** So this is nobody's misreading: **the map author's row was true, the dispatcher's judgement was sound, and the defect is that a dispatch inherits a map's `asOf` and nothing carries it forward.** Distinct from `BEN-228` (a stale index) and from `BEN-239` (evidence unread): **here the document was correct at its own timestamp and the timestamp is the missing operand.** **RULE, and it is the executable form: a dispatch derived from a routing document must STATE THAT DOCUMENT'S LAST-TOUCHED SHA AND TIME, so a reader can see whether the state it describes could have moved.** One field. `git log -1 --format=%h %cI -- <map>` — and it is the same discipline as binding a number to a sha, applied to a *task assignment* rather than a measurement. **WHAT ACTUALLY REMAINS IS ONE SENTENCE OF JUDGEMENT, NOT FOUR HOURS OF AUDIT**, and the receipt's author named it: **`"M MEASURED is not M ACCEPTABLE. Whether this magnitude leaves X's published numbers standing is a physics-presentation judgement and is NOT taken here."`** *(Usable figures: total ratios `1.0594` / `1.0313`, per-band medians `2.10` / `1.78`. **NOT the distribution's `max = 2.53e+22`, which is a near-zero-denominator artefact — the receipt already declines to report a max, `BEN-064` honoured.**)* **AND TWO SECOND-ORDER CORRECTIONS TO THE DISPATCH.** (i) It is **not cluster-free**: the script's own usage line is *"on Perlmutter, inside the ROOT env; source setup_salloc_env.sh"* and it opens ROOTs on scratch — **so *"no cluster time"* is wrong and I could not have executed it from this host regardless** (no Slurm client, no `/pscratch`). (ii) **`M` does NOT transfer to a replacement product, and the CRITERION says so rather than me**: `CRITERIA-20260811` §2 cause 1 asks for *"X built both ways on **X's OWN bank**"*. **So the magnitude is bank-scoped; the `C` leg (code path) transfers if the candidate shares the modules, `P` and `M` do not.** The mediator raised exactly this before authorising the spend — **the right question, arriving one step after the spend it would have prevented had already been made by someone else.** [full](FINDING-20260818-a-maps-currency-is-not-its-correctness.md) *Lane C (PET). THERE IS NO SHARED SEED — my wording assumed a fact and specified on top of it.** **THE RESOLUTION COMES FROM THE MECHANISM I CONCEDED ON:** the retired jitter term (`a0cdc01:225-227`) — *"the block units + `x_cv` all share one seed, **so their jitter cancels in `(x_b − x_cv)`**"* — **means estimator noise is CORRELATED between legs sharing a seed and independent between legs that do not. So *coherent* refers to WHICH LEGS SHARE NOISE WITH WHICH, and the measured structure is TWO COHERENCE GROUPS: `{42}` internally correlated, `{1000}` internally correlated, the two mutually independent.** **RULED (ii) OFFSET, and (i) REFUSED ON PHYSICS INDEPENDENTLY OF COST.** A common value `S` makes all four legs correlated — **so it does not vary the existing product's estimator noise, it measures a DIFFERENT product whose correlation structure the option itself created**, answering a question nobody asked about an object that does not exist. *(B's missing-anchor objection to specification:** ~~*"a coherent variation of the shared seed"*~~ → ***"a variation that moves every leg's estimator seed while PRESERVING each leg's seed-sharing relationships — a common OFFSET from | BEN-463 | **A NUISANCE TERM'S *DIRECTION* CAN SETTLE A DISPOSITION THAT ITS *MAGNITUDE* CANNOT — and the rule is an asymmetry: a bias toward the CONSERVATIVE verdict is DECLARABLE, a bias toward PASS must be CORRECTED.** `M(ii)`'s anchor `k=0` carries two seed coincidences (`g1 -> 42` inside bootstrap replica seeds `[1,100]`; `g2 -> 1000` inside per-throw draw seeds `[1000,1159]`), **both properties of the PUBLISHED ARCHIVE rather than of the scan** — so member 0 differs structurally from members 1..49, which is what the attributability constraint forbids. Three dispositions were on the table (declare / drop the anchor / rule it immaterial) and **all three are answerable from ONE LINE OF ALGEBRA:** contaminating one member of `n` gives `E[s^2] = sigma^2 + sigma_d^2/n >= sigma^2` (lane A's simplification of my `sigma_d^2 (1-1/n)/(n-1)`, identical for all `n` and **making the `1/n` scaling visible, which is why `n=6` is four times worse than `n=50`**), **so the confound inflates the measured spread and therefore biases toward UNMET — AGAINST the negligibility claim the bar tests.** **A verdict of MET reached DESPITE an upward bias cannot have been manufactured by it, so MET stands with the confound declared; UNMET is NOT declarable until the magnitude is bounded, because there the confound points the same way as the verdict.** **The contrast that makes it a rule rather than a case: `c4(n)` biases the same sd DOWNWARD, toward PASS, and is therefore CORRECTED rather than declared.** Same asymmetry as `INCONCLUSIVE -> NOT MET`, applied to a nuisance term instead of to a bound. **AND ONE OF THE THREE OPTIONS TURNED OUT NOT TO EXIST, WHICH IS THE STRUCTURAL HALF: *drop the anchor and anchor differently* is unavailable, because A CLEAN ANCHOR IS NOT AN ANCHOR.** The anchor's function is to reproduce the published product exactly; the published product HAS the coincidences (production ran `--estimator-seed 1000` while throw 0's draw RNG is `default_rng(1000+0)`); **so a run without them is not the archive and a run reproducing the archive has them — the confound and the anchoring are the same fact.** Consequence for pricing: a failed `P-ANCHOR` costs a member AND leaves the confound in place, because there is no clean anchor to buy. **AND THE EXEMPTION'S FORM IS LOAD-BEARING: express it as a two-entry COINCIDENCE ALLOWLIST, each entry carrying where it was measured — NOT as `j != 0`.** A member skip passes *any* coincidence at the anchor, including one introduced later by widening an `--array`; an allowlist of two FAILS when a third appears, **and a third appearing at the anchor is precisely the event nobody would notice, because the anchor is the member everyone has already agreed is special.** The exemption is a NARROWING, so it gets a test that it does NOT fire — a fixture with a third coincidence the predicate must still reject. **AND MY OWN ERROR, CAUGHT BEFORE PUBLISHING AND RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THIS LANE'S MOST-REPEATED ONE: I closed the magnitude gap by pairing the inflation bound with an outlier test on the archive and wrote *the two arguments leave no gap* — CHAINING TWO DIFFERENT `sigma`s.** The outlier test's is the per-REPLICA scatter; the inflation formula's is the per-SCAN-MEMBER scatter of the block sum; **they differ by the leverage of one replica on that sum (~1/100 of `C_stat`'s share of a `C_syst`-dominated total), and that ratio is not a detail — it is the entire conversion.** So the check is TWO steps, displacement then leverage, both **reads of products that already exist at zero new compute**, with family-wise thresholds (`|z| > 3.48` over 100 replicas, `3.60` over 160 throws) stated against the expected clean maximum (`2.58` / `2.73`) **so the test cannot fire on ordinary scatter.** **AMENDED ON LANE A'S SECOND, AND ONE OF THE THREE AMENDMENTS IS A CORRECTION TO MY OWN TABLE.** *(i)* The direction TRANSFERS to `f_med` — a median over 285 bins — **but NOT for the `c4` reason, and the difference is load-bearing: `c4` survives a median because it is UNIFORM, this survives because it is UNIVERSAL.** A median is robust to a MINORITY of contaminated arguments, and here the displaced member is displaced in EVERY bin at once because it is one run, **so there is no clean majority to fall back on** — quoting `c4`'s reasoning would have reached the right answer by the wrong route. *(ii)* **MY FOUR FIGURES ARE THE CONSTANT-DISPLACEMENT CASE AND ARE NOT UPPER BOUNDS ON `f_med`** — every non-constant pattern inflates MORE (measured independently at `n=50`, 285 bins, 4000 trials: constant `0.99%`, uniform 0..2 `1.32%`, half-0/half-2 `1.95%`, lognormal `1.23%`, **script tracked as `docs/orchestration/mii_anchor_confound_mc.py` so the table is falsifiable**), `E|d_b| = m`, Jensen gives `E[d_b^2] >= m^2` with equality IFF `d_b` is CONSTANT, so the constant case MINIMISES the inflation over the whole family.** A floor by proof rather than by sampling. *(And the measured/predicted ratio drifts `1.000 -> 0.932` as `E[d^2]` grows, so leg A's exact formula is a CONSERVATIVE proxy for leg B.)* **AND TWO OF A'S FOUR MC FIGURES DID NOT REPRODUCE, reported `0.007%`, while constant and half-half match exactly — **and the `E[d^2]` law says `1.46%` is UNREACHABLE for `uniform(0,2)`, whose `E[d^2] = 4/3` caps it at `1.32%`**, so the setups differ (most likely a varying `sigma_b` against an absolute `d_b`, or `lognormal(0,0.5)` whose mean is `1.133`). **RESOLVED THE SAME DAY, AND MY OWN BENIGN HYPOTHESIS WAS THE WRONG ONE:** A ran exactly my model (`sigma_b = 1` every bin), so the varying-`sigma_b` guess is closed. **A drew ONE `d` array per pattern and held it fixed; my script REDRAWS it every trial and averages — so A reports a single realisation and I report the expectation over realisations.** A's `uniform(0,2)` array realised `E[d^2] = 1.5015` against a population `4/3`, which is a **`+2.38 sigma`** draw for 285 samples (`sd = 0.0706`, verified) and gives `1.49%` against A's measured `1.46%`; its half-half realised `2.0070` and therefore agreed. **Neither run has a defect, and the diagnostic A offered — *the pattern that order and it would have cost one glance.** **AND TWO OF MY OWN CLAIMS FROM THE FIRST AMENDMENT ARE NOW CORRECTED, BOTH BY THE SAME MECHANISM.** *(a)* **MY JENSEN FLOOR OVER-CLAIMED.** It is EXACT for mechanism's hypothesis on the way.** *(It applies to this campaign's case for a PHYSICAL reason and not a mathematical one: one perturbed replica moves the whole unfolded spectrum, not one cell — a theorem plus a physical fact, and the second is the part a later lane forgets.)* *(b)* **MY *"the ratio drifts as `E[d^2]` grows"* WAS A TWO-AXIS CONFOUND.** Holding `E[d^2]` fixed at `1.65` and varying only a lognormal's `sigma`, the pred/meas ratio runs `0.993 -> 0.954 -> 0.793 -> 0.499 -> 0.274` for `sigma = 0.3 -> 1.6` — **a span of `0.719` at CONSTANT second moment, so the ratio is set A's mechanism is the foundation; my theorem is the conditional refinement on top of it.** **AND A'S GENERAL FORM OF THE ASYMMETRY, ADOPTED VERBATIM BECAUSE IT IS BETTER THAN MY TWO-CASE ONE: *a bias is DECLARABLE when its sign is certain and points toward the stricter verdict; it must be CORRECTED when its sign is certain and points toward the lenient one; and when its sign is UNCERTAIN it must be BOUNDED before any verdict is read.*** Both of my cases need the sign CERTAIN, not estimated; **the third case does not arise here and naming it is what makes the first two a rule rather than a pair of decisions.** **ONE CAVEAT I OWED AND ADD: the sign is certain IN EXPECTATION, not per realisation** — measured `P(confound pushes f_med DOWN) = 0.0025` with a worst excursion of `-0.21%`, against the `16.8%` headroom leg B's `MET` threshold has at `n=50`, **so a sub-1% excursion cannot cross it and the caveat is subsumed by the bound already required.** [full](DETERMINATION-20260818-lanec-anchor-confound-is-declarable-by-direction.md) *Lane C (PET), as `(B)`'s owner. regions 2-4 needed `assertGreater(touched, 0, ...)`, or an excision matching nothing passes on an unmodified source. **RECONSTRUCTION FIDELITY, this lane's: a pre-fix reconstruction is defined by what it KEEPS** — the `write_ew_outputs(...)` call must SURVIVE, or you have built *a module that writes nothing* rather than *the pre-fix module*, and the assertions pass for the wrong reason a third time in one test. **MY OWN MISTAKE, one layer down: extending the excision I DELETED a line carrying a propagated argument inside a multi-line call, leaving an unclosed paren — and EVERY token-absence assertion PASSES on mangled source.** Caught by D's `ast.parse` arm, written without a concrete case, whose docstring says exactly this; blanking every matching LINE then orphaned an f-string's continuations and an `if` body (`IndentationError`), so the excision is now by STATEMENT with AST boundaries and **the ordering is load-bearing** — statements first, then substitute the occurrence inside an expression. **Two catches, two manglings, one guard.** **THE FAMILY, and the row's reason: FIVE INSTRUMENTS IN ONE EVENING THAT EXECUTED SUCCESSFULLY AND WERE BLIND** — a substring-absence check over a corpus missing its members (`BEN-235`); the static cause-6 test that stayed green with both `print`s deleted (`BEN-450`); the concordance's RENDER printing 3 of 6 citers as basenames (**`BEN-026` relocated to the output layer, and HARDER than the original because the evidence survives, so nobody re-runs**); a marker-based power test; and a citation check comparing COUNTS — **`103 → 103`, a delta of exactly ZERO while 38 of 50 cited lines rotted and 10 went extinct** (`26e4e343` → `3dd5e66e`). **D's MECHANISM, adopted as the cause: each was CORRECT ABOUT THE THING IT WAS LOOKING AT AND SILENT ABOUT WHETHER IT WAS LOOKING. *Is this assertion right?* and *does this assertion bind?* are different questions, and nothing in the act of writing a correct assertion prompts the second — so reviewing any of the five for CORRECTNESS would have cleared every one, and a MUTATION is the only operation that asks directly.** **D's pairing: `BEN-450` is detection without propagation, a count check is propagation without detection; Same defect, same rate — an argument for the exchange, not for either party being more careful. | BEN-248 | **CORROBORATION AND ECHO HAVE OPPOSITE EVIDENTIAL VALUE, SO A RELAY THAT OMITS THE ROUTE DESTROYS THE DIFFERENCE RATHER THAN DEGRADING IT — `BEN-312` running in reverse, and the reverse direction is the ORIGINAL row's own iatrogenic side effect.** `BEN-312` is three parties deriving one number from one source, where **agreement read as corroboration** (inflation). **This is the same mechanism inverted: genuine independent corroboration reading as an echo** (deflation). **Instance:** a peer lane independently derived this lane's gate-1 conclusion — that `bootstrap_nd.py:28-29` already routes `--seed` to the estimator under `--fixed-data-seed`, that `seedscan_split.py:36` exposes `--estimator-seed`, and that gate 1 is two modules rather than four legs — **reading the code, not this lane's document.** It was then **relayed to me as new information**, framed as *"smaller than your own report said."* I flagged it back as my own conclusion returning to me: **correct on the facts I had and wrong on the merits.** The mediator self-reported the transport error. **The fact was corroboration; the framing made it novelty; those have opposite value and the relay collapsed them.** **WHY THIS EARNS A ROW RATHER THAN AN ANNOTATION ON `BEN-312`, and the reason is not the instance's cost:** a row that records only the **inflation** direction teaches a reader to **discount agreement**, which makes the **deflation** failure MORE likely. **The two directions are not two instances of one lesson — they are a lesson and its own side effect**, so the remedy for one is not a widening of the other (distinct from `BEN-386`'s category error, where no widening helps at all). *That framing is lane A's and is better than the cost argument I opened with.* **There was no reading of the relay that preserved the fact, because the relay did not contain it** — I nearly discarded a second derivation of my own load-bearing finding, and accepting the framing instead would have recorded a peer's independent confirmation as my own restatement: the same loss by the opposite route. **THE CHECK IS ONE CLAUSE AND NEEDS NO TOOLING: when relaying a claim, say where it came from.** It cannot fire on a healthy case, which is `BEN-390`'s criterion for a rule worth writing rather than linting. **COMPOSES WITH `BEN-247`'s STOPPING CONDITION:** *"I got rows"* satisfies a search, *"a peer told me X"* satisfies a belief — in both, the signal arrives without the dimension that would let you evaluate it. **Lane A's addition, and it makes this the worse of the two: a search you can re-run; a relay you cannot, because the route is gone once the message is sent.** **SECOND INSTANCE, SAME DAY, SAME CHANNEL, OPPOSITE DIRECTION:** the same relay later carried a **true** capability claim without the **specification** it depended on — and when lane C conceded spec (B) (`M(ii)` is a *joint* measurement), the capability stopped helping and `sweep_bank_5d.py:252` became the blocking dependency rather than one of two parallel edits. **So the route matters for a claim's DEPENDENCIES as well as its ORIGIN.** **THIRD INSTANCE, in the attribution itself:** the crediting name *"Assistant"* has been used for at least two distinct live sessions, and **lane A had to DECLINE a credit** to prevent a misattribution in the flattering direction (`BEN-214`) — *"the only mechanism available is the party declining it."* **THE GENERAL STATEMENT, which is this row's most portable claim and is a better statement of `BEN-077` than `BEN-077` makes: SHIP WHAT WOULD LET A READER FALSIFY THIS.** The receipt-ingredients principle has now been useful in **four registers in one day** — operands of a **number** (`BEN-077`), scope of a **grade** (`VL66`'s named falsifier), units of a **ratio** (`BEN-152`'s extension), and provenance of a **claim** (this row) — **so the rule was never about numbers.** *Filed in this lane's own block at lane A's insistence and on its reasoning: A was not party to the relay, cannot verify it, and filing it would instantiate the very defect it describes. **a partial success is a worse signal than a clean failure, and that is why the leg with a live-looking id is the one to re-count.** **A CORRECTION TO THE CORRECTION, same principle as the one that saved 99 A100-h:** the mediator proposed `14.36` = 19 x the 14-task mean; **no scaling is needed because all 19 universes have a measured realized elapsed** (5 in `55891346`, 14 in `55894759`, disjoint and complete precisely because of the resume skips) — `14.2075` is a measurement, `14.36` is a forecast, and they agree to `1.1 %`. `55891346`'s `1.6981` A100-h of FAILED/CANCELLED work is excluded from **both** totals: a re-seed does not reproduce another run's failures. **The conclusion did not move — `28.50` and `39.078` both exceed the `24` A100-h grant, so the same request goes to Joseph — but a MEASURED number quoted to him was understated by 38 %.** **MECHANISM RE-LOCATED BY LANE A, and A is right — the operative variable is NOT the job state.** I framed it as `CANCELLED`-vs-partially-`COMPLETED`; **the general form is whether the signal SATISFIES THE STOPPING CONDITION.** A `CANCELLED` id returns no rows so the search continues; a partial id returns rows so the search stops — **and it stops because it succeeded, not because it was complete.** Job state is one instantiation. **A's is the same mechanism a layer down and in a different instrument:** `agy` exits `0` and emits 303 bytes of fluent prose, and **both** the exit-code check **and** the non-emptiness check are satisfied by a dispatch that did nothing. So: **A PARTIAL SUCCESS IS THE WORST SIGNAL BECAUSE IT SATISFIES THE STOPPING CONDITION WITHOUT SATISFYING THE QUESTION.** **The remedy survives the generalisation unchanged**, which is a point in its favour — *cross the returned set against the design's own member count* is precisely *"do not let the stopping condition be 'I got rows'"*. A also notes the self-diagnosis has **a within-document control** and I had under-claimed it: same author, same day, same instrument, two legs, and the by-name scan plus the 169-against-169 count went to exactly the leg that returned nothing — **the technique's presence two sections away rules out "didn't know to do it,"** which is the explanation that would otherwise absorb the finding. Observed: `P0` control (`reco_scalars` x1.01) **CHANGED** as predeclared, so the probe had power; `P1` (`truth_scalars` x1.05), `P2` (`truth_scalars` rows permuted) and `P3` (`part_gen` x1.05) each left `event_reco` **BIT-IDENTICAL** to baseline `8c88e159…`, with the proxy confirming each perturbation landed. **That is a clean NEGATIVE result.** Only `P4` (`w_truth` x1.05) failed, and it failed by **not running** (`arrays_actually_changed: {}`, `proxy_hits: 0`). **Cause, one token:** the arm flag is tri-state — `True` ran-and-matched, `False` ran-and-**CONTRADICTED** (the only value that may produce `LEAKAGE`), `None` did-not-run-**exclude** — and `:219` at `f6a52ed` assigned a **VOID** arm `False`. `False is not None`, so the filter at `:232` admitted an arm that never ran, forced `clean` False, and the verdict fell through to the `LEAKAGE` else-branch. **The file's own docstring `:41-44` already stated the intended semantics** (*"a perturbation that did not perturb turns 'no leakage' into 'no test'"*) — docstring right, code wrong. Fixed to `None` at `:224` (`143f859`); replay of the recorded arms yields **`NO TRUTH LEAKAGE DEMONSTRATED on 3 of 4 truth perturbations`**. **Caught ONLY because the receipt shipped its ingredients (`BEN-077`, second such catch):** the headline is unfalsifiable alone — a `LEAKAGE` verdict on a leakage probe is what a real leak looks like — and the per-arm `sha256`/`arrays_actually_changed`/`proxy_hits` beside it made the contradiction **arithmetic rather than interpretive**. **Direction: it failed ALARMING, strictly safer than failing quiet** (a void arm can never make a dirty run look clean, since `clean` is an `all()` over a falsy injection) **and still not free** — truth leakage is the campaign's most load-bearing purity property, so a false `LEAKAGE` competes for exactly the attention a real blocker would need. **Transferable check: enumerate a verdict-flag's states and name the ones meaning "no measurement"; a tri-state in a boolean is a defect awaiting its third state, and that state is the one no normal run reaches, so a test must supply it.** Regression written FIRST and observed **3/6 RED** at `f6a52ed`, **6/6 GREEN** after; it pins BOTH directions (a genuinely `CHANGED` truth arm must still produce `LEAKAGE`) and parses its arms out of the preserved stdout, so the headline test is a **replay** of `56975592`, not a re-enactment. **`P4`'s void arm is `OI-124` and the offered hypothesis is REFUTED:** the loader **does** read the NPZ's raw `w_truth` (`:1251`) and derives the trainer's weights from it (`:1323`/`:1332`→`:1349`), but `event_reco` is assigned at `:1241` — ten lines earlier — so `P4` is **structurally unfalsifiable at that capture point**, not accidentally void. | BEN-300 | **THE HOLDER OF A TASK IS A HAND-MAINTAINED FACT WITH NO MACHINE-DERIVABLE SOURCE.** Leg 0 was dispatched twice by the mediator — to a one-shot `claude -p` lane (which produced `692c6bd`) and, while that was still running, to lane A, which had **already been holding Leg 0** from an earlier dispatch. A independently re-implemented the same `--checkpoint-tier` flag, wrote its own regression test, and rediscovered the three-launcher pin problem, then dropped all of it (`git reset --hard origin/main`) when `692c6bd` landed first — **~40 min of a lane's time, zero of the repo's.** **The mediator's first framing — *"I failed to check who held the task"* — is useless, because there is nothing to check:** no file in this repo records who currently holds a task (`LIVE-STATE.md` is generated over a hand-authored input, `OPEN_ITEMS.md` tracks items not holders), and `ListAgents` reports which sessions are **alive**, never what they are **doing**. A rule nobody can comply with is not a rule. **This is `BEN-228` one level up: not a stale index OF DOCUMENTS but a stale index OF WHO IS DOING WHAT** — and unlike every `BEN-228` instance (line numbers, the free-list, `MANIFEST`'s producer claim, a digest in prose), where the fact was machine-derivable and somebody wrote it down instead, **here there is no source to derive from**, so *derive, do not narrate* has no purchase. **Enabling conditions, measured:** lanes are killed and respawned without notice (twice tonight, four at a time) and **keep their names**, so `ListAgents` showing `A - ORCHESTRATOR` says nothing about whether it is the A that accepted the task; one-shot `claude -p` lanes do not appear in `ListAgents` at all; and a dispatch is a **message**, so the only record of the holding lived in the holder's own context — exactly what a kill destroys. **Two closures, neither costed:** a task-holder file written at dispatch and cleared at report (making `BEN-228`'s remedy apply), **or accept the duplication as cheap** — it bought an *independent re-derivation* of `692c6bd`'s two central design decisions, which is corroboration this campaign otherwise pays to manufacture. **Caused by the mediator, who reported it as its own failure; the correct diagnosis is lane A's**, supplied unprompted in the same message in which it reported losing the work. **GENERAL FORM, unifying `BEN-228` and `BEN-244`: consensus among restatements of a single source is not corroboration** — whether the restatements are citations, index cells, or two lanes each believing they hold a task. A nearly filed a duplicate `BEN-229` row because **two independent-looking statements agreed** (`FINDINGS.md`'s index cell and the finding file's own header, 200 lines apart) — one author, one moment of truth, count of 1. The stale Gate-4 blocker had eleven copies and three readers who each checked and found agreement. **Before treating agreement as evidence, ask whether the agreeing statements have SEPARATE ORIGINS.** Plus lane A's tell: *a claim that cannot be wrong at the next reading is usually a claim about the wrong object* — a mechanism says what you would see where you have not looked, a symptom only what you already saw. [Detail](FINDING-20260815-who-holds-a-task-has-no-source.md) **SECOND INSTANCE 2026-08-15/16, AND IT CARRIES A REMEDY THIS ROW DOES NOT HAVE.** The `OI-6` note-text task was dispatched to the propagation-correction lane **at least two hours after it had already been completed by another lane**: Joseph's ruling landed `361d83e` `16:51`, the text landed `e61624b` `18:52` (*"Place the standard-5D purity footing and its limit in the analysis note: app_negweight.tex B.6"*) and was refined `7d884da` `19:01`; the dispatch arrived after `c179a35`. **The receiving lane found it by reading the target file before writing, and wrote nothing** — had it started from the brief it would have written a second copy of an existing subsection into a file whose gate Joseph had opened for exactly one passage. **THE ADDITION: this row's thesis is that the HOLDER of a task has no machine-derivable source, and that is right — but WHETHER THE WORK IS ALREADY DONE frequently DOES.** Here one command over the target artifact (`git log -S '<the section heading>' -- <file>`) would have answered it before dispatch, and the answer was a commit with the task's own words in its subject. **So duplicate dispatch has a cheap check that duplicate ASSIGNMENT does not: before dispatching, ask the artifact whether the change is already in it.** That is `BEN-228`'s *derive-don't-narrate* applied to task state rather than to document state, and unlike a task-holder file it needs no registry and cannot go stale. **Filed as an annotation rather than a new `BEN` because the mechanism is identical to this row's** **THIRD INSTANCE 2026-08-16, AND IT MOVES THE FINDING FROM "NO SOURCE EXISTS" TO "THE CHANNEL LIES."** The mediator dispatched the `N3`+`N4` repair to the bare name `A` **twice**; `SendMessage` returned `{"success": true}` both times, warning only that it was disambiguating between two live sessions sharing that name. **Neither registered** — the lane later replied *"not holding, never touched `p4_lib.py`"* and offered to take the GBDT work **as though it were new**, while the mediator reported to Joseph for hours that *"A is busy, presumably on N3."* Measured throughout: `git log -1 -- nd-unfolding/p4_lib.py` → `5fc06b6`, `grep -c` on the defect line → `1`. **`{"success": true}` FROM A DISPATCH ESTABLISHES THAT THE CHANNEL ACCEPTED THE SEND — NOT DELIVERY, AND CERTAINLY NOT ACCEPTANCE OF THE TASK** (**CORRECTED 2026-08-16: this clause first read "ESTABLISHES DELIVERY, NOT ACCEPTANCE", which is INVERTED and contradicted its own evidence two clauses later — if the lane never knew it was asked, the message was not delivered. The inverted form licenses precisely the inference this row exists to forbid: a reader believing delivery is established concludes the lane received the brief and did not act, which is "A is busy, presumably on N3" rebuilt out of the correction. The correct form yields the only useful conclusion — YOU DO NOT KNOW IT ARRIVED, SO CONFIRM. Caught by lane C, which read the commit rather than the mediator's summary of it, and to which the sentence was miscredited in its inverse.**), and this row's thesis was previously that no source records the holder — this is worse, because **a source did answer and answered wrongly.** Joins the *tool reported success over failure* family and is its worst member so far: a no-op `git add` leaves an inspectable diff, whereas **a dispatch that reports delivery and vanishes leaves the sender believing a lane is working and the lane never knowing it was asked** — which is the same 40 minutes this row already cost, arriving by an independent route. **A SECOND, DISTINCT DEFECT FOUND IN THE SAME EPISODE: `ListAgents` IS NOT A SHARED VIEW.** The mediator's listing showed two rows named `A` (`[422728]`, `[404e02]`); the lane's own listing, one turn earlier, showed **one**. **So *"there is only one lane with this name"* is a claim about the observer.** That damages this row's own remedy space directly: **a task-holder registry different lanes read differently is worse than none**, because it yields confident disagreement instead of acknowledged ignorance. **Lane C's hypothesis — bare-name delivery fails silently when the name is ambiguous — is NOT SUPPORTED as stated and is recorded here so nobody adopts it:** other bare-`A` sends in the same session (a status request, an ack request) **did** register and were answered, with the same ambiguity present. The mechanism is therefore unexplained; what is measured is that delivery confirmation and task acceptance came apart at least twice. **The actionable half needs no mechanism: address by `name [ref]` when a name is ambiguous, and treat a dispatch as unaccepted until the lane says otherwise in its own words.** *Mediator; ambiguity fix and the delivery-vs-acceptance framing from lane C; the `ListAgents` divergence measured and reported by lane A.* — the instance is new, the finding is not. *Propagation-correction lane; caught by reading the target before writing.* | formulation and tell attributed to lane A | EP-2026-08-15-eavail |
| BEN-301 | **A HASH PIN AUTHENTICATES CONTENT AGAINST AN EXPECTATION STORED IN THE SAME TREE, SO IT IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THE TREE ITSELF BEING STALE — BOTH SIDES GO STALE TOGETHER AND AGREE PERFECTLY.** Found during the independent digest confirmation that was to be the **last step before launching** the authorized arm-1 resubmit. The `G0` wrapper pin is correct and well maintained — four documented moves in two days, `ee269b09 → b24cfefe → 0e1471ba → 7499814e → e284cdbc`, verified here with `hashlib` rather than the reporting lane's `sha256sum`, exactly one pin line changed and the receipt-bound DRIVER untouched (`BEN-270`). **The cluster holds wrapper `ee269b09` — the PRE-MOVE-1 file — with a pin literal of `ee269b09`.** Its `HEAD` `683bdcc` is an ancestor of local HEAD **663 commits back**, and `behind 487` its own `github/main`; 19 tracked files dirty or staged, 735 untracked. **`G0` would have PASSED and the run would have contained none of MOVE 2 (anneal attestation), MOVE 3 (the end-of-run recorder the run exists to carry) or MOVE 4 (`BEN-342`).** **THE ASYMMETRY: a pin is strong against "someone edited this file" and blind to "this checkout is 663 commits old", because the second failure moves the checked object and its expectation by the same amount.** `MOVE 1`'s own note shows the design was understood — `logs/ff_57038937_{3,4,5}.out` carry the digest those tasks ran — **but that is a FORENSIC property, not a gate: it tells you what ran once you go looking; it does not decline to run.** Compounding: `train_fullevent_nominal.py`, whose `:576-577` is *the definition* the new instrument was verified against, is itself modified on the cluster (9 insertions, 26 deletions) — the reduction survives, but the file the verification treated as fixed ground is not fixed there. **THE RULE: a digest pin cannot authenticate the tree. Before a launch, check the DEPLOYMENT'S REVISION against the revision you verified — a separate question no pin will ever answer.** Corollary, and the cheaper half: **`G0 PASS` in a log is not evidence that current code ran — read the printed digest against the digest you expect, not the verdict.** **No run was launched; escalated to Joseph** rather than resolved, the cluster fix being a potentially destructive action on shared dirty state under the standing "do not pull the cluster science repo" prohibition. | BEN-302 | **A RETRACTION REACHES ONLY AS FAR AS THE CORRECTOR'S MAP OF THE CORPUS, AND THE SITES IT MISSES ARE EXACTLY THE ONES THAT PROVE THE ERROR SPREAD.** Sequence in one hour: lane B finds `~105 draw-sd` does not follow from its stated operands (correct); **the mediator over-corrects**, writing that it *"derives from nothing"* and that the sd yielding it *"appears in no artifact"* — **without grepping for it** (`6e05985`); the Assistant lane recomputes `0.030252/2.889e-4 = 104.7` rather than accepting the retraction and finds the operand in the receipt under the self-labelling key `in_draw_sd_of_that_row`, so the figure was **MIS-NORMALISED, NOT FABRICATED**; the mediator retracts the retraction and **enumerates the fix list from memory** (`0b1d33a`, `1f6bafa`), naming two sites to lane B. **There were three** — the third being lane B's own `BEN-342` row, carrying the mediator's wrong claim, in a file the mediator had edited twice that hour (`f386aa0`). **THE MECHANISM IS NOT CARELESSNESS:** the corrector's model of where an error went is complete only for the sites the corrector WROTE, and an error's whole nature is being repeated by others. The `grep` WAS run at discovery — that is how eight sites were counted — and was **not re-run one turn later at retraction, when it mattered.** **THE RULE: a correction is bounded by the corrector's map; `git grep` is the only thing that isn't. Re-run the search at RETRACTION time, and again after landing, because the corpus moved while you were correcting it.** **ADJACENT RULE, lane B's wording and better than the mediator's: A NULL GREP IS EVIDENCE ABOUT THE SEARCH, NOT ABOUT THE WORLD** — three instances this session in three lanes (mediator: *"appears in no artifact"*; lane B: *"stated nowhere"*, having grepped the wrapper, test and predeclaration but **not the receipt**; Assistant: two files reported ABSENT ON BOTH TREES from **guessed paths**, caught only by `git ls-files`). **All three caught by somebody searching a second time; none by a rule; two would have shipped a false absence into a committed document. The frequency is the finding.** **NOT `BEN-228`:** there the fact was derivable and somebody narrated it; here the values WERE re-derived correctly at every step and what was carried from memory was **the derivation's SCOPE** — a rule about deriving values does not reach a claim about coverage. | BEN-303 | **A CONSTRAINT BLOCK IS A STATUS REPORT AND GOES STALE LIKE ANY OTHER; A JOB ID IN A RECEIPT IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A LABEL.** For ~5 hours the mediator closed every dispatch with *"no scancel/scontrol — allocation `57128458` is held and idle, leave it up"*, to four lanes, repeatedly. Measured: `sacct -j 57128458` → **`TIMEOUT 03:00:03`, ended `2026-08-16T18:37:31`**; `squeue` → a **different** allocation `57142574` running since `20:04:51`. Lane B's `hRowIndex4D` readback, whose receipt named `57128458`, in fact ran in `57142574.1` — proved from step records (`.0` 29 s probe, `.1` 12 s readback, `.2` 9 s key listing, `.3` 14 s extension, in issue order). **No measured value was affected — the products were read, not the queue; the defect is ATTRIBUTION.** `BEN-027` requires every ID, count and queue name to come from a command run in the same turn, **and the mediator wrote that rule into a dozen footers on the same night without applying it to the one fact those footers were asserting.** **THE MECHANISM: boilerplate looks like policy — stable, restated — and policy is the one thing readers do not re-derive, so a measurement embedded in it inherits the boilerplate's credibility and none of its own freshness.** **WHY NOBODY CAUGHT IT: the tooling was good.** `alloc_run.sh` auto-requests a fresh allocation when the 3-hour one expires (`AGENTS.md`), so every dispatch succeeded and nothing failed in a way that would prompt a re-check — **a stale id survives precisely when the tooling is good**, whereas an expired allocation that BROKE a run would have been caught in seconds. Same family as the sweep's `|| true` false positive and `BEN-345`'s colour assertion: **the failure mode is the absence of a symptom, not the presence of one.** **THE RULE: before restating a constraint containing an ID, count or queue name, re-derive it; if that is too expensive to do every time, it does not belong in boilerplate — put a pointer to the command there instead of the answer.** Division recorded as the lanes settled it: the mediator claimed the larger share for amplifying it; **lane B declined that and is right** — B put the false id in a **receipt**, the durable artifact, while footers are transient; the new half is the mediator's, that **footers carry measurements at all**. | BEN-324 | **AN OWNER RECORDED IN AN ARTIFACT IS NOT AN OWNER WHO CAN BE ASKED.** `gate5_cstat_contract.json` names its owning lane in its own `lane` field; when a ruling was needed on whether a reduced-`n` diagnostic is coherent under it, **that name had no addressee** — `minerva-omnifold-f7` was asked twice, directly, and did not answer, having been told that "the owner is not reachable" is an actionable answer where silence is not. **`lane` is PROVENANCE, NOT A ROUTING TABLE:** `ListAgents` reports which sessions are alive, never what they own; sessions are **renamed and respawned keeping their names** (this lane was `minerva-omnifold-8e` and is now `A`, mid-task; two sessions claimed `minerva-omnifold-8e` inside a minute from different sockets); and a lane label is not an address — **the mediator named a DIFFERENT peer `C` and treated it as the spec owner, and that peer refused, correctly**, which is `BEN-080`'s two-referents-one-label shape in the lane namespace. **`BEN-300` one level out, and worse in one specific way: a task can be re-dispatched to whoever is available; A SPEC RULING CANNOT — authority to interpret does not transfer by availability**, so it escalates to Joseph or it stalls, and the unreachable-owner failure is not recoverable by the mechanism that recovers an unreachable worker. **NOT ONE ITEM, which is why it is filed: TWO critical-path items are blocked on unreachable owners simultaneously** — `OI-81` (lane C's script, the safety argument under a promoted nominal) and `OI-58`/`OI-57` (needs whoever owns the Gate-6 Leg 0 launcher and its receipt, never identified). **The decision that surfaced it was reached correctly WITHOUT the ruling, and noticing that is the transferable move: a decision NOT to run needs no spec interpretation.** **RULE: before a decision depends on a named owner's ruling, establish the owner is REACHABLE; if not, say so and escalate — unreachability is a finding, silence is not.** Corollaries: ask for the ruling before it is a blocker; **record unreachability as a STATE** ("asked twice 2026-08-15, no answer" is worth more in a row than the owner's name); | BEN-370 | **TESTING THE GATE DISABLED THE GATE, FOR EVERY LANE, SILENTLY — and within the hour of adding a check whose whole subject is a guard reading green on the case it does not cover.** To prove pre-commit check 8 blocks a real commit I ran `git config core.hooksPath <throwaway worktree>/.githooks` **INSIDE A LINKED WORKTREE**, then removed the worktree. **`extensions.worktreeConfig` is not enabled here, so a worktree's config write lands in the SHARED `.git/config`** — measured, `git config --show-origin` → `file:.git/config`, pointing at a path `ls` reports absent. **Git skips a missing `core.hooksPath` with NO diagnostic**, so a commit running zero checks is indistinguishable from one running all nine except for one line of output. **THREE GENERAL PROPERTIES had to hold for the invisibility, and all three are reusable: (i) a worktree's config write is NOT scoped to the worktree — `CONVENTION-lane-worktrees.md` establishes isolation for the index and the working tree and it does NOT extend to settings, a third exception to "worktrees isolate WRITES, not ATTENTION" that nobody had named; (ii) a dangling hooks path FAILS OPEN, and uniquely cannot be made to fail closed, because the thing that would refuse is the thing that is missing; (iii) the evidence a hook ran is the PRESENCE of a line, and absence does not look like failure** — `BEN-344`'s rule turned on the hook itself, and **I caught it only because I happened to be reading commit output for that line for an unrelated reason.** **BLAST RADIUS MEASURED, and one commit was not mine: `388abd8` (01:36, another lane's PET critical-path map) and `b7b7c0c` (01:44, mine) each ran ZERO of 9 checks.** All nine then run directly against `HEAD`: **every one `rc=0`**, including `verify_hash_bindings.py`, the freeze gate whose silent absence would matter most — **so nothing broken landed, but that is a post-hoc verification and NOT the gate having run**, and the other lane was told rather than left to assume its commit was checked. **FIX: `git config core.hooksPath .githooks` restored; the habit is `git -c key=value <cmd>` for a one-shot, which cannot leak because it never touches a config file — shorter than the command that caused this.** **The committed tooling was NOT the fault and that was verified rather than assumed:** `shared_push.sh` drives its worktrees with `git -C` and runs no `git config`; the e2e suite's `git config user.email` calls are in independent `git init` clones. **NOT FIXED, deliberately: there is still no signal that the hook is LIVE, and the natural check cannot live in the hook — a disabled hook does not run to complain.** It needs a lane-invoked self-test or a workflow line; routed to the mediator rather than patched, because the lane that broke a shared mechanism is the wrong one to design its tamper-detection unprompted. | BEN-329 | **`0.0 * nan` IS `nan`, SO A SPARSE PROJECTION DOES NOT LOCALISE A NON-FINITE BIN -- ONE BAD INPUT BIN POISONS EVERY OUTPUT BIN.** Found while writing `N4`'s test, by an expectation of mine that was WRONG and a code path that was right: I asserted that one `nan` in `x_high` would leave 1 of 4 cross-check bins non-finite, and measured **4 of 4**. The mechanism is that a ZERO entry of `M` does not isolate anything -- `0.0 * nan` is `nan` and `1.0 + nan` is `nan` -- so the marginal `M @ x_high` is non-finite in **every row that has any column at all**, which for a coverage-complete `M` is all of them. **Scale on the real products: one bad reported 5D bin of 10,694 takes all 4,825 reported 4D bins with it, and the cross-check's `median`/`p90`/`p99`/`max` all become `nan` while `n_over_3pct` reports `0`, because `nan > 0.03` is False.** A summary block that reads as "no bins over tolerance" is the WORST available rendering of "the input was unusable", and it is what this function printed and wrote to the receipt for nine days. **The transferable part is the asymmetry: a non-finite value propagates through a sparse linear map as if the map were dense, so sparsity is NOT a containment argument for `nan`** -- if you reason "only the affected block can be hit", you are wrong by the size of the matrix. **And the second-order trap, which is why the counts are reported and not just a boolean flag: when EVERYTHING is non-finite the finite-only summaries are `nan` too and the finite-only counts are `0`**, i.e. the fallback is indistinguishable from a clean result unless the excluded count is printed beside it. Both regimes are now pinned in one test (`test_crosscheck_reports_nonfinite_and_still_never_raises`), localised and amplified, so neither can be mistaken for the other. **`REPORT ONLY` is unchanged and deliberately so** -- the guard reports, it does not gate, because gating here would need a decision the function is specified not to make. [full](FINDING-20260816-zero-times-nan-defeats-sparsity.md) *Lane A.* | `BEN-323`, `BEN-327` | EP-2026-08-16-n3-n4-repair |
| BEN-328 | **A RECOMPUTATION IDENTITY CANNOT VALIDATE ITS OWN PREMISE, AND THE INDEPENDENT ROUTE PRESCRIBED TO FIX `N3` WOULD HAVE FAILED THE BAR `N3` WAS WRITTEN FOR.** The brief prescribed the honest form of `check_projection_validity`'s second leg as *"derive the index groups from `M`'s structure and accumulate `C_low[a,b] = sum sum C_high[i,j]` with no matrix multiplication in that path"*, against a predeclared bar (`B1`, `bf97279`) requiring *"a test that fails on the pre-fix form **and on a wrong `M`**"*. **Those two are incompatible, measured before implementing: a block sum whose groups and weights are read off `M` computes `M C M^T` BY DEFINITION for any `M` with one nonzero per column, so all four corruptions tried -- a row scaled by 3, one weight scaled by 3, a column moved to the wrong row, two rows swapped -- came back at `1.5e-16 .. 5.0e-16`, INVISIBLE, exactly like the re-associated form it replaces.** The prescription fixes the docstring's honesty and the identity's meaning; it does not and cannot reach the bar. **THE REASON GENERALISES PAST THIS FUNCTION: "wrong" is not a property of `M`, it is a relation between `M` and the recipe that produced it, ~~so no function of `(C_high, M)` can decide it~~ -- CORRECTED 2026-08-16, see below: that last clause is FALSE AS STATED and the RELABELING class is the part that survives.** Both legs sharing `M` is not an implementation weakness to be engineered around -- it is the premise, and a premise is not checkable from inside the computation that assumes it, **for the class where that holds.** **CORRECTION, from the independent second read on `N3`/`N4` (which HELD the remedy and refuted the ARGUMENT for it), reproduced by this lane before accepting it: a function of `M` ALONE catches 3 of the 4 corruptions I chose.** Invariant: (a) exactly one nonzero per column, (b) all nonzeros positive, (c) every row carries the same multiset of nonzero values -- which follows from my own construction, since `M[row,col] = wdrop[k]` depends on the DROPPED INDEX and never on the row. Measured: row scaled by 3, one weight scaled by 3, and one column moved to the wrong row are all **CAUGHT** with no recipe; **two rows swapped is a pure relabeling (verified: the swapped matrix is the same multiset of rows), every structural invariant survives it, and it provably needs the recipe** -- so the recipe gate is necessary regardless and the remedy stands unchanged. **WHY THE OVERREACH MATTERED, and it is the same shape as the docstring it fixed: as filed, this row licensed a future lane to skip a cheap structural check on the grounds that checking is impossible.** **AND THE OPERATIONAL SHARPENING, which the second read explicitly left open and this lane then measured: the invariant's ENTIRE discriminating power is clause (c), the coverage-conditional one.** (a)+(b) alone catch **none** of the four. Clause (c) dissolves as drop-axis coverage falls -- distinct row multisets `1` at full coverage, `4` at 10% of high bins dropped, `6` at 30%, `7` at 50% -- and on the PRODUCTION configuration it cannot hold at all: 10,694 reported 5D bins over 4,825 reported 4D bins is a **mean W multiplicity of 2.216 of 6**, so almost no row is coverage-complete. **NOT measured on the real masks** (they need ROOT products unreadable from this checkout) -- this is an implication of committed counts, stated as such. **So the correct reading is: the impossibility claim is false in general, and on THIS configuration the recipe gate is doing all of the work anyway.** **So the repair is TWO gates, not a better version of one:** `_block_sum_projection` (numpy reductions, no matmul -- genuinely independent OF `project()`'s expression, catches a doubled result at rel `5.0e-1`, and **measured 1.00 s vs 6.62 s for the BLAS product at the real 10694->4825 shape, because `M` holds one nonzero per column, so the honest route is also the cheaper one**), plus `check_projection_matrix_matches_recipe`, which rebuilds `M` from `(edges, drop_axis, mask_high, mask_low)` by a deliberately different algorithm (`unravel_index`/`ravel_multi_index`/`searchsorted` rather than a `//`-and-`%` loop with a dict) and requires EXACT equality -- catching the probe's corruption at `max\|diff\| 3.0` where every identity route reports `~1e-16`. **Exactness is available and used, because a tolerance on that comparison would be a number nobody can justify.** **The identity gate's blindness is now ASSERTED IN A TEST AND REPORTED IN THE RECEIPT (`projection_identity_gates_M: False`) rather than merely documented**, so the next reader cannot infer from a green identity that the map was checked -- and the docstring contradiction that made `repair-10` quote the promise as the contract is resolved by SCOPING the promise, not deleting it. **Before/after is one command, not a narrative:** the pre-repair `p4_lib.py` (`sha256 aa3470e4…`, matching the baseline lane D recorded at `3fe11de` before the repair could overwrite it) exits **2** with *"PRE-REPAIR TREE -- the defect is live and `M` is ungated"* and reproduces the `3.033e-17` corrupted-`M` pass; the repaired tree exits **0**. **`BEN-316`'s sections 1-4 are EXPECTED to keep passing after the repair and do** -- section 4 is *"a corrupted `M` passes the identity leg"*, which is still true by construction and is the point -- so the probe now keeps its two expectation sets in separate buckets, because one exit code meaning both *"`BEN-316` no longer reproduces"* and *"the repair has not landed"* would license opposite conclusions. | BEN-321 | **A CONTROL-PLANE BLOCKER LIST VETOED WORK ITS OWN OWNER HAD AUTHORIZED, AND THE DOCUMENTED FIX FOR IT IS A SILENT NO-OP.** `live-state.json` had **3 of 4 `blockers` false plus `state` and `next_authorized_action` false** — five fields — for two days, rendered into `LIVE-STATE.md`, the file `CLAUDE.md` sends every session to FIRST. The costly one: `blockers[2]` paraphrased `do_not_retry_unchanged` as *"no retry"*, **dropping the qualifier and thereby forbidding the CHANGED Gate-6 retry Joseph authorized at `043d572`** — while `next_authorized_action` eleven lines below stated it correctly, which is the only reason it was findable without an external reference. **MECHANISM (the reusable part): the file is a HAND-AUTHORED INPUT that `MANIFEST.tsv` labels `event_status: generated`, `consumer: <a script that only reads it>`, `immutable: yes`.** It therefore *looks machine-owned*, so no lane edits it and it rots — invisibly, because the renderer refreshes `Observed:`/`Git:` around unchanged prose. **The generator admits this in its own freshness output** (*"regeneration … does NOT revalidate `Declared state`, which is authored prose the generator carries forward"*): **a freshness indicator that certifies only the parts nobody doubted vouches for the stale ones.** **AND THE PRESCRIBED REMEDY CANNOT WORK, MEASURED NOT READ:** `generate_manifest.py` applies the override, then `if is_runs_or_state(rel):` runs **unconditionally afterwards** and resets `event_status = "generated"` — so `MANIFEST-overrides.tsv` is **inert for every `state/` and `RUNS` path**. **Worse, the clobbered override is still added to `applied_overrides`, so it counts in `overrides=N` and is EXCLUDED from the "unused overrides" warning: the one diagnostic built to catch a dead override is blind to this case by construction, because the entry is not unused but used-then-discarded.** Appending the override yielded `overrides=49` and `event_status=generated`, exit 0. `immutable` is unreachable too (`derive_immutable` returns `yes` for `is_runs_or_state` and the overrides file has no such column). **So cite prohibitions BY KEY, not by paraphrase.** `do_not_retry_unchanged` carries `unchanged` *inside the identifier*, so any faithful copy keeps it and the claim becomes a set comparison against `prohibitions_applied`. | BEN-320 | **`BEN-228`'S REMEDY IS UNAVAILABLE WHEN THE CITING ARTIFACT IS IMMUTABLE, AND THE ONLY REMAINING MOVE IS TO PRESERVE THE COORDINATE.** `BEN-228`'s rule is *re-derive every cited line after the last edit, search for content not coordinates* — which assumes **the citation is yours to update.** Correcting the mediator's overstatement in `sbatch_p5a_fullevent_nominal_extract.sh` required editing a header comment **above** guard `G1`, and `G1` is cited as `sbatch_p5a_fullevent_nominal_extract.sh:153-182` in **two committed receipts** (`state/RECEIPT-vl100-shape-corrected-foldforward-20260815.json:301`, `state/probe-vl100-shape-correction-scan-20260815.py:249`) **and in `BEN-311`, another lane's row.** A receipt records what its run asserted, so rewriting one destroys the record; editing `BEN-311` is the overreach `BEN-159`/`BEN-223` both refused. **So all three of `BEN-228`'s options were closed: cannot re-derive (not my artifact), cannot re-address (already emitted), cannot leave it stale (a `G1` citation resolving to a comment is how `BEN-215`/`BEN-216` read).** **What is left is the fourth option `BEN-228` does not name: make the edit LINE-COUNT-NEUTRAL and verify the neutrality**, which converts the coordinate from a stale index back into a true one. Measured here: `git diff --numstat` → `13 13`, then `sed -n '153,182p'` re-read to confirm `G1` still occupies the range. **The generalisation is a rule about WHERE you may spend lines:** in a file whose line ranges are cited by anything you cannot edit, **an edit's line count is part of its contract**, and the free-to-restructure region is only the part below the last cited range — here everything after `:182`, which is where the substantive rewrite went. **Cheap executable form, not written:** a pre-commit check that greps emitted receipts for `<tracked-file>:<a>-<b>` citations and fails when a commit changes the line count above `a`. **Related asymmetry worth stating: `.sh` launchers carrying embedded `python3 - <<'PY'` heredocs are unchecked by `bash -n`, which validates the shell and treats the heredoc as an opaque string** — the Python inside can be syntactically broken and `bash -n` still exits 0, at a cost of the whole GPU allocation at receipt-writing time, i.e. after the expensive work. Verified here by extracting all three `PY` blocks and `compile()`-ing each, then **executing** the receipt writer against stub arguments to confirm it emits parseable JSON. **Do that before committing an edit to a heredoc; the shell check cannot see it.** [full](FINDING-20260815-a-citation-you-cannot-re-derive.md) *Propagation-correction lane.* | `OI-71`, `CLM-012` | EP-2026-08-15-vl100-propagation-correction |
| BEN-330 | **AN ARM WHOSE PREDECLARED OUTCOME IS ENTAILED BY STATEMENT ORDER IS NOT A TEST, AND REPOSITIONING THE OBSERVATION POINT CANNOT REPAIR IT.** `OI-120(c)`'s `P4` perturbed the NPZ key `w_truth` ×1.05 and required `event_reco` bit-identical; job `56975592` recorded `proxy_hits: 0`. **That is not a perturbation that failed to land** — `event_reco` is fully assigned by `build_event_features`, *the exact call where the probe raises `_Captured`*, before the loader reads `w_truth` at all. The predeclared `IDENTICAL` followed from the order of two statements, so **no perturbation of any magnitude could have made the arm fail**, and it occupied a row a reader counts as a test. **The offered hypothesis is REFUTED and must not be revived:** the loader *does* read raw `w_truth` and *does* derive the trainer's weights from it — **the cause is ordering, not indirection**, and the two have opposite repairs. **Option (b), move the capture point, was costed and is a NON-FIX rather than an expensive fix:** `event_reco` is bound ONCE and never rebound, so a later capture returns a **bit-identical array** — the arm still cannot fail, merely later and after dragging in the TF/ROOT split the early stop exists to avoid. **GENERAL: you cannot falsify a statement about what was read BEFORE an observation point by moving the observation point.** Ask whether a dead arm is dead *positionally* or *structurally* before proposing to reposition anything; and the third option — make it perturb harder — has nothing to perturb harder AT. **DISPOSITION (a) TAKEN: retired, and the ordering argument kept as a CHECK** (`test_loader_ordering_reco_before_truth_weight.py`) that re-derives from the source and records **no coordinate** (`BEN-228`), with premises `P-ONCE/P-USED/P-ORDER/P-ESCAPE/P-LINEAR/P-FIXED`. Two carry the weight: **`P-USED`**, because an absence-based guard that also passes when the thing is absent is `BEN-250`'s shape (rename the key and the claim holds vacuously); and **`P-ESCAPE`**, which closes the call-graph hole a line-range `awk` cannot see — a helper called before the binding reading the key on the caller's behalf. **The guard ships FIVE MUTANTS that make it fail, plus a test that the mutants really mutate** — a guard nobody has seen fail is indistinguishable from a guard that cannot fail, which is precisely the defect being retired; 9 tests, 6 of them observations of failure. **UNANTICIPATED AND WORTH THE ROW: retiring a test subject orphans the regressions that used it as a fixture.** Three of `BEN-290`'s tests took `P4` as their void arm and broke with `KeyError`; the wrong repair is deleting them, since the tri-state defect is a property of the **scoring code**, not of which arm is void — re-pointed at live arms, **then verified by re-introducing the original one-token bug: 5 RED including `BEN-290`'s own guard, 26 GREEN restored.** **Before retiring a test subject, grep for what uses it as a fixture; a regression triggerable only by an arm the probe no longer runs has stopped running, and it stops silently.** Denominator moved `3 of 4`→`3 of 3 live`; **three arms, three bit-identical hashes, one control that fired — unchanged**, `BEN-290`'s quoted string left intact because it is true of its commit, both strings pinned in the test and the correspondence carried in the receipt. Residues closed: the all-void branch said *"the loader refused every truth perturbation"* on a branch where the loader refused **nothing** — **a right conclusion with a reason that could be false, the harder defect because nothing downstream contradicts it** — now reports counts (`BEN-077`); and the per-arm label is now **derived from** the observed value rather than re-stated. **VOID is a fact about the probe, REFUSED a fact about the loader.** Lane D's file; **D authored it and reviewed none of this.** [full](FINDING-20260815-an-arm-whose-answer-was-entailed-by-statement-order.md) | `OI-124` (closed), `BEN-290`, `BEN-250`, `BEN-228`, `CLM-002` | EP-2026-08-15-oi124-disposition |
| BEN-331 | **A MUTATION TEST THAT NEVER APPLIED ITS MUTATION, AND REPORTED GREEN — "the perturbation did not perturb" one level up, in the harness checking the probe that invented the rule.** Verifying that `BEN-290`'s regression still fired after `P4`'s retirement meant re-introducing the one-token bug and expecting RED. The compound command began with a **relative-path** `cp`; an earlier turn had left the shell's cwd inside `docs/orchestration`, so the `cp` and the mutating `python3` both died `No such file or directory` — and the pytest call that followed, using bare filenames that resolved **from the new cwd**, ran against the **unmutated** probe and printed `17 passed`. **The transcript reads as a successful negative control: mutant applied, suite green — which is exactly the report of a regression suite that has been DISARMED.** Only the interleaved `cp:` errors above it revealed otherwise, and **a `tail` would have eaten them** (`BEN-026`). **RULE: a mutation test must assert the mutant is PRESENT before believing the suite's verdict, in the same command that runs the suite** — here `grep -c MUTANT "$P"` → `1` before, `0` after restore. **Three enabling conditions, all repo-general:** `cd` **persists between `Bash` calls while shell state does not**, so a working directory set many turns earlier silently re-scopes every relative path later; **`set -e` was absent**, so two failed commands did not stop the third; and **the failure was structurally invisible to the success signal** — pytest's exit code is about the suite, and the suite was fine, so **nothing pytest can report distinguishes "the regression holds" from "you tested the wrong file."** Same family as the handoff's *six tools reported success over failure in one day*. **The probe already knew to assert its arrays actually changed before scoring an arm (`BEN-181`); the harness checking the probe did not apply the probe's own rule to itself.** Self-caught, self-reported, cost ~2 min. | BEN-332 | **A TRACKED FILE WHOSE CONTENT IS A FUNCTION OF UNTRACKED LOCAL CACHES — SO ITS `--check` GATE CAN NEVER BE GREEN ON TWO MACHINES AT ONCE.** `generate_manifest.py` `os.walk`s `docs/orchestration` and **inventories gitignored artifacts** as well as tracked ones (its own docstring says so). Committed `MANIFEST.tsv` therefore carries **45 rows for `__pycache__/*.pyc`, `.DS_Store`, `.pytest_cache/*`, `runs/*.log` and `state/locks/*.lock`** — one machine's transient state, in a tracked file, gated by `--check`. **Measured, not inferred:** regenerating at the SAME commit in a **clean `git worktree`** produced `rows=768 tracking=tracked:768` and a diff **deleting 40 rows**; regenerating in the main checkout produced `rows=813 tracking=ignored:45,tracked:768` and deleted **none**. All 40 were verified present in the main checkout and `git check-ignore`-positive — **the worktree was not detecting drift, it was CAUSING it.** **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE PUBLISHED REMEDY: `CONVENTION-lane-worktrees.md`'s worktree isolation is exactly WRONG for this one script**, and the instruction to regenerate from a worktree — correct in its reasoning, since a repo-wide regenerator over another lane's uncommitted files would bake them in — **would have committed a 40-row deletion under a narrow message.** What actually makes a main-checkout run safe is narrower and checkable: the generator reads **only `docs/orchestration`**, so the exposure is `git status -- docs/orchestration`, not the whole tree. **THE SENTENCE THAT GENERALISES FURTHEST, and it is the orchestrating session's: an isolation mechanism was reached for because nobody had established which of the two the situation needed** — a clean *tree* or a clean *subdirectory*. Isolation is chosen for what it excludes, and the question nobody asks is what else it excludes that the tool depends on. **The `45` is DERIVED, not counted by eye or by pattern** (`git check-ignore` over every row's path → 45, matching the generator's own `tracking=ignored:45`); a plausible `grep -E` over the obvious path shapes returns **44**, and an independent reader of the committed file reported **47**. Three instruments, three answers, one of them derivable — `BEN-228`'s *validate an instrument against a case it should get wrong* applied to a number in this very row. **Generalisable: before isolating a generator in a worktree, ask what it reads that a worktree does not have.** A worktree reproduces the COMMIT; it does not reproduce the WORKING DIRECTORY, and any generator that walks the filesystem rather than the index sees the difference. **Second-order, and the reason this is not merely cosmetic:** running the test suite for `BEN-330` created 5 new `.pyc` files, so **an agent's own act of verification mutated a tracked artifact** — `--check` cannot distinguish that from real staleness, which is why a `--check` gate here would fail closed on every machine that has ever run `pytest` and would then be switched off (`BEN-228`'s measured pattern). **Not fixed, and deliberately: excluding ignored artifacts is a change to another lane's script and to 45 existing rows, and this lane was asked for a regeneration, not a redesign.** The regeneration was committed as generated — hand-editing generated output is strictly worse. **Cheap fix if anyone takes it: skip `git check-ignore`-positive paths in the walk, or record them in a sidecar that is not `--check`ed.** [full](FINDING-20260815-a-worktree-reproduces-the-commit-not-the-working-directory.md) | `OI-73`, `BEN-228`, `CONVENTION-lane-worktrees.md` | EP-2026-08-15-oi124-disposition |

this is about *authorship* — the specific artifact stating a rule is unusually likely to violate that rule, and the violation survives review because a reader who agrees with the stated rule stops reading. **SIX VERIFIED MEMBERS, one night: (1)** `FINDINGS.md`'s *"`221-229` free"* clause, wrong from `BEN-221`, **in the file carrying the *derive, do not narrate* rule that forbids the clause**; **(2)** an index cell asserting *"NO BEN ROW"* for a finding that had rows, in the file whose rule is that this is one `grep` away — lane A nearly filed a duplicate `BEN-229` off it; **(3)** `ecf014a` cites the probe at `340`/`17186` where it is `343`/`17508`, **derived before that commit's last edit, in the commit whose headline deliverable is a guard against recording coordinates for exactly that reason**; **(4)** a `grep` pattern reporting `47` gitignored manifest rows offered as corroboration of a derivation's `45`, **inside the row citing `BEN-228`** — measured, `git check-ignore` over every row path → `45`, agreeing with the generator's own `tracking=ignored:45`, while a plausible `grep -E` → `44`; **a derivation and a pattern match are not two measurements of the same thing**, and agreement between a derivation and a guess reads as corroboration (`BEN-300`); **(5)** this lane, verifying the list above, used **three successive wrong greps** — one matched the `*(unallocated)*` row *itself* (making it trivially equal to the taken block), one matched the **prose** of every block row (each contains the words *"the `*(unallocated)*` row"*), and **both returned a confident answer that would have been reported as a finding**; caught only by `BEN-228`'s own remedy, validating the instrument against a case whose answer was known independently. **(6) A PREVENTED FAILURE RE-OFFERED AS AN OCCURRENCE, and the only member with a motive:** the orchestrating session proposed member (5*) below, while **its own commit `d10dd78` says the opposite in its body** — *"the `*(unallocated)*` row is advanced to 310-319 IN THIS COMMIT. Leaving it reading '300-309 free' … would have reproduced `BEN-228`'s free-list instance inside the commit filing `BEN-228`'s generalisation."* It **noticed the hazard, prevented it, wrote that down, and then offered the prevention as an occurrence.** Self-diagnosed by its author. **Every other member is a fact nobody checked; this is a fact its author DID check, whose outcome was inverted in the retelling** — so: **evidence assembled from memory of one's own recent work will preferentially recruit near-misses as instances, because the prevention and the occurrence share a memory**, and a near-miss feels like evidence precisely because you lived it. **ONE PROPOSED MEMBER WAS REFUTED AND THAT IS THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE HERE:** *"the mediator's `*(unallocated)*` row went stale after it took a block"* is **FALSE** — every block-taking commit advanced it in the same commit (`300-309`→`310-319` at `d10dd78`, `310-319`→`320-329` at `66c1f0e`, `320-329` and `330-339`→`340-349` at `3262ccf`), and all eight live per-row *"free"* clauses currently hold. The real instance is the **per-row** free clause, not the `*(unallocated)*` row — two different objects in one table. **So a count offered in support of "rules get broken by the artifact asserting them" itself carried an unverified member** (`BEN-313`), which is why this row enumerates with verification instead of counting. **The count was then queried as "should say four" — checked, and it should not: the five never included the refuted member (the four-item proposal did), so the refutation subtracted nothing, and member 6 makes six. What is left is narrow enough to use: **when you write a rule down, spend one check applying it to the artifact you are writing it in — not to the codebase, to THIS paragraph, THIS commit body, THIS row.** All five would have been caught by that check; four of five were caught only by a peer reading afterwards. **Enforcement is attention, stated rather than hidden.** Members 1-2 taken as recorded in `BEN-228`, not independently re-derived; 3, 4, 5 and the refutation measured here.
