| BEN-334 | **THE FILE YOU ARE ABOUT TO RUN ALREADY SAID NO — a ~6-12 GPU-h run was proposed for `OI-125`, and the launcher being proposed carried the denial of that exact run, written the same day.** `67c94df`'s `sbatch_foldforward_instrumented_closure.sh` states verbatim: *"NO RUN IS ATTACHED TO THIS MOVE. The 3-draw re-run was proposed and DENIED on 2026-08-16 … **This lands so the next run that happens FOR ITS OWN REASONS carries the value for free.**"* **The instrumentation was deliberately designed NOT to justify its own run**, and was re-proposed one day later by the session that recorded the denial, without the file being run having been read. **RULE: before approving a run, read the LAUNCHER — not the proposal for it.** A launcher here carries its own predeclaration, expected exit codes, arm layout and standing decisions, none of which appear in the request. **`BEN-333` member 6 one turn worse:** there a near-miss was remembered and mis-classified; here a written decision was not consulted at all, and `BEN-333`'s own remedy (*go and read what you actually did*) failed **because the decision lived in a launcher comment, which nobody classifies as a record** — perfectly placed for whoever executes, perfectly hidden from whoever approves, and those are different people by design. **SECOND AND MORE DANGEROUS HALF: a correct conclusion resting on a REFUTED premise.** The denial's ground was *"the driver takes no seed flag, so a re-run produces new draws"* — **false**: `closure_powered_truth_reweight.py` calls `tf.keras.utils.set_random_seed(int(pol["estimator_seed"]))` against a **frozen policy**, so the training seed is PINNED and only `--split-seed` is a flag. The conclusion survives for a different reason the launcher itself states — the three draws are *"the SAME configuration … so the spread is training nondeterminism"*, **sd `0.000820128`** across `56552326`/`56611837`/`56626305`. **What the false premise would have cost: asked whether closure needs a seed flag that does not exist, it answers YES, and a lane gets dispatched to build one — but a seed flag cannot fix comparability when the seed is already pinned. The missing property is DETERMINISTIC EXECUTION**, a different and far larger job. **A conclusion that survives refutation of its premise is the one most likely to aim the next lane at the wrong target, because the right headline stops anyone re-auditing the reason. When a premise falls but the conclusion stands, re-derive every OTHER decision that rested on it.** **THIRD: the cost figure was right by accident.** *"~6 GPU-h, the prior array ran 3 × ~1h57m"* — the array is **SIX** tasks (`0-2` arm 0, `3-5` arm 1) and `sacct -j 57012031` shows `_0/_1/_2 COMPLETED` at ~1h56m against **`_3/_4/_5` FAILED at ~2 MINUTES** on a dtype defect since repaired (`4e85f0e`, `BEN-314`). **~6 GPU-h was a measurement of a FAILURE read as a measurement of the WORK; a healthy six-task array is ~12 GPU-h.** **An elapsed time from a task that failed is not a cost estimate for a task that works — check the exit state of anything you extrapolate from.** **DISPOSITION: DENY the run; the recorder rides the already-authorized arm-1 resubmit (`AUTHORIZATION-20260815-arm1-resubmit.md`, 3 draws, 5.9 GPU-h) at zero marginal cost — but arm 1 is the CORRECTED arm and `OI-125` is about the UNCORRECTED one, so reporting it as a closure would be `BEN-360` repeating one level up, in the second instrument built to close the hole the first one missed.** `OI-125` closes only on an arm-0 run happening for independent reasons. | BEN-335 | **FOR "DOES X CALL Y", THE TEXT IS NOT THE PROGRAM — a grep answered 2 where the truth was 0, and reporting it would have inverted a verdict.** repair-11 turned on whether `projection_M_from_recipe` independently reconstructs the projection matrix or **re-executes the function it checks** — which would be the `OI-120(c)` defect this lane retired a probe arm for, would leave the wrong-`M` property unestablished, and would have made the verdict `BLOCK` with `authorizes_covariance_stages_4_6: false`. Measured at `13f9e0a` over the same function body: **`grep -c build_projection_M` → 2; `ast` walk over `Call` nodes → 0.** **BOTH HITS ARE DOCSTRING MENTIONS**, and the second one is the sentence *"Kept deliberately free of `build_projection_M`'s helpers"* — **prose asserting the very absence being tested for, counted as its presence.** **A textual hit is not a call:** it may be a docstring, a comment, a string literal, a different function in the same file, or an unused import. **The remedy is not a better regex, it is a different instrument** — four lines of `ast.walk` over the target `FunctionDef` collecting `Call` nodes answers the question actually asked and is immune to all of those. **WHY THIS IS NOT `BEN-344`, and the distinction is the useful part: `BEN-344` is a NULL that could not have been otherwise, and its remedy is *show the instrument capable of firing*. This is a POSITIVE hit that is not the thing it appears to be — the grep CAN fire, DOES fire, and fires on the wrong thing, so `BEN-344`'s remedy would have CONFIRMED it rather than exposed it.** One substitution, two opposite failure directions. **AND THE SAME SUBSTITUTION IS ALREADY IN THE CAMPAIGN FROM THE OTHER SIDE:** `R11-1`, filed by this same lane in the same verdict, is `assertIn("check_projection_matrix_matches_recipe", src)` — **which accepts a commented-out call as evidence of wiring.** There, text-present read as call-present is too generous; here it was too damning. **One instrument, two opposite wrong answers, in one verdict.** That is why lane B's fix for `R11-1` is the right shape: an **execution witness** (`nnz > 0`, `entries_differing == 0`) is a number a commented-out call cannot manufacture — **where `ast` is unavailable, or the question is "did it run" rather than "does it call", the answer is a number the absent behaviour cannot produce.** **WHAT DID NOT CATCH IT, which is the uncomfortable part:** `BEN-344`'s remedy would have confirmed it; a `falsified_by` field would have been written as *"a hit showing delegation"* and satisfied by the grep; and the predeclared bar `B1` **named what must be established and not what may establish it.** **A verdict discipline that specifies the requirement but never the instrument leaves the instrument unexamined, and the instrument is where this one lived.** Caught only by READING the two hits instead of counting them — because `2` is a strange number of times to call a helper you claim not to use. **Scope:** one instance; remedy is specific to *does X call Y* in **Python** — the shell drivers have no AST and the honest instrument there is execution. | BEN-340 | **A "SHARE OF THE CROSS-SECTION" COMPUTED WITHOUT BIN WIDTHS ON A DENSITY ARRAY, IN A RECEIPT WHOSE OWN HEADLINE TOTALS ARE WIDTH-WEIGHTED — SO THE RECEIPT CONTRADICTS ITSELF FROM ITS OWN SHIPPED OPERANDS.** `state/p5a-nominal-vs-cstat-family-percell-20260815.json` reported the 63-cell tail as **3.1% of the cross-section**; the share of the published nominal integral is **15.5%**. And the step the receipt presents as removing a *domain* mismatch (`ratio 1.10267` → `same_domain.total_ratio 1.05656`, with an explicit `WHY_THIS_OVERSTATES` note) removes the **BIN WIDTHS**: both figures are over the **same 257 cells**, the domain contributes `2.9e-5`, and `width_weighting_applied = false` records that the arrays are densities. **So the `+10.267%` SURVIVES as a same-domain, width-weighted statement — the thing the receipt was correcting was not the thing that was wrong.** **CAUGHT PURELY BY FAILING TO RE-DERIVE A PUBLISHED TOTAL FROM PUBLISHED ARRAYS**, with nobody suspecting a defect: `sum(nom·width)` reproduces the receipt's own `nominal_total` to `2.9e-5` while the unweighted sum is **11.3x** off, the `2.9e-5` residual is then *explained* as the 5 excluded flicker cells to **0.7%**, and the extraction's own `total_sigma_cm2_per_nucleon` confirms it at source. **This is `BEN-077` / `CONVENTION-receipt-ingredients.md` working exactly as designed and is evidence FOR that convention, not merely against the receipt — the second such catch in one day.** **THE TRANSFERABLE QUALIFIER, and it is why a careful author produced it: THE DEFECT IS INVISIBLE IN A RATIO.** `sum(nom)/sum(mean)` looks dimensionally innocent because the widths appear to cancel; they only fail to cancel because numerator and denominator **weight the cells differently**, so a width-free sum is a different linear functional of each. ***"It's a ratio, the units drop out"* is the reasoning that produces this defect** — true for a scalar, false for a ratio of sums over unequal bins (`Δp_∥` spans 0.5 to 60 GeV here). MINERvA's own data release flags the same trap unprompted, in an `IMPORTANT` block: *"All cross sections and uncertainties in these files are differential … divided by bin areas(width). IF you want to make Figures 9 and 10 you will need to remove the area(width) normalization."* **Both shares were also computed on the FAMILY MEAN, not on the nominal — a share of the cross-section must be a share of the object that will be published.** **THE WORST COST WAS NOT THE NUMBER:** the wrong figures reached `HANDOFF-20260815-0455Z.md`'s **`RESOLVED TONIGHT — do not re-investigate`** section, and a wrong *do-not-re-investigate* is not a wrong value but **an instruction not to look** — the cheapest way to make a live defect permanent, in the one file a context-less session is told to trust. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, stated because a correction is read as a demolition: it does not overturn the receipt's conclusion.** The median per-cell ratio is weighting-independent and stands, and the concentrated-tail verdict is now **better** supported than the receipt made it — dropping the 63 moves the width-weighted total ratio to `0.99471`. **Remedy followed rather than proposed (`BEN-227`): the two shares are marked `RETRACTED` in the receipt rather than refreshed, and their corrected values live with every operand in `state/RECEIPT-20260815-cstat-tail-geometry-and-weighting-correction.json`.** Found by peer session `B` on the mediator's dispatch; **the receipt's author verified it independently before accepting it and supplied the "invisible in a ratio" qualifier.**  **RECURRED IN THIS ROW'S OWN FILER WITHIN A DAY, in the general form: a proposal has two independent preconditions -- affordable and POSSIBLE -- and the expensive-looking one is not the one that fails.** `OI-126`'s diagnostic was costed from a real `total_seconds` and had NO ENTRY POINT (four fail-closed guards); Joseph authorized it before anyone noticed and no GPU time was spent only because the guards fail closed before work starts. **The tell is identical both times: the derivation was never attempted** -- nobody summed the array two ways, nobody ran the script. [full](FINDING-20260815-a-share-of-total-without-bin-widths.md) | `BEN-077`, `BEN-227`, `BEN-228`, `OI-126` | EP-2026-08-15-cstat-tail-tiering |
| BEN-341 | **THE EXPECTED ANSWER WOULD HAVE BEEN PRODUCED BY A BROKEN INSTRUMENT, AND THE RAW ESTIMATOR MANUFACTURED THE HYPOTHESIS IT WAS TESTING.** Asked to confirm that a replica target's zero-weight fraction is `exp(-1)` in every `p_parallel` column, the honest hazard is that **a mis-ordered row-to-coordinate map produces exactly that answer for the wrong reason** — permuting a constant-rate Bernoulli field still yields `exp(-1)` in every bin — so *"constant, as expected"* from an unvalidated map is worth nothing. Two positive controls were therefore run BEFORE the measurement and reported either way: a **split-point** control (the `[data then bkg]` blocks must be distinguishable — measured mean ratio `0.167`, so they are) and a **structure** control (per-column mean nominal weight must not be flat — measured `max/min = 1.699`). **Neither can prove the map right; both can catch it being wrong, and that asymmetry is the whole design.** **THEN THE PART WORTH KEEPING: the RAW per-column fraction showed an excess that GREW as the column got smaller** — `+2.82%` at the smallest column, `~5 sigma` on the mean of 6 replicas — **an n-dependent structure with the same qualitative shape as the hypothesis under test.** The cause was a contaminant of the estimator, not a property of the bootstrap: **1,916 rows whose NOMINAL weight is already zero (Stay-Positive clipped) are zero in every replica whatever their Poisson multiplicity**, and they inflate a ratio hardest where the denominator is smallest. Conditioning on `nominal > 0` collapses the worst column from `+2.82%` to `+1.08%` (`+1.8 sigma`) and every one of 18 columns to within `1.9 sigma`. **So an unvalidated version of this probe would have returned a FALSE POSITIVE pointing exactly the way its author expected**, and the expected-null branch is the dangerous one precisely because nobody re-examines a confirmation. **RULE: when the expected result is a constant, ask what ELSE would produce that constant before believing it; and before dividing by a per-bin count, enumerate the rows that are in the numerator for reasons unrelated to the effect.** Generalises `BEN-228`'s *validate an instrument against a case it should get wrong* from instruments that report the wrong number to instruments that report the RIGHT number for the wrong reason. Found while closing out a tiebreak this same lane designed and which was refused on review — **third instance in one day of `BEN-340`'s shape, and the invalidating operand (`exp(-1)`) was already in this lane's own committed receipt.** [full](FINDING-20260815-the-expected-answer-from-a-broken-instrument.md), [receipt](state/RECEIPT-20260815-oi126-tiebreak-retirement.json) | `BEN-340`, `BEN-228`, `OI-126` | EP-2026-08-15-oi126-tiebreak |
| BEN-342 | **A POWER TEST LICENSES ONE AXIS, AND THIS FIXTURE IS NUMERICALLY DEGENERATE ON THE AXIS THAT MATTERS.** Checking `67c94df`'s end-of-run fold-forward recorder by `BEN-360`'s own rule: **the instrument is CORRECT** -- same leg-resolution rule, same mask, same quotient as the definition at `train_fullevent_nominal.py:553-577`, and only `CompileModels(fixed=True)` intervenes before the driver's read (`omnifold.py:172-177`; zero `weights_push` references inside it; the other assignment at `:433` is in `LoadStart()`, called at `:166` BEFORE the loop). **The defect is in its test.** The load-bearing bit-identity assertion is built the right way -- `driver_ratio` is an INDEPENDENTLY written expression of the driver's formula, not a call back into the recorder -- and it ships a power control showing a wrong-MOMENT capture failing. **But the fixture's `RunStep2` assigns a UNIFORM push, and for a uniform push `sum(w*push)/sum(w) = push` for ANY `w`:** measured on it, the reco-leg and truth-leg reductions are **bit-identical (`1.2999999523162842`)**, so the assertion would pass unchanged had `_ff_reduce` used `mc.weight` instead of `mc_weight_reco`. **That is precisely the axis this codebase documents as hazardous** (`omnifold.py:209` *"TRUTH leg -- deliberately `self.mc.weight`, NOT `self.mc_weight_reco`"* against `:189`'s reco leg; `train_fullevent_nominal.py:575`). One-line fix, measured: a non-uniform push gives `1.3133333333` vs `1.3100000000` and the legs separate. **UNEXERCISED, NOT WRONG -- no number in any artifact is affected.** **RULE: `BEN-314` asks whether a test CAN fail; that is necessary and not sufficient, because one control licenses exactly the axis it perturbs and is SILENT on every other.** Enumerate the plausible-wrong variants first, then check the fixture's VALUES for degeneracy on each -- all-ones weights and uniform arrays are chosen because they make the arithmetic readable, which is the same property that makes them blind. And **name the axis a control covers**: *"power-tested"* unqualified invites exactly this generalisation. **SECOND, `BEN-361`'s shape: the *"75.8 draw-sd"* figure in three places (instrument `:196`, `:561`; test `:564`) does NOT re-derive** -- gap `-0.030253` against the final-push `sd 0.000399` is **`75.8` draw-sd**. **CORRECTED 2026-08-16: this row first said `105` "needs `sd 0.000288`, stated nowhere" -- WRONG, and the error was this lane's, from grepping the wrapper, test and predeclaration but NOT the receipt (a null grep is evidence about the search, not the world). `104.7` is REAL and correctly computed and its own key says what it is:** `RECEIPT-foldforward-instrumented-closure-20260815.json`, section `THE_QUANTITY_MISMATCH_READ_THIS_FIRST` / `the_naive_read_and_what_it_would_have_produced`, `predicted_minus_this 0.030252362049327908` / `sd 0.0002888930898171582` / `in_draw_sd_of_that_row 104.7181920082435`. **So it was MIS-NORMALISED -- divided by the spread of the SUBSTITUTED consumed-push rows, the sd OF THAT ROW -- not fabricated, and those have different fixes; "derives from nothing" invites a later reader to conclude it was invented.** The corrected figure now re-derives from two receipt FIELDS rather than prose: `0.030252362 / 0.00039936135` (`arm0_recovered_final_push.sd_n3`) `= 75.75`; `131.3` is a third normalisation nobody claimed. **The predeclared `E4` amplitude DOES re-derive (`-2.991%` for *"roughly -3%"*) and the sign flip is true -- so the predeclaration passed and the prose around it did not.** [full](FINDING-20260816-a-fixture-degenerate-on-the-axis-under-test.md) | `BEN-360`, `BEN-314`, `BEN-361`, `BEN-077` | EP-2026-08-16-endofrun-instrument-read |
| BEN-343 | **A TEST THAT FAILS FOR ANYONE RUNNING THE SUITE FROM A WORKTREE BEHIND `main`.** `test_p4_token_gate_scope_and_rev.py::Defect5_SymbolicCodeRev::test_MUTATION_prefix_helper_reports_every_symbolic_rev_in_history` loops over `("HEAD", "main", "HEAD~0", "HEAD~3")` and asserts each is in history. `code_rev_in_history` asks whether a rev is an ANCESTOR of `HEAD`, and in a worktree on a feature branch `main` resolves fine but **is not necessarily an ancestor.** Measured at the failure: branch `worktree-lane-b-oi126`, `HEAD e07b986`, `main 6e05985`, `merge-base --is-ancestor main HEAD` **false**, `rev-list --count HEAD..main` **1** -- another lane had pushed mid-edit. Cleared on rebase, and the ancestry was **re-measured to confirm the mechanism rather than inferred from the failure going away.** **Nothing about the test's subject is wrong**; the fourth loop element asserts a property of the RUNNER'S GIT POSITION. **`BEN-332`'s shape with a different dependency** -- there a tracked file's content was a function of untracked caches, here a tracked test's RESULT is a function of the runner's branch. A test that passes on `main` and fails from a worktree is not testing the code, it is reporting where you are standing. **Why it matters here rather than in general: this campaign runs most work from worktrees** (`CONVENTION-lane-worktrees.md`, and audit lanes are explicitly given throwaway ones), and a worktree is behind `main` for the whole window between another lane pushing and this one rebasing. **So it fails for most lanes most of the day, and each pays the triage cost of ruling out their own edit** -- which is the real damage, since a spurious failure adjacent to your own change cannot be dismissed without checking. **THE FIX IS FREE AND COSTS NO COVERAGE: `HEAD`, `HEAD~0` and `HEAD~3` are symbolic and in history by construction from any checkout, so `'main'` is the ONLY environment-dependent element and adds nothing** -- the property under test is that the pre-fix helper treats SYMBOLIC names as in-history, and `HEAD~3` is symbolic. Drop it, or create a probe branch in the test instead of borrowing the repository's. **NOT APPLIED -- another lane's file, and what the test means to cover is the owner's judgement (`BEN-300`).** Filed so the next lane seeing a red suite does not re-triage it from scratch. | BEN-344 | **A NULL THAT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN OTHERWISE IS NOT WEAK EVIDENCE -- IT IS NOT EVIDENCE, AND IT READS AS STRONG BECAUSE IT IS WELL-FORMED.** repair-10 `#8` cited `grep -c p4_check_verifier_token` returning **0** in both the sweep tool and the committed snapshot. The tool half was a real omission (fixed). **The snapshot half was structurally incapable of returning non-zero:** measured before the fix, the same grep returned **0** for `p4_lib`, `p4_evidence`, `p4_adopt_standard`, `run_p4_standard` and `p4_validate_active_lateral` -- **every module, swept or not** -- because `summary()` recorded the sweep's OUTPUT (`n_fields`, `n_gates`, `fields`, `gates`) and never its SCOPE. No module name was ever in that file. **Distinct from `BEN-315`**, where the search was real and came back empty; here no input could have made it succeed, so it measured the FILE FORMAT. It read as strong evidence because every property that makes a citation trustworthy was present -- a named symbol, a real command, reproducible, citing a committed artifact -- and the one absent property is the one nobody checks: **that the measurement had a reachable other outcome.** **THE COMPOUNDING FACT, which makes the class dangerous rather than merely wrong: closing the gap produced no signal either** (`n_fields 115 -> 115`, `n_gates 28 -> 28`, because that module writes 0 swept fields and defines 0 `check_`/`require_`/`prove_` gates). **A gap that produces no signal when closed produced none when opened** -- self-perpetuating, and fixing the instance without the format would have left the next omission equally undetectable while looking like a fix. **RULE, in lane `C`'s stronger form, ADOPTED here over this row's original three-step version because it is a habit rather than a diagnosis: A NULL RESULT MUST BE SHOWN CAPABLE OF BEING NON-NULL BY THE SAME INSTRUMENT, IN THE SAME RUN.** *Same instrument* and *same run* both do work -- a check that fired last week, or a sibling that fires on similar input, licenses nothing about the silence in front of you. `C` accepted this row at `bf97279` after verifying `#8` at its own `code_rev 0e83b54` (0 for six modules), **then reproduced the class within the hour**: comparing the execution surface at `0e83b54` against HEAD it ran both derivations in one worktree and compared the old surface WITH ITSELF, printing `IDENTICAL`. Two guards caught it -- inject a line and require the `diff` to fire, AND compare the two outputs' `sha256` to prove they came from different derivations. **The second exposed a THIRD failure the first could not: one side had produced an EMPTY FILE from a wrong cwd, and an empty file diffs clean against anything** -- so even a `diff` shown able to fire reports success on two absences, because **the identity of the operands is a separate claim from the result of comparing them.** Generalises past `grep` to any comparison whose pass is an absence: `diff`, `assertEqual`, `assertNotIn`, every guard that succeeds by finding nothing. **OPERATIONAL FORM, adopted in the repair-11 verdict schema at the orchestrator's request: every defect row carries a `falsified_by` field naming the observation that would have shown the defect ABSENT, and a row where that cannot be written is a SUSPICION, not a defect** -- under which repair-10's `#8` would have been filed as a suspicion. **INSTANCES ENUMERATED, NOT COUNTED (`BEN-313`): four in about one day** -- `BEN-331` (a mutation test that never applied its mutation, reported green), `BEN-342` (a row-uniform fixture, so `sum(w*push)/sum(w)` equals `push` for ANY `w` and the leg could not be distinguished), this row's `#8` grep, and `C`'s surface self-comparison. **Two lanes, four instances, and in three of the four the author found it** -- which is the argument for the executable form: the class is caught neither by care nor by review, only by making the instrument demonstrate it can fail in the run whose silence you are about to believe. Run the null against a case that must fail it (`grep -c p4_lib` would have taken seconds); ask what closing the gap would change, and if nothing is observable then THAT is the defect; and distinguish **not present** from **not recordable** -- they read identically and license opposite conclusions. **AUDIT OF ALL SEVEN repair-10 DEFECTS' EVIDENCE, requested by the mediator and re-derived this session: one half artifact (`#8`), one falsifiable but measuring the WRONG PROPERTY (`#7` -- "unchanged since `c308a9c`", derived 0 commits, is evidence of NEGLECT not INADEQUACY; the adequacy claim rests on the author's self-declaration, i.e. testimony), and FIVE properly evidenced** -- `#9` strongest of all, since re-running its own cited command yields the six `Repair-4 defect(s)` commits that refute the claim it sits beside. **So NOT a systemic failure of this campaign's verdicts, stated as plainly as the defect** because "one verdict had an artifact" invites a conclusion the audit does not support. **The pattern in both weak items: an observation about the file's HISTORY or FORMAT substituted for an observation about its BEHAVIOUR** -- `git log` and `grep -c` are the cheapest measurements available, which is why they get reached for when the claim needs the expensive one. Verdict JSON NOT edited; cited, not amended. | BEN-314 | **A TEST SUITE THAT COULD NOT FAIL ON THE INTERFACE IT EXISTED TO PROTECT — 18 passing tests, both guards mutation-tested, and none of them could see it.** Arm 1 rescaled the engine's `weights_push` by a Python-float scalar, promoting a **float32** array (`omnifold.py:164,168`) to float64; the engine packs it into `y_true` col 1 (`:360`) and `net.weighted_binary_crossentropy:13` multiplies it against float32 logits, so job **`57012031_3` died `1:0` at `00:02:05`** in ITERATION 1 / STEP 1 — the first point the corrected weights meet the loss. **The tests exercised the correction's ARITHMETIC against fixtures (scalar-not-per-cell, applied-before-delegation, ratio-equals-R) and every one of those was CORRECT.** The defect was the dtype of the object handed BACK. **A suite that exercises your code without exercising the collaborator your code FEEDS cannot fail on a contract violation at that boundary, and mutation testing does not rescue it — I mutated the arithmetic three ways and caught all three, which measured the guards against the arithmetic and said nothing about the boundary its output crosses.** The check is: **name the next function that touches this object, and call it.** Aggravating: the trap is written in the wrapped driver's own docstring ("FLOAT32 INTO THE ENGINE", measured 2026-08-05) and that driver fails closed on the two LOADER weight dtypes — but `weights_push` is engine-internal and **this module is the first thing outside the engine ever to write to it**, so it is the first place that guard's coverage gap could bite. Remedy is executable: a test that packs exactly as `:360` does and calls the engine's OWN loss, plus a power test that the loss really rejects float64. Re-introducing the bug turns **8** tests red. Distinct from `BEN-310` (a measurement that could not move) and `BEN-119` (power on the inputs, not the evidence class); the axis is **who else consumes your output**. **Arm 0 never applies the factor and was healthy throughout.** [full](FINDING-20260815-a-suite-that-could-not-fail-on-its-own-interface.md) *Executor lane.* | CLM-012 | EP-2026-08-15-foldforward-closure-run |
| BEN-315 | **FOUR CLAIMS ABOUT CODE, EACH INFERRED FROM ITS STRUCTURE, NONE READ AT THE SITE — all four caught by another lane, in one session, by a lane enforcing the same standard outward.** (1) the `[0,49]` replica-index bound reported in **one** file when it is in **three** (`build_fullevent_replica_target.py:150`, `train_fullevent_replica.py:320`, `extract_fullevent_replica.py:443`) — the `grep` was cut by `head -8` and **the truncation was invisible in the output**; (2) `uint8` truncation attributed to the **data** factor when every cited site is on the **sig/bkg** streams and the data factor is cast to float at `:649/:696/:948` and hashed **uncast**; (3) *"the (b) hypothesis is anchored on nothing"* when it was anchored on the definition — one line, `r = T_n[bc] / T_k[bc]`, settled it; (4) row-level `Poisson(1)` zeros called "not the same object" as a bin observing zero counts, when a bin count **is** the sum of its rows' multiplicities, so for a one-row bin they are identical — exactly the sparse regime under study. **THE SHAPE: a claim about what code DOES, inferred from how code is ARRANGED. In all four the check cost one command and the wrong answer was the structurally plausible one; (2) and (4) also ran with the lane's own prior argument, which is the direction a claim is checked least.** Rule: **a claim about what code does is not established until the code is read AT THE SITE, and a truncated read is not a read.** `BEN-026` is the ancestor and instance (1) is it in non-shell clothing — the same truncation applied to source instead of a job log, by the lane that was enforcing `BEN-026` for job output and had not generalised it. Executable: never `head`/`tail` a `grep` you will conclude from (count, or read all of it); name the array at every site for a dtype claim; **quote the line that computes it** for a claim about a computed quantity. **The hazard is not laziness — a `grep` result, a docstring and a parameter list all produce the sensation of having looked, and three of the four were made while looking at real output.** Same family as `BEN-312` (an assertion that looks like verification) and `BEN-314` (exercising a thing adjacent to the claim). **FIFTH INSTANCE 2026-08-16, in §6 of the long form, and the truncation was the lane's OWN tooling: surveying a nested receipt with `if len(s) < 200: print(...)` silently dropped 18 of its 280 leaves, including the 250-character field that DEFINES the population — on which evidence the lane concluded the receipt documented that population by count and not definition, a `BEN-077` row it was one edit from filing. `grep -c pass_truth` would have returned `1`.** The new generalisation: **a length filter over a document is a SEMANTIC filter, because in a well-written receipt the short leaves are values and the long leaves are the definitions, provenance and caveats** — so it removes exactly the fields that answer *why*, and removes them without saying so. **Unreported omission is the entire defect in both truncation instances (`head -8`, `len(s) < 200`), and a one-line "N of M omitted" count defeats both.** Also the first instance running toward a finding worth filing rather than toward the lane's own prior — **the more tempting direction on a campaign that rewards finding things.** Caught by the lane itself before filing, which is §5's standard met once and is not a trend. | BEN-316 | **A GATE WHOSE ONLY MEASURABLE QUANTITY IS BLAS ACCUMULATION ORDER — and a `BLOCK` that quoted a disclaimer as the promise it broke.** `p4_lib.py:1413-1435` `check_projection_validity` gates the 5D→4D projection on `M C M^T` agreeing with what its docstring calls *"a direct block-sum recomputation"* by *"an independent route"*. The code is `MH = M @ C_high` then `direct[i,:] = MH[i,:] @ M.T` row by row — **which IS `MH @ M.T` by the definition of matrix multiplication**, so the leg is the same product written out one row at a time and **the only quantity `err` can ever hold is the difference between a row-at-a-time GEMV and a whole-matrix GEMM.** MEASURED at `HEAD` (`p4_lib.py` byte-identical to the verdict `code_rev` `0e83b54`): `relerr = 1.851e-16` against a `1e-9` threshold, **5.40e6× headroom**; `project()` bit-identical to the one-shot; and on the suite's own fixture (`tests/test_p4_repair.py:136-143`) **EXACTLY `0.0`**, so `assertLess(relerr, 1e-12)` compares zero to a tolerance (`BEN-314`/`BEN-312` family, not filed separately — an identity leg that cannot fail cannot be given a test that can). **CONFIRMS repair-10's `N3` and CORRECTS IT TWICE.** (1) **The quotation is INVERTED.** `N3` says the docstring *promises* it *"compares against an independently-produced product"*; `:1414-1415` says **"Recomputation identities only -- nothing here compares against an independently-produced product."** The words match because the verdict lifted them from the sentence that negates them, and **the two repairs differ**: the quoted promise points at building a product comparison the function deliberately refuses, the real overclaim points at writing an actual `sum_{a,b} M[i,a] C[a,b] M[j,b]` accumulation or deleting the leg and the sentence together. (2) **"A check that cannot fail" is falsifiable and FALSE** — mutation-tested, a `2.0 *` edit to `project()` **IS caught** at `rel 5.000e-01`, so it is a **source-drift regression guard on `project()`**, while a corrupted `M` (row 0 ×3) **passes at `3.033e-17`**. The exact defect: *not* a check that cannot fail, but one that **cannot fail for any reason connected to the validity of the projection** — every error in the premise both legs share is invisible. **`N3`'s severity survives that narrowing intact** (stages 4-6 need the second thing and the gate supplies only the first), **but a defect stated in a falsifiable form that turns out false is the kind a repair lane dismisses wholesale, including the two-thirds that is correct.** `AGREED-WITH-CORRECTION` (`BEN-352`'s shape): the `BLOCK`, the six other defects, `self_guards_adequate: NO` and `authorizes_covariance_stages_4_6: false` are **untouched**, and this lane holds no `P4_VERIFIER_PASS` token and adjudicates nothing. **A recomputation that re-encodes the formula it is checking is `BEN-300`'s single-source case.** Defect already on the record at `ND_OMNIFOLD_RUN_LOG.md:8805` from repair-8 — **this is the second lane to read it and the first to measure it.** Executable: `state/probe-projection-identity-leg-20260816.py`, any cwd, exits non-zero if the behaviour changes. RESUBMIT; **nobody applied it to arm 0.** All three arm-0 logs are **3-pin** — they ran before `c6edc13` added the wrapper pin, which is the pin that covers the instrumentation — **and the old launcher printed NO per-file digests at all**, so arm 0 does not even record the three values it did check. **Arm 0 is the run `VL134`/`VL135`/`VL136` come from and half of `VL138`.** **RECOVERED FROM THE PRODUCTS INSTEAD OF THE LAUNCHER, which is a measurement and not the timeline:** two versions existed at arm 0's `12:23:59Z` launch, and `948e2b07`'s `install_fold_forward_recorder(base)` had **no `correct` parameter**, so it wrote none of `fold_forward_arm` / `fold_forward_correction_applied` / `correction_requested` — all three present in arm 0's report — while an unrenamed `recovery_criteria_met` and an absent non-quotability `label` exclude `b24cfefe` from above and the timeline excludes `ee269b09`. **⇒ arm 0 ran `253f25c0`**, a linkage written down nowhere before this finding: that digest appears in exactly ONE tracked file, the authorization, and there it means *the stale copy to be replaced*. **THE TWO ARMS OF `VL138` RAN DIFFERENT WRAPPER VERSIONS** (`253f25c0` vs `ee269b09`) and the contrast **SURVIVES**: `git diff` is **one hunk, 21 insertions**, every changed line inside the `if correct:` block at `:141` (the `BEN-314` dtype repair), which arm 0 with `correct=False` never enters — **diffed rather than assumed, which is the only difference between this and `BEN-315`.** **STILL OPEN, and the reason this is filed rather than closed: NO RECEIPT ATTESTS THAT THE ANNEAL TOOK EFFECT.** All six ran `--annealed` and the composition is real, but the wrapper binds `lr_records` and discards it, writing only `fold_forward_composed_with_annealed_arm = True` — **which records that the install function was CALLED and is `True` even if `fit_lr_records` is EMPTY, the exact state `closure_powered_annealed_lr.py:114-115` exists to fail closed on.** `assert_anneal_took_effect` is called only from that module's `main()` (`:178`), which the wrapper bypasses by design — **so the one guard that distinguishes an annealed run from an un-annealed one sits in the tree wired into a path these six runs did not take** (`BEN-312`'s family). Measured: **no `lr_proof` in any of the six receipts, no `[annealed] LR pattern VERIFIED` in any of the twelve log files** (the only `anneal|learning` matches are the word inside `G0`), **and none of the 29 wrapper tests exercises the annealed composition.** **BOUNDED, NOT ATTESTED:** the band `VL136` passes lives on runs whose anneal WAS proven — `annealed-shape-r2-terminal-56552326.json` carries `anneal_lr_proof pass=True`, 2 fits at `1e-4` and 4 at `1e-5`, 6 records at `niter=3` — and arm 0 is **bit-identical** to that run on `h_prior`/`h_target`/`h_untilted` with recovery `0.535` draw-sd from its mean inside a `1.557e-03` band, so an un-annealed arm 0 would have to land there by coincidence. **But the static spectra match because the INPUTS match, which is independent of the learning rate**, so this bounds the risk and must not be recorded as attestation. **Disposition: a PROVENANCE gap, not a suspicion of a wrong configuration.** Fix is TWO lines, not one (`base_lr` is derived from the records at `:177`, so the assertion is **partly self-referential** — it catches an empty record list and a wrong pattern but **cannot** catch a globally wrong base rate); **not implemented, not authorized, and it cannot retro-attest the six runs.** **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE, and it indicts an action this lane was authorized to take: the only direct witness to arm 0's wrapper was the file on `/pscratch`, and the authorized arm-1 copy OVERWROTE it.** It survives only because the interlock demonstration happened to print `253f25c0…` in a refusal message — **a by-product of testing the gate, not a decision to preserve provenance**, and a clean one-step "copy both" would have lost it. **Before overwriting any cluster file a completed run's provenance depends on, record its digest in the same turn; a launcher that does not pin a file makes every later copy of it destructive, and the run's own logs will not tell you what it ran.** General form: **`G0`'s pin set defines what a run can prove about itself after the fact, so a file added to the pin map later leaves every EARLIER run permanently unattested on that axis — and the earlier runs are the ones already published.** [full](FINDING-20260816-the-arm-whose-instrumentation-nothing-pinned.md) *Executor lane, against its own instrumentation.* | `BEN-077`, `BEN-312`, `BEN-314`, `BEN-315`, `OI-125` | EP-2026-08-16-arm0-provenance-and-anneal-attestation |
| BEN-318 | **REDUNDANCY THAT ARISES BY CONSTRUCTION IS A FREE INTERNAL CHECK, AND TRIMMING IT IS THE TEMPTING MISTAKE — plus the harder rule that resolves an apparent contradiction with `BEN-316`.** `67c94df` hooked `RunStep2` beside the existing `RunStep1` hook, and `niter-1` of the new rows **duplicate rows already in the report** because the push `RunStep2(i)` leaves IS the push `RunStep1(i+1)` consumes. **The duplication was not designed; it fell out of hooking two adjacent points in one loop.** Two options: TRIM (emit only the one new row -- smaller output, no information lost, **and no check**) or ENFORCE (gate the overlap to EXACT equality, `!=` on floats, no tolerance). **Trimming looks like housekeeping, a reviewer optimising for concision would ask for it, and the argument "those rows are already in the report" is TRUE** — what it discards is the only thing in the run able to catch `BEN-360`'s failure, a recorder capturing the neighbouring quantity. **Before: "the recorder might read at the wrong moment" was a paragraph in a finding. After: the run refuses.** `CLAUDE.md`'s own trade — a document costs tokens in every future session, a check costs zero and cannot be skipped — reached from the other direction, **and the gate cost nothing: both numbers were already being produced and the only decision was whether anything compared them.** RULE: **when two parts of a system compute the same quantity by construction, do not deduplicate -- COMPARE.** **§2, AND IT IS THE PART THAT TOOK WORK: this finding appears to CONTRADICT `BEN-316`, filed hours earlier by the same lane.** `BEN-316` condemns a gate for checking `M C M^T` against a second computation that **re-encodes the same formula** (*"`BEN-300`'s single-source case"*), and then this finding's gate **deliberately makes both sides share one implementation** — `_ff_reduce` was EXTRACTED so the two hooks cannot compute the fold-forward differently, where two copies would have been more independent. **The lane argued for independence in the morning and against it in the evening, and both are right.** The resolution is **what the check is FOR**: `check_projection_validity`'s leg claims *the formula is correctly implemented*, so it must share the INPUTS and differ in the ROUTE — it fails because it shares the route, the thing it claims to test. The overlap gate claims *two hooks read the same array at the same logical MOMENT*, so it must share the REDUCTION exactly and differ only in the moment. **Duplicate the reduction and the gate fails in the mirror-image way: any implementation difference surfaces as a value difference, which is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a timing error** — it would fire on a formatting change and report "the hooks disagree" when what disagreed was the arithmetic. **A confounded check is not a weaker check; it is a check of a DIFFERENT PROPOSITION.** RULE: **a cross-check must SHARE exactly what it is not testing and DIFFER in exactly what it is; ask what the check CLAIMS before reusing an implementation, because "more independent" is not automatically stronger and "shared" is not automatically a restatement.** Cheapest way to spot the `BEN-316` family in future: **a check whose two sides differ in nothing that matters cannot fail; one whose sides differ in too much cannot localise.** **RECORDED AGAINST THE LANE: `_ff_reduce` was extracted for TIDINESS first, and only afterwards did it become clear that two copies would compare implementations rather than moments while looking identical in the report and in the test names. The right decision was reached for the wrong reason, and the wrong reason does not generalise.** Filed as its own row at the mediator's direction rather than as a clause inside `BEN-360`, for the reason `BEN-361` needed separating: **a clause inside another finding is read as that finding's illustration, not as a rule.** Power-tested by `test_the_overlap_gate_REFUSES_a_disagreement`. | BEN-319 | **A CROSS-CHECK REPORTED AS ESTIMATOR DISAGREEMENT THAT IDENTICALLY MEASURES A SUPPORT MISMATCH — and the mislabel cost a whole hypothesis space aimed at the wrong question.** Stage 6 prints `marginal vs INDEPENDENT 4D: n=4825 median=0.0443 p90=0.2083 max=0.7285 over3%=3009 integral_ratio=1.005578`, which reads as two estimators of one quantity disagreeing by a median `4.4%` with a worst bin at `72.9%`. **MEASURED: `max|rel - (frac_seen - 1)| = 1.187e-14` against `max|rel| = 0.7285`**, where `frac_seen[r]` is the summed REPORTED 5D `hUnfoldND` over column `r`'s W bins divided by the 4D `hUnfoldND` — **so the cross-section conversion CANCELS EXACTLY and the number carries no information beyond the per-column CONTENT RATIO.** `corr = 1.000`, checked ALGEBRAICALLY because a correlation of 1 can still hide an offset. **A typical column reports 2 of its 6 W bins (median coverage `0.333`, fully covered `3.56%`), and on the commensurable subset — `frac_seen` within 1% of 1, `n=702` of 4825 — the two agree to `0.45%` median, `max 0.999%`.** So the `4.4%` is not created by projection or marginalization; it is inherited one-for-one from two reported supports being different subsets of one spectrum. **ALL THREE COMMUTATION MECHANISMS (nonlinearity / prior-weighted response collapse / regularization mismatch) REFUTED BY ONE TABLE:** median `|rel|` by reported-W-count is `nW=1 -> 0.0572`, `2 -> 0.0519`, `3 -> 0.0363`, `4 -> 0.0215`, `5 -> 0.0190`, `6 -> 0.0264`. **At `nW=1` the row of `M` has ONE entry, so no marginalization occurs at all — and those bins disagree the MOST. A commutation effect must VANISH where there is nothing to marginalize; this one is MAXIMAL there.** That is the wrong SIGN OF DEPENDENCE, which no coupling strength repairs. **PROJECTOR EXONERATED independently:** `M @ x5` vs the 5D producer's OWN `hXSecND_dropLast_flat` agrees at `max rel 3.107e-16`, median exactly `0`. **NOISE STORY EXCLUDED BY DATA, not argument** (`BEN-025`, realized exceedance): median `|rel|` by cross-section quartile is flat at `[0.0373, 0.0456, 0.0553, 0.0439]`, and the `|rel|>10%` bins are **3.6x BRIGHTER** than typical (`3.852e-41` vs `1.070e-41`) — only the five bins above `50%` are faint. **THE DEFECT IS THE LABEL, and it fails by being INVESTIGATED rather than loudly:** the framing licensed a physics hypothesis space, and the reader it misdirected was the campaign's most careful one. **CONVENTION INTACT AND STRENGTHENED** — bin-by-bin equality was never coherent across different supports, so retiring the `3%` gate (2026-08-09) was right — **with ONE wording correction: the docstring says the comparison "characterises the independent unfold"; it does NOT. It characterises THE SUPPORT DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO PRODUCTS, a third thing belonging to neither estimator**, and that attribution is what invites the next reader to re-ask this. FIX: report `frac_seen` alongside, or quote only the commensurable subset. **NOT PATCHED — the lane that verifies a gate should not also edit the module under it** (repair-12 was under this lane's verification when this was found). **STILL OPEN and not read as closed:** `frac_seen > 1.01` in **1876 of 4825 columns (39%)** — the summed REPORTED 5D content EXCEEDS the 4D content, which "the 5D reports a subset" cannot explain, so the well-posed question survives and is where mechanisms (1) and (3) could legitimately live. **AGAINST THIS LANE, TWICE, while producing this:** a completeness probe for mechanism 2 returned exactly `0.0000` on all 4825 bins — the signature of an IDENTITY, not an agreement — and three weightings including UNIFORM all gave `~1e-14`; **a quantity that does not move when you change its own weights is not measuring the weighting** (`BEN-316`'s shape in this lane's own instrument). Its real content, kept rather than deleted: completeness has **no W-dependence within a column**, so mechanism 2 via efficiency is **structurally absent** rather than small. And a "`|rel|` grows with W-shape concentration" result was **withdrawn as corroboration** on noticing its top quartile `[1.00,1.00]` was exactly the `nW=1` bins — `BEN-300` applied to this lane's own evidence. all five copies were prose and none named the gate, so none was cheaply falsifiable. **CAUGHT ONLY BY THE PRINCIPAL'S OWN CONDITION** (*"if anyone else agrees with you"*), and the non-author returned **AGREED-WITH-CORRECTION** — agreeing the disposition while refuting the basis, **a verdict a plain agree/disagree bit cannot express and would have logged as clean agreement.** RUN_LOG entries corrected by APPEND, not rewrite: they were true as chronology and false as present tense. **TWO ITEMS ESCALATED, NOT ADJUDICATED (`OI-75`): the 2026-08-08 run is unreconciled with the standing *"no cluster P4 run"* hold at `P4_STANDARD_STATUS.md:4` and has no record anywhere in the repo — Joseph's question, unanswered; | BEN-351 | **TWO LANES WROTE THE SAME PUBLICATION PASSAGE FROM THE SAME PROMPT, ONE OF THEM REPORTED DEAD WHEN IT HAD IN FACT FINISHED AND PUSHED — and the duplication is the only reason a stale code claim in the landed text was caught.** This session was told *"a previous session was given this and stopped responding; you are a fresh start"* and *"the working tree is clean — nothing was left half-written."* **Both statements were true of the WORKTREE and false of the REMOTE.** The predecessor had completed the assignment and pushed `e61624b` while this session was writing; the duplicate surfaced only at `git push` → `! | BEN-360 | **AN INSTRUMENT BUILT TO CLOSE A "THIS QUANTITY IS NOT RECORDED" HOLE RECORDED THE NEIGHBOURING QUANTITY, LEFT THE ORIGINAL ONE UNRECORDED, AND MADE ITS OWN PREDECLARATION LOOK REFUTED.** `OI-125` says the closure records no fold-forward scalar, so `dev = |ratio/R − 1|` is not formable for `VL100`'s run. The instrument built to fix that hooks `RunStep1` (`closure_foldforward_instrumented.py:115`) and records the push at the point of **consumption**, with `:243-246` asserting `len(records)==niter` — so at `niter=3` the series is complete, self-consistent, and **excludes the push `RunStep2(2)` leaves**, which no `RunStep1` consumes. **But the quantity `OI-125` is about is the end-of-run one:** `train_fullevent_nominal.py:576-577` computes the nominal's recorded fold-forward from `push` **after `Unfold()`**, so the nominal's `0.736746` — the 34% deficit the whole `OI-71`/`OI-125` argument rests on — is the end-of-run scalar and the like-for-like closure number is recorded by no row. **THE COST IS THAT THE RECEIPTS APPEAR TO REFUTE THEIR OWN PREDECLARATION.** §2 predicted `≈1.011418`; the last recorded row is `≈0.9812`, so a reader keying off "the final iteration" gets `−1.9%` against `+1.1%` — **75.8 draw-sd away with the sign of `ratio − 1` flipped**, which reads as the loudest possible disagreement on a predeclaration that pre-committed to disagreement outranking everything else. Recovered from `weights_push` + `dump_rows_b` under the recorder's own reduction, arm 0 gives `1.010879 ± 0.000399` (3 draws) and §2 **AGREES** at `1.17` prediction-sd. **The receipt says so in a key beside the numbers** — `push_entering_this_iteration_left_by` reads `'RunStep2(1)'` on the last row — **so nothing was hidden; the field that disambiguates it is one a reader has to already suspect they need.** **THE TRANSFERABLE SHAPE: when you add an instrument to close "X is not recorded", write down the definition of X from the artifact that already records it, and check your instrument's output against that definition — not against your own reduction, which is guaranteed to agree with itself.** Same family as `BEN-312` (the verifier must name every object the run's behaviour depends on) one axis over: here the object was named and the *reduction* was not. **`OI-125` is NARROWED, NOT CLOSED**, and its own instruction holds — do not close it by citing `1.011418` **or** the number above; both are reconstructions. **Long form: [FINDING-20260815-an-instrument-recorded-the-neighbouring-quantity.md](FINDING-20260815-an-instrument-recorded-the-neighbouring-quantity.md).** [receipt](state/RECEIPT-foldforward-instrumented-closure-20260815.json) *Fold-forward closure lane.* | `BEN-312`, `BEN-310`, `BEN-227`, `OI-125`, `OI-71` | EP-2026-08-15-foldforward-closure-read |
| BEN-361 | **THE SAME CONFLATION MADE ONE PREDECLARATION SECTION LOOK REFUTED AND ANOTHER LOOK CONSERVATIVE — one error, two opposite-signed reading failures, and the second is the one that would have been believed.** §6 of the fold-forward predeclaration declared *in advance* that arm 1's scale-only correction would be *"a rescale of order 1%"* because *"this closure's fold-forward ratio is ≈ `1.011418`"*, and therefore that arm 1 would likely be **underpowered by construction**, with *"the honest report a BOUND, not a null."* **That premise sizes the perturbation off the END-OF-RUN ratio, and the correction is applied to the CONSUMED one** (`R/ratio` at the ratio entering each iteration, `closure_foldforward_instrumented.py:141-167`). Measured from the receipts' own `applied_correction_factor`: iteration 1 is `1.046109`, **a 4.61% rescale — `4.04×` the declared amplitude** — and the realized `|Δrecovery| = 0.006888` is `16.2×` the pooled within-arm sd with **disjoint arm ranges** and **9/9** realized pairwise exceedance. **So arm 1 is NOT underpowered and the honest report is a measured effect, not a bound.** **WHY THIS IS WORTH AN ID SEPARATELY FROM `BEN-360`:** a predeclared limitation is the one thing a later reader will not re-derive — it is written to be trusted, its whole value is its timestamp, and *"§6 said this would be underpowered"* is exactly the sentence that survives a relay while its arithmetic does not. A pre-committed *pessimism* is as falsifiable as a pre-committed prediction and gets checked far less often, **because a result that beats its declared limitation looks like good news rather than like a discrepancy.** **RULE: re-derive a predeclaration's own amplitude estimate from the run's realized operands before repeating it, including — especially — when the run cleared it.** **Long form: [FINDING-20260815-a-predeclared-pessimism-is-the-least-checked-claim.md](FINDING-20260815-a-predeclared-pessimism-is-the-least-checked-claim.md).** [receipt](state/RECEIPT-foldforward-instrumented-closure-20260815.json) *Fold-forward closure lane.* | `BEN-360`, `BEN-025`, `BEN-244`, `OI-125` | EP-2026-08-15-foldforward-closure-read |
| BEN-345 | **A GUARD CARRIED "OPEN STRUCTURALLY" FOR THREE ROUNDS BECAUSE ITS PRECONDITION CONTRADICTS THE PRECONDITION FOR RUNNING THE SUITE — the impossibility was a property of the FRAMING, and observing it found four defects in the guard plus four errors in my own account of it.** repair-10 `N6` said, honestly and identically each round: *the guard is inert wherever a writable tmpdir exists, which is the condition this round also had to create in order to execute the suite at all.* True of the process running the suite; **false of a subprocess** — a manufactured `sitecustomize.py` on `PYTHONPATH` breaks temp-directory creation before pytest, before conftest. **RULE: when a guard's precondition contradicts the suite's, the answer is a second process, not a caveat.** **WHAT OBSERVING IT FOUND.** (1) Detection read only the test function's source for `TemporaryDirectory`, so **171 items requesting pytest's `tmp_path`/`tmpdir` were never looked at — and that is the ONLY stratum that ERRORS** (setup phase), which is the failure mode the file exists to suppress; plus **119** building it in `setUp`, **26** calling `mkdtemp`/`NamedTemporaryFile` in the body, **12** one indirection past `setUp`. Derived on one tree from `git show HEAD:…conftest.py`: **157 skipped before, 485 after** of 1465 (6 of the 119 and 1 of the 157 are this change's own probes, so pre-existing exposure was 113/156). (2) **A simulation the code under test can route around is not a simulation** — patching the `mkdtemp` family left all 171 passing, because `tmp_path` calls `gettempdir()` then `os.mkdir`; the patch had to move to the `os` layer. (3) **Being MORE faithful broke the harness, and that is a result**: with `gettempdir` raising, pytest never starts (`_pytest.capture` builds `FDCapture` from `tempfile.TemporaryFile` during `pytest_load_initial_conftests`) — so **the guard's reach is bounded to sandboxes where pytest survives**, and **conftest.py's own account of the historical sandbox cannot be literally true**, since pytest ran there and reported 23 ERRORS. The condition consistent with the history is narrower: `mkdir` under the temp root refused, temp FILE fine. **When a faithful reproduction kills the harness, the recorded description of the original failure is wrong, and the discrepancy tells you what it actually was.** **FOUR CORRECTIONS TO MY OWN WORK, each caught by an assertion rather than by review.** (a) `ERROR` is **not an outcome** — `TestReport.outcome` is `"failed"` for a setup break too and the terminal derives the label from `when != "call"`, so `assertEqual(outcome, "error")` can never pass. (b) pytest runs unittest `setUp` in the **call** phase, so that stratum yields FAILURES — **inverting my ranking of which miss mattered**; I had written that `setUp` was the erroring shape. (c) **`BEN-342` reproduced by the lane that filed it**: mutant `M4` (delete the direct `setUp` check) SURVIVED, because the probe used the *indirection* shape and the depth-1 walk covered it — a fixture degenerate on the axis under test. (d) Chasing `M4` exposed a real over-reach in the fix: the walk read the source of anything `setUp` mentioned, and `setUp`'s first name is `tempfile`, **whose module source contains every API string — so the walk was matching the stdlib, not the helper.** **THE RULE WORTH CARRYING, the negative-side twin of `BEN-344`: a guard gets a test that it FIRES; a FILTER gets a test that it does NOT.** Mutant `M7` (delete the in-module restriction) survived until a probe asserted the imported-helper residual **stays** undetected — the filter was unfalsifiable and widening it later would have looked free. Same for the over-fire boundary: whole-class scanning was the obvious fix and was **measured before rejection at 154 items** whose sibling methods use a tmpdir while they need none, which is the mirror image of the bug. Final: **6/6 mutants caught**, 7 new tests, suite `1461 passed / 3 failed` (all three pre-existing off-cluster path failures; the hook early-returns wherever a tmpdir is writable, so it cannot be implicated). `N6` closed in the tree — **not a verdict**; repair-11 unissued, record still says 7 outstanding, tree says 2 (`N3`,`N4`, both lane A). [full](FINDING-20260816-a-guard-that-could-not-be-observed-where-it-was-needed.md) [receipt](state/RECEIPT-20260816-n6-tmpdir-guard-observed.json) | `BEN-344`, `BEN-342`, `BEN-331`, `BEN-314`, `BEN-228` | EP-2026-08-16-repair10-N6 |
| BEN-346 | **A SPENT AUTHORIZATION READS EXACTLY LIKE A PENDING ONE — and the check I was about to propose for it is FAIL-OPEN, measured 7/17.** A dispatch asked me to confirm/deny re-running arm 1 of the fold-forward closure *"to complete arm 1, which has **never succeeded**"*. **It had succeeded the previous day:** the receipt reads arm 1 = `57038937_3/_4/_5`, three completed draws, all six clearing `CLM-012`, and `sacct` in the mediator's own turn (`BEN-027`) returned `COMPLETED 01:56:59 / 01:57:24 / 01:58:29`, `0:0`. The claim is true of `57012031_3/_4/_5`, the **first** array, and false of the second. **Two lanes stated it, neither checked, a third ran `sacct`.** Cost avoided: **5.9 GPU-h and a duplicate arm-1 population** at a third wrapper digest under one authorization, when `RESULT_2`'s 16.2σ delta already spans two job ids — `4e85f0e`'s own worry one level out. **MECHANISM, and it is not `BEN-300`'s:** an authorization is written to permit work that has not happened; once the work happens **the document does not change**, so its existence is evidence in BOTH directions and reads forever as the prospective one. `AUTHORIZATION-20260815-arm1-resubmit.md` was written *because* a resubmit was authorized, by the same session that later cited its existence as evidence the resubmit was **still pending** — a TIME-DIRECTION error, invisible to re-reading because nothing in the document is wrong. The backward link even existed: the receipt names the authorization **and its blob** (`0fb56af`) in its own `authorizations` array. **THE PART WORTH THE ID: I drafted the obvious remedy — *grep the receipts for the authorization* — and TESTED WHETHER IT GENERALISES BEFORE PROPOSING IT. It does not.** Measured over `docs/orchestration/`: **17** `AUTHORIZATION-*.md`, **7** whose consumption is recorded in ANY of the 274 JSONs (47 carry an `authoriz*` key), **10 for which the grep returns nothing** — and empty would be read as *not yet consumed*, the exact inversion that caused this. A narrower first pass over `state/RECEIPT-*.json` gave **2/17**, so **the answer depends on how hard you looked**, and a remedy with that property is not one. **THE STRUCTURAL FACT: this repo's paper trail is written strictly FORWARD** — proposal → predeclaration → authorization → receipt — **with no backward links. Every artifact names its antecedents; none names its consequences.** So *"has this been acted on?"* is **structurally unanswerable from the document you are holding**, however carefully you read it. That is a property of the convention, not of a lane's diligence. **THE AUTHORITY IS THE SCHEDULER, NOT THE PAPERWORK:** `sacct` over the jobs the authorization describes is the system of record, cannot be fail-open the way a document search can, and cost one command. **Remedies:** run `sacct` before re-issuing, not a grep; treat an authorization as a **single-use token** and append its consumption to **the authorization document**, the only place its reader looks (10/17 record nothing — a convention gap); and **when a dispatch rests on a premise about past state, put the confirmable fact first and make everything downstream conditional on it** — which is what stopped this, the mediator having run the `sacct` before finishing the message. **AND THE NOTE ON MY OWN CONTRIBUTION: I nearly filed a fail-open remedy as the fix for a fail-open reading.** What prevented it was applying `BEN-345`'s rule to a *proposal* rather than to code — **a remedy is a claim and gets tested like one** — which took two minutes and changed the advice from "grep the receipts" to "ask the scheduler", two things that do not fail the same way. *Premise error the mediator's, reported by it unprompted and in full; `sacct` confirmation its own.* [full](FINDING-20260816-a-spent-authorization-reads-as-a-pending-one.md) | `BEN-300`, `BEN-344`, `BEN-315`, `BEN-244`, `BEN-027`, `BEN-345` | EP-2026-08-16-arm1-adjudication |
| BEN-347 | **AN ENVIRONMENT PRESCRIPTION THAT TRAVELS IN A MESSAGE IS THE FAILURE — and the tell that caught it is UNIFORMITY: 10/10 identical failures is a property of the harness, not of ten independently produced files.** Asked whether stage 2's resume would SKIP or re-run the 10 standard-P4 endpoints, I reproduced the launcher's SKIP predicate read-only under the incantation the mediator supplied (`module load tensorflow/2.15.0`) and got **10/10 `WOULD-RERUN: valid_root failed`**. **False, and one message from being escalated as ten corrupt physics products authorising a full re-unfold.** Cause, measured: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ROOT'` — **ROOT is not importable under the TF module** — and my check ended `>/dev/null 2>&1`, so an `ImportError` became a verdict about ten files. Re-run under the launcher's own environment (`setup_salloc_env.sh` → `root_6_28`, **Python 3.11.14 / ROOT 6.28/12**): **10/10 `WOULD-SKIP`**, the opposite answer, with both rejection controls firing in the same run (wrong `--tag`; foreign `--merged`) so the SKIPs are a measurement not a tautology. **THE PRESCRIPTION WAS NOT STALE, IT WAS SCOPED:** the TF module IS correct for what it was given for — the cluster default `python3` is **3.6.15**, where `p4_lib.py:13`'s `from __future__ import annotations` is a `SyntaxError`, so the TF module is what yields an interpreter new enough for the verifier-token check. **This repo needs two environments for two checks and which one you need is not visible from the command you are about to run** — so the defect is not a better incantation. **RULE: `setup_salloc_env.sh` for anything touching ROOT; the TF module only for `p4_lib`-only checks; and any script that cares must SOURCE ITS OWN and PRINT the resolved `python3 -V` and `ROOT.gROOT.GetVersion()` into its output**, so the environment is evidenced in the artifact rather than assumed from the caller's shell — a future reader of the new logs can see which interpreter produced the verdict; a reader of the original could not. **SECOND INSTANCE, SAME DAY, SAME CLASS:** dispatching the run gave `bash: /tmp/p4_runner_laneB.sh: No such file or directory`, `exit code 127` — **`/tmp` is node-local on Perlmutter**; `scp` landed it on the login node and `srun` ran on `nid004254`. Earlier probes worked from `/tmp` only because they never crossed to a compute node: **a location assumption carried from a context where it happened to hold**, the same shape as the ROOT/TF error. That one cost a minute because it was LOUD; the ROOT one cost more because it was SILENT. **AND THE RULE THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT BOTH: never suppress stderr on a check whose failure you intend to interpret** — `2>/dev/null` turned a diagnosable `ModuleNotFoundError` into an undiagnosable physics claim, which is `BEN-026`'s *do not destroy the evidence at write time* applied to a probe instead of a long run. **NO SINGLE LANE'S DILIGENCE WOULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS:** the mediator could not see my `2>/dev/null` and I could not see that its incantation was scoped to a different check — wrong incantation the mediator's (reported unprompted and in full), suppressed stderr and the false result mine. | BEN-380 | **"READ BACK FROM THE ADOPTED PRODUCT" NAMED A FILE WHOSE OWN RECEIPT SAYS `adopts_nothing: true` THREE TIMES — and the phrase is unfalsifiable by every check in this repo, because it is an artifact reference written as a description.** `CRITERIA-20260811` §3 grades the provenance leg MET for quarantine causes 2, 3 and 4 citing values *"read back from the adopted product (job `56695424`)"*. Measured at `00e794e`: that job's artifact is `STAMPTEST2_bkgaware_meancentered.root`, `892170857` bytes, with `test_product_only: true`, `adopts_nothing: true`, `test_product_adopted: false` in its own committed receipt — and **mean-centered only, so the CV-centered arm was covered by nothing at all.** The arms actually named as the adoption candidate are `892170881` / `892232198` bytes from job `56720356`. **The same document had already refused this exact substitution one section earlier** — cause 2's discharge box says *"declaring on A1/A2 while citing stamps verified on a different file is the invented-after-the-fact closure this document exists to prevent"*, and cause 2 was re-cited to regenerated adoption-named arms for that reason. **Causes 3 and 4 were never re-cited, and nothing recorded that they hadn't been**, so `MAP-20260817` inherited the citation five days later and priced two blockers off it. **WHY NO MECHANISM CATCHES THIS AND THAT IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART: a hash binding is checkable, a path is checkable, and "the adopted product" is NEITHER — it is a DEFINITE DESCRIPTION, and a definite description silently re-points when a second object satisfying it comes into existence.** `verify_hash_bindings.py` cannot fire on it; a grep for the sha finds nothing because no sha is written; the sentence stays literally true of the file its author meant and becomes false about the file its reader means. This is `BEN-100`'s one-tally-two-products and `BEN-080`'s `B1` at the level of the *reference* rather than the *identifier*. **Rule: a provenance citation names an artifact by PATH-PLUS-DIGEST or it is not a citation — and when a test product and a production product are produced by the same code within hours, the test product's own `adopts_nothing` flag is evidence ABOUT THE CITATION and should be read as such.** **SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT IN THE SAME CHAIN, and it is `BEN-112` recurring inside the fix for `BEN-112`:** `adopt_unified_5d.py:215-219` fails closed on six keys — three `*_checked` flags and three `TNamed`s — and **the three `upstream_*` VALUE parameters are not among them**, yet `:220-223` prints `provenance stamped AND read back: … upstream={…}` where that dict is the plain-Python capture from `:103-106` and **was never read back out of anything.** The committed `STAMPED_HASH_RECEIPT.slurm-56720356.json` then defers to that log (`verdict: HASHES_COMPLETE_READ_STDOUT_FOR_ARM_VALUES`) and the log lives only on purgeable scratch. **So the chain "launcher pinned → exit 0 → success line" never attested the three numbers the P leg is about**, and `BEN-112`'s own lesson — *a print is not a check* — was reproduced by a repair that verifies six things and prints three it does not. **RESOLVED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY ARGUMENT:** both candidate arms were opened read-only and every stamp read out of the artifact, predeclared branch **S1**, with the two July products carried in the same run as negative controls and required to come back with all nine stamps ABSENT — which they did, so the reader is shown failing in the direction it acts. Receipt `nd-unfolding/uq_5d/receipt_candidate_stamps_5d.json`; ~2 minutes on a login node, no batch job. **AND THE HEADLINE CORRECTION IS THAT THE MISSING THING WAS NEVER AN EDIT:** the map costs these two causes as *"`BEN-106`'s stamp propagation, one edit"*, but that edit landed 2026-08-11 and had been applied on 08-12 — **what was missing was a READ, and six days of two documents pricing the work as pending code is what an uncheckable citation buys.** It closes one leg of four for each cause and **discharges neither** (cause 4's `M` is UNRESOLVED by construction; cause 3's `M` is graded two different ways inside `CRITERIA-20260811` itself). | BEN-381 | **AN ALLOW-LIST KEYED ON `file:line` IS A FALSE-POSITIVE GENERATOR, AND I PROVED IT WITH A COMMENT.** Quarantine cause 1's reintroduction guard, `test_the_only_outer_product_on_X_path_is_the_documented_norm_band`, asserts `found == ["analyze_universes_5d.py:109"]` — an allow-list of permitted `np.outer` sites keyed on **line number**. **Mutation M4: one added comment line, zero semantic change of any kind, turns it red** (`['analyze_universes_5d.py:110'] != ['analyze_universes_5d.py:109']`). **The damage is not the red, it is what the red teaches:** a guard that fires on innocent edits trains its reader to wave it through, and the next red might be an actual one-sided `np.outer` — the guard's entire true-positive case. It also **fails the pre-commit dispatcher's own admitting rule** (*"a check belongs here iff a committer who did nothing wrong can always make it pass"*), so it can never be promoted to a hook as written, which is a silent ceiling on the strongest check cause 1 has. **Fix is one line and is NOT applied: key the allow-list on the enclosing construct or the normalized source text (`np.outer(v, v)` inside the `add_norm` block) rather than on `(module, lineno)`.** **WHY NOT APPLIED, and this is the transferable half: a lane must not both grade a leg and modify the instrument that grades it.** I was assessing cause 1's `T` leg; the guard *is* that leg's evidence; editing it would make my own verdict unfalsifiable by the only instrument that could contradict it. Same separation the mediator invoked when routing causes 3 and 4's `M` judgements to a different lane, and the same one lane C insisted on refusing to verify its own repair-12. **SECOND, SEPARATE DEFECT IN THE SAME ROW'S SUBJECT, and it is the third instance of one class in this campaign: `CRITERIA-20260811` §3 grades cause 1's `C` leg MET citing `(§4.8)`, and §4 of that document runs 4.1 THROUGH 4.7 — there is no §4.8.** The audit it points at is real and is stronger than the criterion demanded (executable `Cause1PathAuditTests`, not prose), **so this is a CITATION defect and not a missing audit** — a distinction that survived only because the search was covering rather than a single grep, and one that would have inverted the finding had I stopped at the dangling pointer. **The recursion is the point: §4.4 of that very document IS the finding *"the only predeclared discharge criterion any of these five causes has is cited by a line number that no longer contains it."*** A document recorded the class and then committed it about its own audit, one section later. **Rule, which generalises past both halves: EVIDENCE MUST BE ADDRESSED BY SOMETHING THAT MOVES WITH IT** — a section number, a line number and a `file:line` allow-list all decay silently under ordinary editing, and each of the three failure modes here (dangling pointer, false-positive guard, `BEN-380`'s definite description) is the same defect in a different notation. Cite by content, by name, or by digest. [full](DETERMINATION-20260817-cause1-census-and-magnitude-measured.md) *Lane E.* | `BEN-380`, `BEN-344`, `BEN-228`, `BEN-103` | EP-2026-08-17-gbdt-blockers |
| BEN-382 | **`git rebase` DOES NOT RUN `pre-commit`, SO THE NEW `Checks:` TRAILER SURVIVES ONTO A TREE THE HOOK NEVER SAW — and the workflow this repo mandates guarantees it happens.** Measured on my own commits within an hour of the trailer's deployment (`4dfeccd`). `e477afb` ran the hook and earned `Checks: 9 passed`. `origin/main` had moved, so I rebased — **which hit a real conflict in `CATALOG.md` that I resolved by hand** — and the result `75fc88d` is a **different tree** (`git diff --stat e477afb 75fc88d` → `CATALOG.md | 2 +-`) **carrying the same `Checks: 9 passed` trailer, with the hook never invoked and no output printed.** Git runs `pre-commit`/`commit-msg` on `git commit`, not on rebase's internal commit path. **The trailer was built precisely because "a hook that never ran leaves a durable absence instead of a missing terminal line" — and this is the inverse and worse case: a durable PRESENCE asserting a check that did not run.** `BEN-084`'s "an artifact asserting the wrong thing beats no artifact for damage", and `BEN-112`'s *a print is not a check* in its most durable form yet, because a commit trailer is permanent and auditable and therefore trusted. **THE EXPOSURE IS NOT AN EDGE CASE: this repo's standing instruction is *"rebase BEFORE measuring anything you will quote, or re-measure after"* and `origin/main` moves constantly with 5+ lanes, so the ordinary path to `main` is commit → rebase → push. Every commit that took that path carries an unearned trailer**, and the conflict case is the dangerous one — a hand-resolved conflict is exactly when the tree diverges most from what was checked. **NO HARM IN THIS INSTANCE, verified rather than assumed: I ran `bash .githooks/pre-commit` directly against `75fc88d` and it printed `pre-commit: 9 checks passed`, exit 0, so my trailer is TRUE — it was just not EARNED by that commit object.** The distinction is the whole finding: truth by luck is not evidence. **Remedies, routed to the trailer's author and not applied by me** (the lane that finds a mechanism's hole is not automatically the one who should redesign it, `BEN-370`'s own closing rule): (i) verify at **push** time rather than commit time, where the tree is final — the only point that is not bypassable by rebase, amend, cherry-pick or squash; (ii) failing that, make the trailer carry the **tree sha it was computed against** (`Checks: 9 passed tree=<sha>`), so a reader can tell in one command whether it applies to the commit it sits on — an unfalsifiable trailer is `BEN-380`'s definite description again, in a third notation; (iii) `git rebase --exec` in the lane workflow is a partial fix and relies on remembering, which is the class the dispatcher header itself calls *"the convention-that-requires-remembering problem"*. **The general rule, and it is the third notation of the same defect this lane has filed today: EVIDENCE MUST BE BOUND TO WHAT IT IS EVIDENCE ABOUT.** A section number, a `file:line` allow-list, a definite description and now a trailer with no tree binding all decay silently the moment the thing they describe moves. [full](FINDING-20260817-a-trailer-that-survives-the-rebase-that-invalidates-it.md) *Lane E.* | `BEN-370`, `BEN-380`, `BEN-381`, `BEN-112`, `BEN-084` | EP-2026-08-17-gbdt-blockers |
| BEN-383 | **A ROUTING MAP'S ONE-LINE SUMMARY OF AN ITEM REPLACED THE ITEM'S QUESTION — and the map is the thing lanes are dispatched from, so the summary is what gets worked on.** `MAP-20260817-pet-critical-path.md:80` states `OI-126` as a question about **SPREAD**: *"is the p∥ 6–20 GeV bootstrap spread (a) this estimator honestly reporting instability, so `C_stat`'s **large bands** are published as-is, or (b) evidence that a Poisson bootstrap of the measured leg is not a valid construction?"* **The `OPEN_ITEMS.md` row states it as a question about CONTAINMENT:** *"the P5A annealed nominal is NOT INSIDE its own 50-replica bootstrap family, and the failure is SPATIALLY ORGANISED"* — perfect behaviour at p∥ < 6 GeV (median z = −0.13), the nominal **exceeding all fifty replicas in 44 of 63 cells** in the 6–20 GeV band, and **the sign REVERSING above 20 GeV** (44 of 45 cells below the family mean), nominal integral at the **98th percentile**. **A family can have honest width and still fail to contain its nominal; a containment failure with a sign flip across a kinematic boundary is evidence of an OFFSET BETWEEN TWO CONSTRUCTIONS, and neither (a) nor (b) is about an offset.** **The map's binary therefore cannot express the row's OWN leading candidate**, which the row states as measured: *"the two arms use different Stay-Positive backends"* — nominal `refinement_backend='precomputed:gate2-published-target'`, replicas **recomputing** it (`refinement_estimator='exact'`, `max_mc_events=200000`) — with other differences recorded RULED OUT. **Bounded rather than asserted:** `grep -in 'backend|stay-positive|containment|outside|percentile'` over the entire map returns **nothing**; it never names the containment failure, the sign flip, the percentile or the candidate. **THE OPERATIONAL COST, which is why this is a finding and not a nitpick: a map exists precisely so a lane can be dispatched without reading every row**, so a lane sent to "settle `OI-126`" reaches for estimator-stability evidence and never learns that the live question is *why the nominal sits outside its own family in one kinematic band*. If the backend candidate holds, the answer is neither branch: `C_stat`'s validity is untouched and only its **pairing with this particular nominal** fails — an outcome the offered binary would have forced into (b), reopening `OI-121`/`OI-122` and **discarding an already-built artifact for the wrong reason.** **Distinct from `BEN-380` and worth separating: there a citation named the wrong OBJECT; here a summary names the right object and the wrong QUESTION about it**, which no digest, path or hash binding can catch because nothing is misidentified. **Rule: a routing document may compress an item's ANSWER but never its QUESTION — and the cheap check is that the summary's branch set must be able to express the row's own leading candidate. If it cannot, the summary is a different item.** Not adjudicated: I did not re-derive any of the row's numbers and take no position on the backend.
