| BEN-384 | **AN ITEM'S COST IS A PROPERTY OF WHERE ITS CODE LIVES, NOT OF THE DIFF — a "one key and two comparisons" fix turned out to require a GATE-2 RE-RUN, and I only learned it by writing the diff and watching a gate go red.** `OI-60`'s next-action prices its fix as one telemetry key plus two array comparisons. I wrote all of it — loader export, target-stage compare, training-stage compare, the `:215` comment corrected from "three" to actually three, a scope list replacing a bare `canonical_replay_verified: True`, and a three-case test power-tested by disabling the production check (`DID NOT RAISE`, then 6 passed). **Then `verify_hash_bindings.py` went `ALL BINDINGS INTACT` → `*** BINDINGS BROKEN ***` on the LOADER EDIT ALONE:** `fullevent_fps_dataloader.py` is pinned at `e1402370…` both as `EXPECTED_LOADER_SHA` in `run_gate2_target_validator.sh:49` and as `/code/loader/sha256` in `G2_GATE2_TARGET_RUNTIME_RECEIPT.json`. **The validator's own header had already foreclosed the escape, about exactly this class of change:** *"Rather than argue the change was semantically inert for the negweight-refined path — **which is exactly the reasoning hash pins exist to reject** — the gate is re-run"*, recording the repo re-running rather than re-digesting on 2026-08-04 **and again** on 2026-08-05, with bit-identical weights required. **Adding a telemetry key no nominal-path code reads IS the semantically-inert argument**, so a repin would be both prohibited to this lane and substantively wrong — it would make a Gate-2 receipt assert that a loader which did not produce the archived target did produce it. **ALL OF IT REVERTED; nothing committed.** **THE GENERALISABLE PART: an open-item's cost estimate is written by whoever understands the DEFECT, and the binding cost is usually imposed by whoever pinned the FILE — two different people, at two different times, neither reading the other.** Every enumeration of "items blocked on nothing" is therefore optimistic by however many of them touch a pinned file, and **the cheapest way to find out is not to read the item, it is to write the diff and run the gate** — the pin is invisible from the row and from the code, and visible in one command. **Rule: before costing a code item as free, run `verify_hash_bindings.py` against the edit you intend, not against the tree you have.** Corollary, learned the same turn: **only ONE of the three files I touched was enforced-pinned**, so "is this area pinned?" has no answer — the question is per-file. Re-cost recorded on the row: one key, two comparisons, **and a ~~GPU~~ Gate-2 re-run**, weighed against a residual the row itself calls narrow. Whether that trade is worth making is the owner's call and is not taken. **CORRECTED IN PLACE 2026-08-17 BY ITS OWN AUTHOR, twice, and the second correction inverts the finding's own conclusion: (1) `GPU` is WRONG — Gate-2 is a CPU job (`--constraint=cpu`; four runs, all CPU partitions, zero `gres/gpu`) costing 0.41 CPU node-hours and ZERO A100-hours; (2) the Gate-2 re-run is the CHEAPEST blocker, not the blocker — the loader digest is compared at ELEVEN sites of which this gate sees TWO, and the one that prices the item is `reconcile_gate5_family.py`'s family-constant, which routes to a 151.175 A100-hour Gate-5 re-run. So "run `verify_hash_bindings.py` against the edit you intend" — this row's own rule — is NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT, and I wrote it believing it was sufficient.** See `BEN-385`. [full](DETERMINATION-20260817-pet-oi82-oi60-oi41.md), [cost + the eleven sites](DETERMINATION-20260817-oi60-rerun-costed.md) *Lane E.* | `BEN-383`, `BEN-077`, `BEN-084` | EP-2026-08-17-pet-items |
| BEN-385 | **A GATE THAT SEES TWO OF ELEVEN PINS IS NOT A PIN CHECK, IT IS A SAMPLE — and I built a whole sweep methodology on it yesterday and called the negatives measurements.** `BEN-384` prescribed *"run `verify_hash_bindings.py` against the edit you intend"* and I ran it as a **binary** over the files each row happened to name. Extending it broke it in three places at once. **(1) A CORRECT MEASUREMENT OF THE WRONG FILE.** For C's `OI-64` the first pass probed `check_canonical_designation.py` and reported it clean; that item's edit sites are `verify_executing_copy_is_committed.py` and the two places that must CALL it — `reconcile_gate5_family.py` and `sbatch_gate5_replica_train_array.sh` — and **both callers are PINNED**. The item whose entire diagnosis is *"an unwired check is a check nobody runs"* was costed on the one file whose wiring does not matter. **(2) "PINNED / NOT PINNED" IS NOT THE STATE SPACE.** `verify_hash_bindings.py` TOLERATES drift on four files (`KNOWN_PREEXISTING`), which are pinned and leave the gate **green** — a state the binary cannot express and which a lane would read as permission to edit. **(3) THE GATE CANNOT SEE MOST PINS, MEASURED ON A SPECIFIC ITEM FOR THE FIRST TIME (`BEN-322` costed).** `OI-60`'s loader digest is compared at **eleven code sites and the gate resolves two**; six of the nine invisible ones `die … 3` **at Slurm task start** — green at commit, dead hours later after the queue wait. **THE FIX IS AN INVERSION, AND IT IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART: SEARCH BY CONTENT DIGEST, NOT BY PATH.** A pin *is* a digest, so `git grep <sha256 of the file>` finds every site that has frozen that content, including the role-keyed receipts and hardcoded launcher maps no path-side collector can resolve. That single inversion found `build_fullevent_replica_target.py` — clean on the gate, digest in 5 receipts including a live comparator — which the first pass had cleared. **AND THE NEW INSTRUMENT'S OWN BLIND SPOT IS EXHIBITED RATHER THAN CAVEATED, because a caveat is what I wrote last time and it did not stop me:** a file that has ALREADY drifted has its OLD content recorded, so a digest search on its CURRENT content returns nothing and it reads clean while being pinned — `wakerctl.py` and `test_wakerctl.py`, `in_inventory=True`, `digest_sites=0`, printed by the run itself. **METHOD, and it is what makes the negatives mean anything: three instruments, each answering one question, with the cheap complete one VALIDATED BY THE EXPENSIVE EXACT ONE.** Instrument 1 imports the gate's own collectors and enumerates all **173** bound files tree-wide (169 + 4 tolerated); instrument 2 is the digest search; instrument 3 is the original perturbation probe, **demoted from measurement to VALIDATOR** — instrument 1 predicts each outcome in advance and the probe checks it, **19/19, zero disagreements**, including both tolerated-drift files predicted GREEN *because* pinned-and-drifted. **A prediction printed beside an observation is not a test; this one fails loudly if instrument 1 is wrong.** **Rule: before costing a code item, ask which of the file's DIGESTS are written down, not whether the path appears in a pin list — and never report a pin question as a binary, because "pinned but tolerated" and "bound by a comparator the checker cannot see" are both edits that look free and are not.** Net effect on the ten-item list: `OI-64C` and `OI-65C` move from cheap to blocked, `OI-61` SPLITS (one half routable, one not), `OI-58` is confirmed cheap on two independent instruments, and `OI-96`/`OI-12`/A's `OI-64`/`OI-65` stay clean. [full](SWEEP-20260817-pet-pin-exposure-complete.md), [the eleven sites and their cost](DETERMINATION-20260817-oi60-rerun-costed.md) *Lane E.* | `BEN-384`, `BEN-322`, `BEN-380`, `BEN-344`, `BEN-077` | EP-2026-08-17-pet-items |
| BEN-386 | **THE FILE AN EDIT LIVES IN IS NOT THE FILE THAT VALIDATES IT — so a pin sweep, however complete, cannot grade an item routable. Third correction of my own instrument in two days, and this one was found by being AUTHORIZED to do the work rather than by more measuring.** `SWEEP-20260817` graded `OI-61(b)` — *"pass a replica-specific tag"* — **routable to the unpinned side**, on good evidence: the row names `train_fullevent_replica.py` as the edit site, and that file is clean on **both** independent instruments (no binding `verify_hash_bindings.py` resolves, zero digest sites tree-wide). The mediator authorized it on exactly that basis. **It is wrong, and argparse says so in one command.** `train_fullevent_replica.py` calls `nominal.main([… "--tag", "nominal" …])` while `train_fullevent_nominal.py:325` **DECLARES THE TAG'S DOMAIN** — `choices=["nominal", "floor"]` — so **the one-line change in the unpinned CALLER is rejected by the pinned CALLEE:** `--tag replica_07` → *"invalid choice: 'replica_07'"*, **exit 2**, measured on this checkout before any diff was written. **THE PROBE HAD TO CHANGE SHAPE TO SEE IT, and that is the transferable half.** `BEN-384`'s probe appends a comment and asks the gate; a comment cannot distinguish *the edit that WORKS* from *the edit that merely COMMITS*, and here those are in different files with different pin status. `state/probe-oi61b-tag-route-20260817.py` applies the **REAL MINIMAL DIFF** for each candidate instead — caller edit → gate **green** *and non-functional*; driver edit → gate **RED**; 2/2 against predictions declared in the script, restored byte-exactly, gate `INTACT` after. **SECOND-ORDER COST IS `OI-60`'S, so the two items are one item for scheduling:** `validate_gate5_training_artifacts.py:189-191` compares every replica receipt's recorded code digest against `EXPECTED_CODE`, whose key for this file is — with no irony intended by its author — **`nominal_driver_unmodified`**. The fix falsifies the name of the constant that pins it and invalidates the archived 50 exactly as a loader edit does. **So `OI-61` DOES NOT SPLIT; both halves ride whatever re-issue `OI-60` rides.** **THE LIMIT THIS PUTS ON THE WHOLE SWEEP METHOD, stated plainly because it bounds a document three lanes are now routed to:** instruments 1 and 2 answer *"is this FILE frozen?"*. **Neither answers *"does this EDIT work?"*** — a question that reaches a second file through an argument contract, an import, a schema, or a declared enum, **none of which is a hash and none of which any digest search can see.** A pin sweep can therefore tell you an item is expensive and can NEVER tell you it is cheap. **Rule: probe the diff that ACHIEVES THE GOAL, not the diff the row DESCRIBES — they coincide only when nobody has checked. The obvious misreading of a "two sessions agree" rule is that agreement is the goal**; `CLAIMS.md` already refuses *worker agreement is not verification* for physics, and the control plane gets the same refusal. **Two of the four instances re-derived here (the sha with a control, the documents/code one via `BEN-391`); the two GPU-h transports are RELAYED AND NOT RE-DERIVED, marked per instance.** [full](FINDING-20260817-unqualified-transport-of-good-measurement.md) *Seconding lane, filing for the mediator, which reported its own four.* **GENERALISED BY LANE B AT `BEN-248` and cross-referenced rather than restated: the receipt-ingredients principle has been useful in FOUR registers today — operands of a number, scope of a grade, units of a ratio, and provenance of a claim — which argues the rule was never about numbers at all: SHIP WHAT WOULD LET A READER FALSIFY THIS.** A better statement of `BEN-077` than `BEN-077` makes, and this row is an instance of it rather than its source. | BEN-393 | **A CORRECTION SITE INHERITS THE TRUST OF THE INDEX AND NONE OF ITS SCRUTINY.** I added a row to `INDEX-retracted-and-superseded-values.md` to stop `28.50 A100-h` propagating, and its *what supersedes it* column said **`39.078` bare — the GPU column ONLY.** One seed across all four blocks is `39.223` A100-h **plus `55.337` CPU task-hours (`2,764.7` CPU-core-h)**, the CPU half is the LARGER one, and it is denominated in a unit the `24 A100-h` grant does not reach. **So the mechanism built to stop one unqualified number propagating was propagating the successor's scoping defect instead — from the file whose own header says *READ THIS AT WRITE TIME* and calls itself a checklist for writers.** **THREE PROPERTIES MAKE THIS STRUCTURAL, none specific to this instance: (i) an index is consulted at WRITE time by its own instruction, so its errors propagate FORWARD into documents that do not exist yet, stamped as the corrected value; (ii) consulting it IS the diligence step, so it satisfies the reader's stopping condition** — `BEN-247`'s mechanism and `BEN-390`'s, arriving in the correction layer; **(iii) the author is the worst-placed party to re-scope the successor, because a correction is written in the moment of being convinced the OLD value is wrong and all the attention is on the delta.** **AND NOTHING CHECKS A CORRECTION** — the row lint checks pointers, `verify_hash_bindings.py` checks digests, the hook runs nine checks, and **none asks whether a superseding value is as well-qualified as the value it replaces.** Verification here is aimed at first-order claims; a correction is second-order. **RULE: a supersession entry gets the same scrutiny as the value it replaces — record the successor's scope, its units, what it is NOT, and NAME ITS NEAR-COINCIDENT SIBLING in the same cell.** That last clause is what this needed: `39.078` (`C_syst`) and `39.223` (all four blocks) **differ by `0.37 %` because `C_syst` is `99.63 %` of the GPU column**, so the hazard is not a misread figure but **the two becoming interchangeable** — the index's own *agreed-at-the-printed-precision* trap, demonstrated inside the index. **NOT WRITTEN AS A CHECK, deliberately:** linting the column for a bare number would fire on every legitimately unitless superseded value (a sha256, a verdict string, a count), and `BEN-381`/`BEN-390` are the precedent for refusing that trade — a check that fires on the healthy case gets switched off. **The available check is human and has a definite answer: for each superseding value ask *what unit is this in, and is there a second unit?*** **COMPOSITE ARM = `39.078` A100-h + `55.182` CPU task-h, and the code REFUSES reuse rather than discouraging it** — traced by B, verified here: `args.seed` fans out at `unified_throw_cov.py:244,281,297`, is stamped per slab at `:254,285,302`, and `:417-419` is a hard `raise SystemExit(... refusing mixed-seed combine)`. So the composite arm's defining move is the leg carrying essentially the whole CPU bill, and a GPU-only figure fails HARDEST where it was quoted. [full](FINDING-20260817-a-correction-site-inherits-the-index-trust.md) *Seconding lane; caught by lane B reading my correction, and extracted into its own row on B's argument that a reader looking for how retractions fail will not find it filed under a transport defect.* **The general form is lane B's `BEN-248`: SHIP WHAT WOULD LET A READER FALSIFY THIS** — a supersession entry that omits the successor's units is that rule broken in the fourth register, provenance/scope of a claim rather than operands of a number. | `BEN-247`, `BEN-248`, `BEN-302`, `BEN-381`, `BEN-390`, `BEN-392` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-394 | **AN UNLOCK TRIGGER WRITTEN TO REMOVE JUDGEMENT IS A STORED MEASUREMENT, AND THE COMMIT THAT SATISFIES IT CAN BE THE COMMIT THAT BREAKS IT.** `.githooks/pre-commit:47-49`: *"when `python3 nd-unfolding/pet/check_canonical_designation.py` exits 0 on a clean tree, move this to the run list as check 7. **No other condition, no judgement call — run the command.**"* Reported to me as fired. **MEASURED, both sides named: `PASS` / exit 0 at `8a34f12^`, `FAIL` / exit 1 at HEAD `a05feee`**, on two `UNACCOUNTED FILE`s — `docs/orchestration/state/regen_canonical_namespace_field_pins.py` (`A`) and `nd-unfolding/tests/test_hash_bindings.py` (`M`) — **both from `8a34f12`, the reporting lane's own commit, announced in the same message.** The parent passing is what makes it causal rather than correlational. **So the report was TRUE WHEN MEASURED AND FALSE WHEN SENT, falsified by one commit, its own** — `BEN-228`'s shape in the one place designed to need no judgement. **FOUR PROPERTIES, none specific to this trigger: (i) a trigger is a stored measurement wearing the grammar of a rule** — *"run the command"* reads as having a stable answer, and the answer is a property of a tree that five lanes push to; **(ii) the predicate is TREE-GLOBAL, so any lane can flip it and none is told** — a test mentioning the namespace flips a latch in a hook header its author has never read, with no observer on either transition; **(iii) a trigger written to remove judgement is the one place nobody re-derives the property** (lane E's formulation, and the load-bearing one — the instruction's promise is that you need not think, and its failure mode is that nobody does); **(iv) the instrument is COUPLED TO THE CORPUS IT MEASURES, including its own documentation** — E measured the digest-site count going `24 → 25` because the determination it wrote quotes the digest in its own table, so **writing about a pin increments its count** and no stored value can be stable. **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT REASON NOT TO WIRE IT, lane E's, WHICH SURVIVES A GREEN:** the trigger tests *exits 0 today*, while the dispatcher's own admitting rule at `:11` (lane D, `OI-64`) is *a check belongs here iff a committer who did nothing wrong can always make it pass* — and `check_canonical_designation.py` **reddens when a committer deletes a prose sentence** (`BEN-387`). **The two fail DIFFERENTLY: mine says the precondition is not met, E's says it is the wrong precondition, and fixing the tree answers only mine.** **NOT WIRED, and not this lane's call:** dispatcher changes go through an `AUTHORIZATION-*` document (two precedents, one of which records that *being overruled on the design is not the same as being authorized on it*), and **`966d202` already declined this exact wiring** — a peer routing a decision to another lane does not create authority for it. **ROUTED TO JOSEPH with the recommendation stated so it can be overruled: do not wire it, and REPLACE the trigger rather than wait for it to go green — a latch on a tree-global measurement with no observer will be stale whenever it is next read. If a trigger is kept it needs the TREE SHA it was measured against**, which is `BEN-382`'s remedy for the `Checks:` trailer and the third notation of *evidence must be bound to what it is evidence about*. **RESOLVED SAME DAY, and the resolution adds three facts the row was filed without. (1) THE COMMIT THAT BROKE THE TRIGGER IS THE `OI-96` FIX ITSELF** — the pin generator and the tests that power-test it both mention the protected namespace in code, so **the remedy for the item falsified the trigger being reported on, in the same hour, and both were announced in one message.** Causally linked and still not connected, because the trigger was measured BEFORE the fix was written and never re-measured after: worse than staleness, and better as evidence. **(2) A COMMITTED ASSERTION SHIPPED ON A TREE WHERE IT WAS FALSE** — `be06d45` added `*** THE TRIGGER FIRED ON 2026-08-17 AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT HONOURED. DO NOT WIRE IT. ***` to the hook header, verified here, at exit 1. **That is `BEN-382`'s durable-PRESENCE defect in a third artifact — a hook comment — and the `DO NOT WIRE IT` is what kept it harmless, i.e. the safe outcome came from the conclusion being right rather than from the premise being true.** **(3) RESTORED to exit 0** at `890fa0f`, verified here — `PASS`, 33 of 294 in-code occurrences dispositioned — via **five line-level `# NS-EXEMPT` markers** (1 in `regen_canonical_namespace_field_pins.py`, 4 in `test_hash_bindings.py`, counted at HEAD), the lighter of the two sanctioned remedies; it leaves lane C's inventory untouched and **the guard PRINTS every exemption on each run**, so the narrowing is visible in output rather than only in a doc. **AND LANE E'S ADDITION TO THE REMEDY, which is better than mine: bind a trigger to the sha AND STATE WHAT WOULD INVALIDATE IT** — this one was invalidated by a file *merely MENTIONING a string*, which no reader of *"exits 0 on a clean tree"* would predict, so a sha alone tells you the reading is old and not what kind of edit ages it. **~~AND E'S REFRAMING: `966d202` had already DECLINED this wiring, so the trigger is a RE-LITIGATION LATCH ON A DECISION ALREADY MADE.~~ WITHDRAWN 2026-08-17 BY ITS AUTHOR (lane E, `e70f864`) AND VERIFIED WITHDRAWN HERE BY READING `966d202`'s BODY, which I had built into this row on a peer's one-line summary — the exact failure the row above it describes.** `966d202` declined on **exactly one ground, and that ground is TREE STATE**: *"BUT IT CANNOT BE PASSED BY A COMMITTER WHO DID NOTHING WRONG … EXIT 1 — 8 unaccounted files, 1 COUNT DRIFT, 2 stale inventory entries … would block every lane on a red none of them caused"* — and it recorded **the OPPOSITE on substance**: *"ON SUBSTANCE IT BELONGS IN THE RUN LIST … this is a regression in the tree, not a check that never worked."* **So the trigger is not a latch somebody left behind; it is the HONEST REMEDY OF A CONDITIONAL DECLINE** — tree red, no; tree green, yes — faithful to its own reasoning, and `966d202` was right on the evidence it had. **THE CORRECT ARGUMENT IS STRONGER AND REPLACES IT: `BEN-387` FALSIFIED THE PREMISE, NOT THE MEASUREMENT.** The enforcement is a whole-file occurrence count and it is **INVERTED**, so it reddens on innocent edits **BY CONSTRUCTION, independently of tree cleanliness** — so the check is *not* "sound, with a dirty tree", which is precisely what `966d202` believed, and **NO GREEN TREE LICENSES WIRING IT. That RETIRES the trigger rather than postponing it**, and needs no claim that anyone was re-litigating anything. (Also from `966d202`, and it closes the obvious escape: *"it cannot be wired as an advisory"* — `run()` discards a passing check's output, so the dispatcher has exactly two channels, silence and failure (`BEN-226`), and the choice really is binary.) **AND MY OWN DECLINE COUNT WAS WRONG: not three declines — TWO unconditional (the mediator as hook owner, and this lane) plus ONE CONDITIONAL that endorsed it on substance.** The conditional one is now retired by `BEN-387` rather than satisfied. **None of them is authorization to wire, which remains the only direction that would need one.** [full](FINDING-20260817-a-trigger-is-a-stored-measurement.md) *Seconding lane; the trigger's state measured here with a parent-commit control, not accepted from the message that reported it. Amended after lane E reproduced the flip independently and fixed it — every figure in the amendment re-measured at `890fa0f`, including E's marker count.* | `BEN-228`, `BEN-370`, `BEN-382`, `BEN-387`, `BEN-393` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-395 | **A COLUMN NAMED `lane/owner` WHOSE CELLS ARE MOSTLY NOT OWNERS — SO AN UNOWNED ROW IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM AN OWNED ONE, AND THE ROW IS WHAT A LANE IS DISPATCHED FROM.** Asked to establish which lane `OI-58`'s row routes its hop-1 fix to, **by reading the row rather than inferring from activity.** Answer: **it routes it to NOBODY.** `docs/OPEN_ITEMS.md:61`'s third column is `lane/owner`; `OI-58`'s cell is **`PET / Gate 5 quoting`** — an area and a topic, no lane, no person. **MEASURED ACROSS THE WHOLE TABLE, because one row is an anecdote: of 93 `OI-*` rows, `65` fill `lane/owner` with an AREA ONLY** (`PET diagnostics`, `storage`, `repo infrastructure`), **25 name a lane or person, 2 say `unowned`, 1 is closed.** So **70 % of the table cannot be routed from its own row**, and `OI-58` is not specially orphaned — it is typical. **THE PREDICTED OUTCOME WAS *"the row names a lane that is no longer a live session"*; the actual one is worse and cheaper to fix: THE FIELD NAMES NO LANE AT ALL, and a populated cell in a column called `lane/owner` READS AS OWNERSHIP.** **`BEN-247`'s mechanism in the control plane: a non-empty cell satisfies the check that an empty one would have forced** — an obviously blank owner sends you looking; `PET / Gate 5 quoting` does not. **AND THE VOCABULARY FOR ALL THREE STATES ALREADY EXISTS IN THIS TABLE and is used inconsistently:** a lane (`C (PET)`, `lane D`, `peer session B`), explicitly none (`OI-130`: *"analysis-note evidence / unowned"*), and the newest rows even split **subject from filer** (`OI-124`: *"lane D (subject — probe owner); verdict-repair lane (filing)"*). So this is a **convention that exists and is not applied**, not one that needs inventing — and `OI-130`'s `unowned` is what makes `OI-58`'s cell *distinguishable from* a deliberate non-assignment rather than merely ambiguous. **COST, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED: lane E declined the best-evidenced cheap item on the PET list because *"its row routes it to another lane"* — the row does not, and E was right to decline on a premise the row does not support**; the mediator then left it ambient for a session. **A correct outcome from a false premise, twice in one day** (`BEN-394`'s hook comment is the other). **`%an` IS NOT A LANE FIELD either** — some commits carry `Lane C (PET)`, others carry `Joseph Bailey` with the lane self-declared in the commit BODY — **so neither the ledger's owner column nor git metadata answers *whose is this*, and the only reliable source is a `SCOPE:` line somebody chose to write.** **REPORT SHIPPED, NOT A GATE: `oi_owner_report.py` classifies every row and lists the 65** — and it **must not become a hook check** until the backlog is dispositioned, because it fails on 65 pre-existing rows and a committer editing an unrelated row could not make it pass, violating the dispatcher's own admitting rule at `.githooks/pre-commit:11`. **That constraint is stated in the script rather than left for whoever tries.** **`OI-58` NOT TAKEN** — a row that assigns nobody is not thereby assigned to the reader, and self-assignment on the Gate-5 replica path is the move a routing question exists to prevent. Reported to the mediator for routing or an explicit `unowned`. [full](FINDING-20260817-a-lane-owner-column-that-names-no-lane.md) *Seconding lane, answering a routing request; the 65/93 split measured with a committed script, and the `OI-58` determination made by reading the row rather than from who has been active near it.* | `BEN-244`, `BEN-247`, `BEN-303`, `BEN-380`, `BEN-394` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-396 | **VERIFICATION EFFORT IS ALLOCATED BY SUSPICION, AND SUSPICION TRACKS DISAGREEMENT RATHER THAN FRAGILITY — so the claims that go unchecked are the ones ARRIVING AS SUPPORT.** Every defect caught today was a claim someone was sceptical of — a cost that looked too cheap, an absence too clean, a seed map too convenient. **The ones missed were agreeing with their user at the moment of use, and a supporting citation is MORE dangerous than a contested number because nobody downstream has a reason to check it: it is doing rhetorical work, so it reads as settled.** **INSTANCE 1, verified here:** *"`OPEN_ITEMS` item 6"* cited as recording recoil non-transferability, inside a declaration whose whole purpose was outside verification — but `docs/OPEN_ITEMS.md:74` is **`OI-6`, the standard-P4 purity decision**, and a covering `git grep -niE 'transferab'` over the live file returns **ZERO**; the real homes are `OI-3` at `:71` and the original phrasing **ARCHIVED** at `OPEN_ITEMS-ARCHIVE-2026-08.md:834` (caught by lane B). **INSTANCE 3, verified here:** the `2.01` quoted at `nd-unfolding/HIGHER_DIM_OMNIFOLD_DESIGN.md` — **that path does not exist**; it is `docs/HIGHER_DIM_OMNIFOLD_DESIGN.md:153-155` (caught by lane C, which read it). **INSTANCE 2 relayed, not re-derived here:** a digest count folded into a three-way *family* and relayed twice including to Joseph without being verified — two verified legs, one relayed leg, **assembled while narrating seven transport errors** (caught by lane D declining to sign the generalisation). **INSTANCE 4 IS THE ONE THAT MAKES THIS FILABLE, because it is the same lane ONE TURN AFTER NAMING THE SPECIES:** it reported *"gate 1 is two modules, not four legs"* — true — and omitted that under (B) all four legs must be seed-variable **SIMULTANEOUSLY**, so partial capability buys nothing toward `M(ii)` and **`sweep_bank_5d.py:252` is THE blocking dependency, not one of two parallel edits.** Gate 1 is **smaller in module count and MORE SERIAL in sequencing**, and only the flattering half was reported. **Verified here: `:252` reads `measured_weights=measured_weights, seed=42, verbose=False)` — the estimator seed is a HARDCODED LITERAL at that call site**, so the claim rests on the line and not on the argument. **Its own account is the load-bearing part: it did not suppress the costly half, it SIMPLY DID NOT LOOK FOR IT** — sceptical of another lane's cheap option, unsceptical of its own expensive one's price. **THEREFORE THE CONCLUSION IS NOT *be even-handed*: NAMING THE BIAS DOES NOT FIX IT, and that is MEASURED rather than asserted — the party who named the species committed it in the next turn.** So the remedy cannot be awareness. **TWO MECHANICAL CORRECTIVES: (i) RESOLVE EVERY CITATION YOU REPEAT, including the ones that agree with you and especially those arriving as support** — *"resolving it was one command, the same command run a dozen times that day on other people's numbers"*, so the capability was present, in use, and aimed outward; **(ii) WHEN YOU ADVOCATE A POSITION, STATE ITS COST THE WAY AN OPPONENT WOULD** — enumerate what it makes harder, slower or more serial, before sending. **AND A DISCOUNT THAT BELONGS BESIDE A DISSENT COUNT RATHER THAN UNDER IT: all three of that lane's sustained dissents were arguments AGAINST SPENDING**, so its scepticism and its conclusions pointed one way and none of the three tested whether it had priced its own alternatives honestly — *"three sustained dissents"* reads as a clean instrument and **the instrument has a known bias in a known direction. The same discount applies harder to the mediator by its own account: eight refusals to spend, two authorizations — and `OI-61(b)`, one of the two, produced `BEN-386`, the day's sharpest result. The one time it spent rather than refused went the other way, and the eight refusals are untested.** **A FRAMING FILED AND REJECTED, because it is the natural excuse for this species and was offered by the party with four instances of its own:** *"the slip was in a citation you INHERITED, not in anything you derived"* — **that describes the ORIGIN, not the RESPONSIBILITY, and the last link is still a link.** **STANDS ALONE rather than merging into `BEN-392`: that row says the qualifier gets dropped in transport, this says WHICH claims get it dropped, so it supplies the SELECTION and its remedy is an ALLOCATION rule** — which claims to spend a check on, answer being the ones you already believe. **THE MATCHED PAIR OF OBLIGATIONS, one clause each and neither substituting for the other: the RELAYER carries WHERE a claim came from** (so a receiver can tell corroboration from echo — lane B's row, `BEN-248`, offered to this block and DECLINED because the instance is B's and this lane was not party to the relay) **and the REPORTER volunteers WHAT IT DID NOT READ**, demonstrated: the clause *"`EXTENT-20260817` exists on main and names this launcher; I have not read it"* is the entire reason B could tell an independent second derivation from an echo. **CREDIT KEPT OUT OF THE DECLARATION THAT WOULD HAVE ABSORBED IT, both lane B's: `grep -ci pet` over `sweep_bank_5d.py` returning `0` (verified here) — a DIFFERENT instrument, not a re-run — and pinning `DETERMINATION` §7 to `:233-237` where a section had been cited.** **ATTRIBUTION RESOLVED 2026-08-18: the formulation is the review/second-key lane's (`Assistant [28640e]`), named at its request against a checkable list it published of its own work. AND THE ROUTE IS A CLEANER INSTANCE OF THIS ROW'S SUBJECT THAN THE UNRESOLVABLE NAME IT REPLACES: it sent the formulation to the mediator, which replied *"let me state it in the first person rather than relay it in the third"* and restated it as its own — CORRECTLY, as an admission about its own day — and it reached this lane originless. **A formulation that is ADOPTED rather than QUOTED loses its origin AT THE POINT OF ADOPTION.** The remedy is not *attribute carefully*; nobody was careless. It is: **when you take another party's formulation into the first person, name whose it was in the same sentence, because adoption is the step that erases it.** The honest move and the erasing move were the same move.** [full](FINDING-20260817-verification-allocated-by-suspicion.md) *Seconding lane. **The species and its formulation are a peer session's, which the mediator names "Assistant" — NOT this lane, and I cannot resolve which session it is; the same name has been used for at least two sessions today. So the formulation is credited to a party I cannot name, which is this row's own defect: a claim arrived without its route.** Instances 1 and 3 and the `:252` literal re-derived here; 2 relayed and marked.* | `BEN-077`, `BEN-312`, `BEN-380`, `BEN-386`, `BEN-391`, `BEN-392` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-397 | **THE RECONCILIATION WAS IN THE RECEIPT, AND THREE LANES REASONED ABOUT THE GAP WITHOUT OPENING IT.** `56863958` (`g6_floor`, Gate-6 across-process floor) declares **`N=5`** with **four** payloads on disk. Two lanes worked the *draw 1 was lost, possibly SELECTIVELY* reading — which would truncate the sample by value and bias `OI-126` toward (a). A third lane measured `sacct`, found the array range was **`[2-5]`** with no task 0 or 1, and concluded *"draw 1 was never submitted, so the sample is 4 by construction."* **VERIFIED HERE FROM THE TRACKED RECEIPT** (`state/gate6-floor-replication-active-56863958.json`): it carries `"array_tasks": "2-5"` **and a two-part inventory** — `draw_1` is an **`EXISTING … member_1 artifact, reused unmodified, NOT retrained`** with `v_at_iteration_2 = 0.9806897311812962` and `d = 0.01931026881870379` recorded, beside `draws_2_to_5` = *"four new trainings, identical policy, varying only process/node/GPU"*. **So `N=5` is 1 reused + 4 new, the array is `2-5` because only four needed running, and `products = N-1` is the DESIGNED state rather than an anomaly.** **THE (a)-BIAS WORRY IS CLOSED, AND `n=5` IS THE ANSWER — WHICH IS WHERE THIS ROW STARTED. IT TOOK FOUR REVISIONS BY THREE PARTIES IN THIRTEEN MINUTES TO RETURN TO ITS FIRST POSITION, AND THAT ROUND TRIP IS THE ROW'S MOST USEFUL CONTENT.** **SETTLED, by lane C's own reversal (`fc8a949`, §2(e)): *"`member_1` IS a legitimate fifth draw. I ruled the other way and I was wrong … `n=5` is correct"*, disposition *"include draw 1, report `n=5`"* — and C credits this row as having ruled for inclusion and been right.** **C'S GROUND IS BETTER THAN ANY OF THE THREE WE CYCLED THROUGH, because it argues from the ESTIMAND: *membership in the process-noise population is determined by the EXECUTION'S CONFIGURATION, not by which leg launched it or whether it was retrained FOR THIS LEG* — so *"reused unmodified, NOT retrained"* is **bookkeeping about the leg, not provenance about the artifact**, and excluding on it would make the population depend on a job's LABEL rather than its CONFIGURATION.** **AND THE STRUCTURAL FACT THAT ENDS IT, verified here in the probe rather than taken from the message: `probe-oi120a-csyst-k-20260814.py:16` lists `("draw1_member1", ".../fullevent_ml_ensemble/member_1/…")` as `FILES[0]`, CO-EQUAL with draws 2-5 — so the committed floor of record ALREADY includes it and the tail probe's `n=4` is the deviation — and `:37` takes the reporting domain from `masks[tags[0]]`, i.e. **member_1's OWN mask**. So dropping draw 1 changes `n` AND changes which artifact supplies the domain: *`n=4` vs `n=5` was never a sample-size choice.* That also closes the `259`/`257` mask question — the domain axis and the `n` axis are THE SAME CHANGE, which is why no sample-size framing came out cleanly for anyone.** **CARRIED FORWARD, because C's disposition is conditional and a bare *`n=5`* would overstate it: membership rests on an UNEVALUATED premise — the clause-8 check (whether draw 1 is drawn from a different process population at identical configuration) is one command that has never been run, so under `CRITERIA-20260811` §0 it is *currently unmeasured*, and *"it barely matters"* is a reason not to spend long on the check rather than a reason to skip it.** *(And do NOT quote `9.3440 %` against `8.7302 %` as a clean pair — C flags that `9.3440 = 8.6088 / 0.9213` is the BIAS-CORRECTED `n=4` value, so the pairing mixes scales.)* **WHAT MY REVISIONS 2 AND 3 GOT WRONG, and it is worse than either individual error: I had the right answer first and was argued off it THREE TIMES IN SUCCESSION, each time by a peer carrying real evidence** — a committed ruling, an on-disk listing, a ledger row — **and revision 3 routed as an *"unresolved tension"* a question C had resolved `2 m 33 s` before I pushed** (`fc8a949` at `15:10:58`, `817d884` at `15:13:31`; timeline re-derived here with `git log --date=format:%H:%M:%S`). **That is not a misread: it is the dated-claim shape at a two-minute scale, and the only defence is re-reading a cited document at the moment of WRITING rather than at the moment of DECIDING.** **THE GENERALISABLE PART IS THE ASYMMETRY IN HOW A POSITION MOVES: three successive peers each moved me, and none of the three was the party who ultimately settled it. Deference to the most recent well-evidenced objection is a random walk, not convergence** — and the fix is not stubbornness but **naming the authority and re-reading it at write time**, which is the same rule this row's other lesson arrives at from the falsifier direction. **THE DISPATCHED RULE WAS ONE STEP SHORT: *check declared `N` against the submitted array range, not the product count* replaces one proxy with a better proxy and never reaches the authority.** `sacct` separates *never launched* from *launched and lost*; **only the receipt separates *never launched because it was not needed* from *never launched by mistake*, and those share an `sacct` signature exactly as *missing data* and *missing intent* share a product-count signature.** **CORRECTED RULE: a declared `N`, a submitted array range and a product count are THREE quantities; the receipt is the authority that reconciles them — read it before reasoning about a gap between any two.** And the premise that motivated the dispatch, *"nothing reconciles them"*, **is FALSE for this receipt: the reconciliation was there before anyone asked.** **THE DISPATCH WAS CATCHABLE ONLY BECAUSE OF `BEN-396`'s REPORTER'S OBLIGATION, discharged unprompted:** *"I have not read the `56863958` receipt itself … if the receipt in fact declares `N=4`, the mismatch evaporates."* **But both anticipated branches were wrong — `N=5` is right AND four tasks is right, simultaneously**, and the receipt's *"reused unmodified, NOT retrained"* was in neither. `BEN-391`'s shape: **a well-posed question whose answer lies outside the space the question offered**, and enumerating two outcomes invites a reader to pick one. **SHARPENED BY ITS AUTHOR, and this is the row's most reusable clause: A TWO-BRANCH FRAMING LOOKED LIKE RIGOUR.** It was written as a falsifier — *"if the receipt declares `N=4` the mismatch evaporates"* — which is the shape of good practice, **and that is exactly why nobody would have questioned it. A falsifier that ENUMERATES OUTCOMES is weaker than one that NAMES THE AUTHORITY TO CONSULT, because the enumeration silently asserts the outcome space is complete.** The stronger form was one clause away at the same cost: *"read the receipt; I do not know what it says."* **So a two-branch framing is a NARROWING, and it is invisible for the same reason a restricting flag is — both look like precision.** **AND A CONCESSION I REFUSED: that lane accepted my *"the wrong half was the dangerous one"* verdict, which C's ruling contradicts. Accepting a concession to a claim I was wrong about would have made the error DURABLE by mutual agreement — `BEN-312` exactly, agreement read as corroboration — so the concession is rejected on the record and the party that offered it was right about `n=4` for the per-cell statistic all along, by a route (*never submitted*) that is not the correct one (*no per-cell array exists*).** **SPLIT OF EVIDENCE, because this row is mostly someone else's: the receipt facts are verified here; EVERY `sacct` fact is RELAYED AND NOT VERIFIED — this host has no Slurm and a null `which sbatch` here is a fact about the host, not the cluster.** *(Flagged, not welded: `CSTAT-CLAIMS-20260814.json:95`'s *"floor's `n=4`"* is a DIFFERENT `n=4` — four of five complete at that time.)* [full](FINDING-20260817-the-reconciliation-was-in-the-receipt.md) *Seconding lane, landing a dispatch from peer session `Assistant [28640e]`, which cannot commit (180 commits behind, nothing written). Its `sacct` legs and its pairing with `BEN-247` are its contribution and are credited; its conclusion is refuted by the receipt it named as the missing check.* | `BEN-077`, `BEN-247`, `BEN-248`, `BEN-391`, `BEN-396` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-398 | **TWO SESSIONS COMMITTED FROM ONE CHECKOUT ALL DAY, AND THE FINDING IS THAT NOTHING SIGNALLED IT.** `git worktree list` measured this turn: `lane-b`, `lane-b-oi126`, `lane-c`, `lane-d`, `lane-e-causes-3-4` each on their own branch — **every other lane isolated, and two sessions defaulted to the main checkout with neither knowing.** It surfaced only because `git` refused a rebase (*"cannot pull with rebase: You have unstaged changes"*) **on a file neither session was writing at that instant — had our writes been to different files, nothing would ever have surfaced it.** **THE LIVE NEAR-MISS, measured by me before any recovery action: my uncommitted `FINDINGS.md` predated lane B's `bd784e4`, so `git diff` showed my working copy removing TWO lines with no replacement — `BEN-248`'s row and its long-form index entry.** Committing my own three-row edit from the shared tree would have **silently deleted another lane's finding, and it would have looked like a CLEAN COMMIT**: one file, a coherent message about my own rows, and **nine passing pre-commit checks, because the hook validates the tree it is handed and has no concept of WHOSE edits are in it.** **So the first symptom of a shared checkout is not a conflict, it is a well-formed commit that removes work nobody was looking at — strictly worse than a conflict, and `BEN-247`'s asymmetry in its purest form: the loud failure forces a search, the quiet success terminates one.** **RECOVERY PATTERN THAT WORKED, in order: preserve the diff BEFORE any git operation** (the standing delegate-diff rule, paying off for a peer's diff); **never `git checkout -- .` / `restore .` / `stash` / rebase on a shared tree** — the status list holds a `D` for a file that EXISTS on `origin/main` and two `M`s belonging to other sessions, and **all three look exactly like damage to clean up and none of them is**; **commit from a throwaway worktree at `origin/main`, RE-APPLYING your edits there** (mine then produced a clean `3 insertion / 3 deletion` diff with no foreign content); **then `git restore --worktree --source=origin/main -- <file>`** to clear the hazard for the next committer without touching HEAD, the index, or any other lane's file. **AND THE MIRROR-IMAGE HAZARD IN THE SAME TREE, the other session's and worse by count: `git reset --mixed origin/main` MOVES HEAD AND THE INDEX AND DELIBERATELY LEAVES THE WORKING TREE, so every file touched by a commit "pulled" that way stays at its old content while HEAD passes it — 13 files, 4 `M` and NINE `D`.** **The nine `D`s are the dangerous shape: files reading as DELETED that were never checked out, including three of MY new `FINDING-*` files, lane C's `RULING`, and three probes — all present on `origin/main`.** To anyone glancing at `git status` that is nine deletions to tidy, and **`git checkout -- .` or a commit titled *"remove stale files"* would have destroyed another lane's work by a path that looks like housekeeping rather than like a conflict** — my near-miss from the opposite direction, in the same tree, the same hour. **Staleness was PROVED rather than assumed before restoring: each file's worktree content matched an EARLIER COMMIT exactly, which is the signature of a stale checkout — a live edit matches no commit.** That test is the transferable artifact. **THE MECHANICAL FIX IS TWO CHECKS, NOT ONE, and the second came out of that:** *(i)* a session that intends to commit asserts it is in its own worktree — `[[ "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" != "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" ]]`, verified both ways this turn (`.git` == `.git` in the main checkout, `.git/worktrees/<name>` != `.git` in a linked one) — **and *(ii)* that its working tree is AT its own HEAD, because a working tree lagging its own HEAD is a second, independent failure the worktree check cannot see.** One command each. **Chosen deliberately over anything touching configuration, because `EnterWorktree` normalises `core.hooksPath` into the SHARED `.git/config` for every lane — a worktree-related fix routed through config is itself a cross-lane write (`BEN-370`); `git rev-parse` reads and writes nothing.** **NOT WIRED and not this lane's call: it would redden for any session legitimately working in the main checkout, so as written it fails the admitting rule at `.githooks/pre-commit:11` and needs to be a lane-invoked assertion.** **`BEN-330` IS THIS HAZARD'S PRIOR INSTANCE AND DID NOT NAME THE CAUSE** — it recorded `git add` by name bounding which *files* travel and not *whose hunks*; **the cause is that the two lanes were in the same checkout and nothing told them.** [full](FINDING-20260817-two-sessions-one-checkout-and-no-signal.md) *Seconding lane; discovered by the mediator by accident, near-miss measured here before acting.* | `BEN-214`, `BEN-247`, `BEN-330`, `BEN-370` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
| BEN-399 | **DEFAULT GIT IDENTITY IS INHERITED FROM THE HUMAN WHO OWNS THE MACHINE, SO UNATTRIBUTED AGENT WORK IS NOT ANONYMOUS — IT IS ATTRIBUTED TO THEM.** Measured on `origin/main`, last 10 h: **61 commits authored `Joseph Bailey <jrbailey555@gmail.com>` — none of them Joseph's** — against `33` `Lane C (PET)` and `15` `Lane D (verifier)`, the last with the **name right and Joseph's EMAIL still attached.** `user.name`/`user.email` resolved from `~/.gitconfig` because **nothing was set at the repo level**, so any lane that did not identify itself committed as the repo's owner. **THIS IS WORSE THAN A MISSING VALUE BECAUSE IT IS A FALSE POSITIVE THAT LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE LEGITIMATE HUMAN AUTHORSHIP, so it survives every check** — and it **systematically fails in ONE direction, toward the human**, which a merely unreliable field would not. **AGAINST ME, and this is the row's sharpest fact: ALL THIRTEEN OF MY COMMITS TODAY ARE AUTHORED `Joseph Bailey` — every one, including `BEN-395`, whose own text says `%an` IS NOT A LANE FIELD, and `BEN-398`, about whose hunks travel in a shared tree.** I filed the day's attribution findings while being an instance of the defect in all of them, and **the check that would have caught it is `git log -1 --format='%an'` on my own commit** — one command, never run, exactly the shape of `BEN-396`'s allocation failure: I audited other parties' attribution and never my own. **`BEN-395` SAID THE FIELD CANNOT BE TRUSTED; THIS SUPPLIES THE CAUSE and upgrades it — the field is not noisy, it is biased, and every `OI-*` ownership audit `BEN-395` contemplates is defeated silently by it.** **FIX APPLIED BY THE MEDIATOR (not by me — a shared config six live lanes depend on is not a thing to change on a peer's message), on Joseph's own words *"I am not committing anything, but it would be nice if we could fix this"*:** `git config --local user.name "MINERvA-OmniFold agent (unattributed)"` and `user.email "agent-unattributed@minerva-omnifold.invalid"`. **VERIFIED HERE INDEPENDENTLY, all three legs, in a throwaway worktree before adopting it: an explicit `-c user.name=` override still WINS (`Lane TEST <test@minerva-omnifold.invalid>`), so C's and D's identification is unaffected; the repo-local value now beats the global fallback (`MINERvA-OmniFold agent (unattributed)`, not `Joseph Bailey`); and it holds INSIDE a linked worktree.** **The fallback is now HONEST rather than FALSE — it does not attribute correctly, it stops attributing WRONGLY**, which is the same distinction as `BEN-084`'s *an artifact asserting the wrong thing beats no artifact for damage*. **`.invalid` is a reserved TLD that can never be a real address, so a forge cannot link these commits to a person: an agent is not a person and the metadata should not imply one.** **EVERY LANE'S ACTION, and this row's own commit is the first instance: pass your identity per-commit — `git -c user.name="<lane>" -c user.email="<lane>@minerva-omnifold.invalid" commit`.** Per-commit `-c` and **not** a config write, because `EnterWorktree` normalises `core.hooksPath` into the shared `.git/config` for every lane and a config-routed identity fix is itself a cross-lane write (`BEN-370`). **THE CLEAN FIX IS AVAILABLE AND DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN: `extensions.worktreeConfig` with a real per-worktree identity changes config RESOLUTION semantics and git requires migrating `core.bare` (set here, `=false`) to enable it — mid-session with six live lanes that is `BEN-370`'s exact shape.** Recorded as available, for a quiet moment. **AND THE MECHANISM IS `BEN-370`'S: git config resolution is SHARED where people assume it is SCOPED, and it fails toward a value that looks correct.** [full](FINDING-20260817-default-identity-is-the-machine-owner.md) *Seconding lane, relaying a repo-wide change made by the mediator and asked to carry it into the record; author distribution, config state and all three precedence legs re-measured here rather than accepted. Joseph's words are RELAYED — I did not hear them.* | `BEN-084`, `BEN-214`, `BEN-370`, `BEN-395`, `BEN-396`, `BEN-398` | EP-2026-08-17-delegate-and-transport |
